Opposite-signed responses to one shared driver is the checkable relationship the primitive asks for -- not an observation that the two happen to be uncorrelated. The two lookbacks are deliberately unequal because the two channels run on different clocks, which is the reason this primitive gives each leg its own: 126d for VNQ because the maturity wall arrives as quarterly cohorts across 2026-27, an installment repricing whose price impact accumulates over roughly two quarters rather than in one jump; 63d for UUP because the differential channel resolves on the FOMC-plus-CPI cadence, and ~1 quarter spans about two meetings and the inflation prints the September question hinges on. Monthly rebalancing matches both drivers -- neither a quarterly refi cohort nor a meeting cycle emits new information at daily frequency, so a finer freq would buy turnover, not signal. Under-used-primitive note: asset_class_trend_hedge has 6 prior proposals and this real_estate-vs-currency pairing is unused; it is distinguishable from the TLT/UUP proposal of 2026-08-07, which put the pure duration channel on both sides, whereas leg A here is a levered cap-rate/coverage claim rather than a duration one. Stated honestly as a hypothesis: the offset claim is exactly what the calibration-window (2007-2017) correlation cap is there to test, and a 2007-2017 sample containing both the 2008 dollar spike against collapsing REITs and the 2014-15 period when a strong dollar coincided with rising REITs may well reject it. If the guard rejects it, the guard is right and this proposal should die there.

Citation: CRE maturity wall: ReedSmith, 'The Debt Maturity Wall and 2026 Wave -- Challenges and Opportunities' (reedsmith.com/our-insights/blogs/real-estate-legal-update/102mijo/the-debt-maturity-wall-and-2026-wave-challenges-and-opportunities/) and MMG Real Estate Advisors, 'The 2026 CRE Refinancing Wall: Opportunities in Multifamily Distress' (mmgrea.com/2026-cre-refinancing-wall/) -- ~$875bn maturing in 2026, ~17% of ~$5tn outstanding, ~$652bn more in 2027, 3-4% originations refinancing at 6-7%. Cap rates: Nareit Q1 2026 REIT Industry Tracker implied cap rates as reported by Selborne Research, 'Commercial Real Estate Cap Rates by Property Type 2026' (selborneresearch.com/guides/reits/cap-rates-by-property-type/) -- industrial 5.2%, self-storage 5.9%, retail 6.2%, residential 6.4%, office 7.7%, against 3.0-3.5% underwriting in 2019-21. REIT leverage/rate-sensitivity mechanism: Simply Safe Dividends, 'How Higher Interest Rates Impact REITs' (simplysafedividends.com/world-of-dividends/posts/20-how-higher-interest-rates-impact-reits) -- REIT debt/EBITDA 5-10x vs under 3x for IG industrials; Crystal Funds, 'The Effects of Interest Rate Changes on REITs' (crystalfunds.com/insights/effects-of-interst-rates-on-real-estate-investment-trusts/). Policy path: CNBC, '2-year Treasury yield keeps going higher after spiking on hawkish start to Warsh's Fed' (cnbc.com/2026/06/18/treasury-yields-investors-warsh-fed-interest-rates.html); 10y ~4.68% and September 25bp hike pricing per Trading Economics, 'US 10 Year Treasury Note Yield' (tradingeconomics.com/united-states/government-bond-yield). Dollar driver and level: MTFX Group, 'US Dollar Forecast August 2026' (mtfxgroup.com/fx-monthly-us/) and Cambridge Currencies, 'US Dollar Index (DXY) Forecast 2026' (cambridgecurrencies.com/us-dollar-index-dxy-forecast/) -- DXY near 99.9 on 2026-08-01, Fed path under Warsh the dominant driver followed by the US-vs-G10 growth/policy gap, H2 range 92-100.


### `rp_cross_sectional_rank_low_vol_252_2` (primitive `cross_sectional_rank`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-09, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: metric=low_vol, lookback=252, k=2. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: The long end is repricing term premium -- the 30y has broken out to ~5.275% while the Fed is expected to keep easing the front end, and the stock-bond correlation has turned positive -- so the realized volatility in this ETF universe now sits in long-duration rates, the leg a cross-asset book conventionally treats as its quiet one. A cross-sectional low_vol rank makes no assumption about which asset class is defensive: it ranks each ETF by its own trailing realized vol, so it structurally shorts whatever is actually carrying the risk right now (long duration, and precious metals in a central-bank-demand-driven bull cycle) and holds the genuinely quiet legs. lookback=252 so the rank reflects the year-long term-premium repricing rather than one month of headlines; k=2 keeps both sleeves at the true extremes; freq=M matches how slowly a 252-day vol rank can move, so turnover cost is not paid for noise.

Citation: Frazzini & Pedersen (2014), 'Betting Against Beta', Journal of Financial Economics 111(1) 1-25 -- the low-risk premium this metric expresses. Current structural context: ICE, 'A duration crisis for markets' (ice.com/insights/fixed-income-data/a-duration-crisis-for-markets) on rates volatility concentrating in the long end, and SSGA 'Gold 2026 Outlook' / World Gold Council 'Why gold in 2026? A cross-asset perspective' for the 30y ~5.275% breakout, the higher stock-bond correlation, and structural central-bank gold demand.


### `rp_static_spread_carry_SLV_USO_a` (primitive `static_spread_carry`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-12, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=SLV, ticker_b=USO, long_leg=a. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: The two commodity sub-complexes inside UNIVERSE are, right now, under opposite physical-supply regimes, which is the structural condition a fixed always-on long-short is built to harvest. Silver is in its sixth consecutive year of structural deficit -- roughly 65-70 Moz short in 2026 on a cumulative 2021-2026 shortfall of ~1,050-1,100 Moz (more than a full year of mine production) drawn down from above-ground stocks, with industrial use ~60% of total demand and solar PV alone absorbing ~120-125 Moz against ~665 GW of 2026 installs; mine supply is rigid because ~70% of silver is a by-product of copper/lead/zinc/gold output and does not respond to the silver price. Crude is in the mirror-image position: OPEC+ added 188 kbpd in August 2026 as it unwinds cuts, Persian Gulf supply recovers to ~90% of pre-war volumes in August and near-full by November, the IEA puts the 2026 surplus at nearly 4 mb/d with the first monthly surplus (~1.2 mb/d) landing in August, and global inventories built 225 mb from January to a four-year-high 7.9 bn bbl. The WTI curve has consequently sat in contango for much of 2026, which matters mechanically for the short leg specifically: USO holds front-month WTI futures and pays that roll cost every month, whereas SLV holds allocated bullion with no roll at all -- so the spread earns the scarcity/roll differential without any directional forecast on either leg. This is a static structural risk-premium bet, not mean reversion: it is deliberately NOT the pair_zscore shape, because a persistent supply deficit on one side and a persistent glut on the other imply drift, not a spread that returns to its mean. The real risk being borne, stated plainly: silver's industrial demand is pro-cyclical, so a growth shock hits the long leg while a supply disruption (OPEC+ reversing, Gulf outage) hits the short leg -- both legs can lose together, and the 2026 glut is a policy choice OPEC+ can unmake. Distinct from the already-proposed rp_static_spread_carry_SLV_GLD_a (silver against a monetary metal, no energy leg) and rp_static_spread_carry_USO_DBC_a (crude against a broad diversified basket, no metals-scarcity claim); neither is this the sign-flip of any existing pipeline row. Chosen over a broad-basket commodity trend leg because the 2026 picture is explicitly divergent by sub-complex -- energy in glut, agriculture amply supplied, metals tight -- so the aggregate index averages the very dispersion this spread is trying to hold. Primitive tally note (2026-08-12): static_spread_carry is the most-proposed primitive at 9 uses against 7 for each of the other five; taken deliberately anyway, since an always-on relative physical-supply bet is the only shape in the current vocabulary that expresses this mechanism (asset_class_trend_hedge mechanically rejects two commodity legs, and pair_zscore asserts the opposite dynamics).

Citation: Silver deficit and industrial/solar demand: Silver Institute-sourced 2026 balance reporting (goldsilver.com/industry-news/goldsilver-news/silver-market-deficit-2026-six-years-and-getting-worse/; bunker-group.com/en/blog/silver-production-balance-a-structural-deficit-in-2026; mintedmetal.com/analysis/silver-industrial-demand-2026/) -- ~65-70 Moz 2026 deficit, ~1,050-1,100 Moz cumulative 2021-2026, ~680 Moz industrial use (~60% of demand), ~120-125 Moz solar against ~665 GW 2026 PV installs. Crude surplus and inventories: IEA Oil Market Report / 'As oil market surplus keeps rising, something's got to give' (iea.org/commentaries/as-oil-market-surplus-keeps-rising-something-s-got-to-give) -- ~4 mb/d 2026 surplus, first monthly surplus ~1.2 mb/d in August 2026, Gulf supply ~90% of pre-war in August to near-full in November, global stocks +225 mb Jan-Aug to a four-year-high 7.9 bn bbl; OPEC+ August 2026 quota increase of 188 kbpd (ebc.com/forex/oil-price-opec-august-2026-inventory-test). WTI term structure in contango for much of 2026: CME Group economic research, 'Implications of WTI Oil Futures In Backwardation Amid the Supply Crunch' (cmegroup.com/insights/economic-research/2026/implications-of-wti-oil-futures-in-backwardation-amid-the-supply-crunch.html); USO's front-month roll mechanics per USCF Investments (uscfinvestments.substack.com/p/understanding-contango-and-backwardation). Divergence by sub-complex: World Bank Commodity Markets Outlook (blogs.worldbank.org/en/developmenttalk/the-commodity-markets-outlook-in-eight-charts3) -- ample grain supply, elevated metals index on tight base-metal markets.


### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_SPY_DBC_252_63` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-13, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=SPY, ticker_b=DBC, lookback_a=252, lookback_b=63. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: The mechanism this pair is built on is a regime change in WHICH asset offsets equity risk, and that change is documented rather than assumed. The San Francisco Fed's August 2026 Economic Letter 'Financial Markets, Oil Prices, and Supply-Side Risks' reads the stock-bond correlation as a live indicator of whether supply or demand shocks dominate the risks to activity, and finds it has flipped -- after two decades in which the primary risks were demand-side (zero lower bound, secular stagnation), market pricing now points to elevated oil prices and inflation as the prominent source of risk, with the stock-oil correlation shifting in agreement. The IMF made the portfolio consequence explicit in February 2026: stock-bond diversification offers less protection from selloffs, because the same inflation impulse that pushes bond yields up is what is hurting equities -- so duration, the offset a SPY/TLT or SPY/IEF construction relies on, is exactly the leg that stops working in this regime. The specific offsetting mechanism claimed here is the regime-conditional sign flip in the equity/commodity relationship, not an observed low correlation: measured across 1970-2026 the commodity-equity correlation is +0.27 with CPI below 2%, +0.02 at 2-4%, and -0.06 above 4%, and the event evidence matches the sign story -- commodities fell WITH stocks in the demand shocks of 2008 and 2020 but rose ~18% against an ~18% S&P decline in the 2022 supply shock. A supply/inflation impulse therefore drives the two legs' trailing-return signs in OPPOSITE directions by construction (input costs up: commodity trend positive, equity margins and multiples compressed: equity trend negative), while a demand impulse drives them the same way -- which is precisely why this is a hedge whose value is state-contingent on the regime the SF Fed letter says we are currently in, and equally why it can lose on both legs in a demand shock. The horizon asymmetry is the mechanism, not a fitted parameter: the equity leg gets the canonical 12-month (252d) time-series-momentum horizon because equity trend regimes turn on a slow clock, while the commodity leg gets one quarter (63d) because a supply shock transmits to commodity prices in weeks -- a 252d commodity leg would still be reading the pre-shock regime at exactly the moment the hedge is supposed to pay. Weekly rebalance for the same reason: at monthly the 63d leg can sit up to 21 trading days stale through the impulse it exists to catch. Honest statement of what is being borne: this is two trend legs, and every trend strategy tested in this lab is DEAD -- the claim is not that either leg has standalone edge but that their shock-response signs are opposed in a supply-driven regime, which is the compositional claim this primitive exists to test and which gate 2's diversifier clause is deliberately skeptical of. Distinct from the already-proposed rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_SPY_GLD_252_252 (gold is a monetary/real-rate leg, not a broad input-cost basket, and at 252/252 it makes no horizon-asymmetry claim), rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_SPY_IEF_252_63 (the duration offset this proposal argues has structurally stopped working), and rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_TLT_DBC_252_60 / rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_VNQ_DBC_189_63 (neither has an equity leg, so neither expresses the equity-vs-input-cost sign flip). Primitive tally note (2026-08-12): asset_class_trend_hedge is at 7 uses, tied for least-used with four others against static_spread_carry's 10, so this is not a default reach for the crowded shape. Note the mechanical guard this must still clear and may well fail: the calibration-window (2007-2017) leg-correlation cap. That window is, by the SF Fed letter's own account, the demand-shock era in which these two legs moved together -- so a rejection there would be the guard correctly refusing to take a regime claim on faith from out-of-window reasoning, and is an acceptable outcome for this proposal.

Citation: Stock-bond correlation flip as a supply-vs-demand-shock indicator, and the accompanying shift in the stock-oil correlation: Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco Economic Letter, August 2026, 'Financial Markets, Oil Prices, and Supply-Side Risks' (frbsf.org/research-and-insights/publications/economic-letter/2026/08/financial-markets-oil-prices-and-supply-side-risks/; also indexed at fedinprint.org/item/fedfel/103632). Portfolio consequence -- duration's weakened offset: International Monetary Fund blog, 18 February 2026, 'Stock-Bond Diversification Offers Less Protection From Market Selloffs' (imf.org/en/blogs/articles/2026/02/18/stock-bond-diversification-offers-less-protection-from-market-selloffs); corroborated by Oxford Economics, 'Stock bond correlation will become positive again in 2026' (oxfordeconomics.com/resource/stock-bond-correlation-will-become-positive-again-in-2026/), with BlackRock's dissenting read (blackrock.com/us/financial-professionals/insights/bonds-offer-more-diversification) noted rather than omitted. Regime-conditional sign of the commodity-equity relationship (+0.27 below 2% CPI, +0.02 at 2-4%, -0.06 above 4%, 1970-2026; commodities down with stocks in 2008/2020, +18% vs the S&P's -18% in the 2022 supply shock; commodity-equity correlation -0.07 in 1994-2007 vs +0.34 in 2009-2026): WisdomTree, 'Adding, Not Replacing: Broad Commodities in the Age of Efficient Capital' (wisdomtree.com/us/insights/blog/adding-not-replacing-broad-commodities-in-the-age-of-efficient-capital); QuantPedia, 'Commodity Portfolio Strategy for a Potential 2026 Inflationary and Supply Shock Regime' (quantpedia.com/commodity-portfolio-strategy-for-a-potential-2026-inflationary-and-supply-shock-regime/). Canonical 12-month equity trend horizon for lookback_a: Moskowitz, Ooi and Pedersen (2012), 'Time Series Momentum', Journal of Financial Economics 104(2), 228-250.


### `rp_pair_zscore_VNQ_IEF_120_2p0_4p0` (primitive `pair_zscore`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-13, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=VNQ, ticker_b=IEF, z_window=120, z_entry=2.0, z_stop=4.0. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: Structural, not a track-record claim -- I assert nothing about how VNQ, IEF, listed real estate or any live strategy has recently performed. THE MECHANISM: a mean-reversion primitive needs a TETHER between its two legs and an identifiable economic agent who closes the gap; most UNIVERSE pairs have the first at best. This one has both, and both are measured in primary data rather than asserted. (1) THE TETHER IS THE DISCOUNT RATE. A listed REIT is a claim on property cash flows discounted at the 10-year Treasury yield plus a cap-rate spread, and IEF is a direct instrument on that same 10-year point. Nareit's own Q1 2026 data confirms the linkage is live rather than notional: the REIT IMPLIED cap rate is 5.89%, 169bp above the 10-year Treasury, and Nareit states explicitly that the REIT implied cap rate 'has reacted meaningfully to changes in the 10-year Treasury yield' while the private NCREIF ODCE APPRAISAL cap rate (4.44%, only 24bp over the 10-year) has not, having sat essentially unmoved at its year-end-2021 level for three years. So the log spread log(VNQ)-log(IEF) is, structurally, the listed property risk premium: the two legs share one dominant driver, and what is left over is the cap-rate spread. (2) THE AGENT WHO CLOSES THE GAP IS A TAKE-PRIVATE BUYER, not an abstract 'it should revert'. US REITs above $200M market cap closed Q1 2026 at a median 19.3% discount to consensus NAV; S&P Global counts four US REIT M&A deals totalling $16.77bn announced through 2026-04-15, three of them privatizations of public equity REITs, and ALL FOUR targeted companies trading at a significant discount to NAV, at 20-40% premiums to the unaffected share price. That is a real, capitalized bid that appears when listed pricing detaches from the private mark -- the closest thing this UNIVERSE offers to a mechanical restoring force behind a relative-value spread. THE SINGLE BIGGEST WEAKNESS, STATED FIRST RATHER THAN BURIED: the restoring force runs on a MULTI-QUARTER clock and this primitive cannot wait for it. Nareit puts the current public-private valuation divergence at 17 quarters and still open; an M&A process takes quarters to announce and close. pair_zscore normalizes against a trailing window capped at 120 trading days (~6 months), so what is actually under test is short-horizon reversion around a ROLLING mean, NOT convergence of the structural gap itself -- and a 17-quarter-old gap is precisely the kind that a rolling mean absorbs into its own baseline instead of trading against. If the only real reversion here lives at a horizon longer than the primitive can see, this should and will die on gate 1, and that is the honest prior going in. SECOND WEAKNESS: VNQ is equity and IEF is not, so the spread carries an unhedged equity-beta residual that no 1:1 log spread removes (this primitive fits no hedge ratio, unlike family N's regressed beta). A broad equity drawdown widens the spread with no REIT-specific dislocation behind it, and March 2020 -- listed REITs sold off hard while Treasuries rallied -- is the worst single realization available in the OOS window. It is exactly the realization that should kill this if the spread is really a levered equity bet wearing a relative-value label. THIRD, AGAINST MY OWN THESIS: the dislocation on offer has been SHRINKING, not widening -- the same median NAV discount was 8.7% by mid-2026 against 19.3% at Q1. A smaller gap means less for the mechanism to close, which is a reason to expect failure, not a caveat appended to a bullish case. I propose it anyway because waiting for a wider gap would be timing, and pre-registration is what stops that from being decided after the fact. DISTINCT FROM WHAT ALREADY EXISTS: no graveyard.csv row uses VNQ/IEF, and family N's six pre-registered cointegration pairs (GLD/SLV, TLT/IEF, EFA/EEM, SPY/QQQ, USO/DBC, LQD/HYG) do not include it -- so unlike most remaining pair candidates in this UNIVERSE, this one is not contraindicated by an already-failed ADF test. rp_static_spread_carry_VNQ_IEF_a touches the same two tickers under a DIFFERENT primitive and a different hypothesis -- a persistent premium in the spread's DRIFT versus reversion around its mean -- with its own name and its own separate verdict, the same distinction this ledger already drew between rp_static_spread_carry_LQD_IEF_a and rp_pair_zscore_LQD_IEF_120_2p0_4p0; neither borrows the other's evidence. PARAMETER CHOICES, each tied to the mechanism rather than tuned: z_window=120 is the LONGEST window the primitive offers, chosen because the dislocation is driven by quarterly NAV marks and multi-quarter deal processes -- a shorter window would normalize away the very deviation being traded. z_entry=2.0 / z_stop=4.0 are this lab's OWN already-pre-registered pair thresholds (family N, from Gatev, Goetzmann & Rouwenhorst 2006's canonical 2-sigma construction), deliberately reused rather than searched over. freq=W because the signal's state changes on a ~6-month normalization against a multi-quarter mechanism: rebalancing daily would pay turnover the hypothesis never earns, and gate 1's null is duty-cycle-matched, so the candidate is charged for exactly that. BREADTH: pair_zscore is tied for least-proposed in this ledger (7 of 46 prior rows, against static_spread_carry's 10), and this is its first use of the real_estate asset class.

Citation: Nareit market commentary, 'Valuing Real Estate Like It's 2021: Private Appraisal Cap Rates Stuck at 2021 REIT Levels' (reit.com) -- Q1 2026 REIT implied cap rate 5.89% vs NCREIF ODCE private appraisal cap rate 4.44%, a 1.45pp gap; REIT implied cap rate 169bp over the 10-year Treasury vs the appraisal cap rate's 24bp; REIT implied cap rates react to the 10-year yield while appraisal cap rates have been static at year-end-2021 levels; public-private divergence now 17 quarters old. S&P Global Market Intelligence, 'US REIT M&A activity ramp-up extends into early months of 2026' (2026-04, spglobal.com) -- four US REIT M&A deals totalling $16.77bn announced through 2026-04-15, three of them privatizations of public equity REITs, all four targets trading at a significant discount to NAV; median NAV discount for US REITs above $200M market cap 19.3% at Q1 2026, narrowing to 8.7% by mid-2026; recent take-privates struck at 20-40% premiums to unaffected share prices. Family N's pre-registered pairs and Engle-Granger results (STRATEGIES.md) confirm VNQ/IEF is not among the six pairs already ADF-tested and dropped. Construction thresholds from Gatev, Goetzmann & Rouwenhorst (2006), as already pre-registered for family N in STRATEGIES.md.


### `rp_single_asset_trend_DBC_126` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-13, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=DBC, lookback=126. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: The broad commodity complex is currently in a deep low-inventory state, which is the precondition the theory of storage says makes a signed directional position on a commodity index economically different from a coin flip. The 2026 Iran conflict and the Strait of Hormuz disruption took an estimated ~5.5 mb/d of Middle East crude production offline on average during July 2026 -- more than 5% of global consumption -- and crude and refined-product curves returned to extreme backwardation after fighting resumed 2026-07-08; the Bloomberg Commodity Index's index-weighted implied carry was reported at +13% as of 2026-03-31 against a firmly negative -1.3% median since May 2011, and the broader complex (copper, platinum) is running physical deficits with global stockpiles and spare capacity eroding. Two mechanical consequences follow, neither of which is a claim about any strategy's recent performance: (1) when inventories fall far enough that convenience yield exceeds storage plus financing, curves invert and a long futures position earns a positive roll return instead of paying contango drag; (2) the same depleted buffer stocks are what normally damp a spot shock, so with them gone price moves propagate rather than being absorbed -- i.e. the sign of a trailing return carries more information about the next period in a low-inventory regime than in a well-supplied one. DBC is a futures-holding ETF whose Optimum Yield roll targets backwardated contracts, so both effects land directly in the price series engine.py loads, making this testable with spot ETF closes alone. A 126-day (~6 month) lookback matches the horizon over which inventory cycles resolve and spans the disruption regime that began in late February 2026; monthly rebalancing keeps turnover low for a slow-moving structural state. Distinct from the already-tested rp_single_asset_trend_USO_126 (single front-month WTI, no cross-commodity inventory channel) and from the DBC legs inside rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_TLT_DBC_252_60 and _VNQ_DBC_189_63, which use different lookbacks and are only ever held against an offsetting leg. Stated honestly against this lab's own record: every trend primitive tested here is DEAD, and backwardation can collapse back to contango quickly -- the pre-registered claim is narrowly that the inventory/roll channel is a structural reason a broad-commodity trend sign might carry information a pure price-trend does not, and the OOS gate decides.

Citation: Mechanism: Gorton, Hayashi & Rouwenhorst (2013), 'The Fundamentals of Commodity Futures Returns', Review of Finance 17(1) -- inventories drive backwardation and the predictability of commodity futures returns (theory of storage, Kaldor/Working). Current conditions: CME Group, 'Implications of WTI Oil Futures In Backwardation Amid the Supply Crunch' and 'How Backwardation Generates Positive Roll Returns in Energy' (cmegroup.com/insights/economic-research/2026/) -- crude and refined-product curves in extreme backwardation since 2026-07-08, positive roll return to a rolled long position; S&P Global Market Intelligence, 'Commodity Price Watch: July 2026' -- ~5.5 mb/d of Middle East production offline on average in July 2026, >5% of global consumption, US-Iran MOU reopening Hormuz transit; Aberdeen Investments, 'Commodities June outlook: An illusion of abundance' (2026-06) -- global stockpiles declining and spare capacity eroding across oil and metals, disruptions absorbed via inventory drawdown; IBKR Campus, 'What does today's commodity market mean for broad allocations?' -- reports BCOM index-weighted implied carry of +13% at 2026-03-31 vs a -1.3% median since May 2011, and describes DBC's Optimum Yield roll methodology.


### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_QQQ_USO_200_40` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-13, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=QQQ, ticker_b=USO, lookback_a=200, lookback_b=40. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: STRUCTURAL, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM -- nothing below asserts that trend-following, commodities, tech or any live strategy has recently performed well; every input is a statement about the current transmission structure between two shock channels. THE MECHANISM, NAMED SPECIFICALLY (required by this primitive, which will not accept 'these two happen to be uncorrelated'): the QQQ leg and the USO leg sit on opposite ends of ONE causal chain -- crude oil is the principal transmitter of inflation shocks into the 10-year real yield, and the 10-year real yield is the dominant valuation variable for the longest-duration equity claim in this UNIVERSE. QQQ's cash flows are the most distant of any UNIVERSE equity ETF, so its multiple is disproportionately a discounted-value function of the real rate; an energy-led inflation impulse raises breakevens and term premium, lifts the real discount rate, and de-rates exactly that multiple, while the same impulse is what a crude trend leg goes long into. Run the chain the other way and it still holds: a disinflationary crude decline lowers the real discount rate and re-rates QQQ upward while the crude trend leg is short. That is a directional, checkable transmission claim -- not a correlation search. WHY THE HEDGE LEG IS A COMMODITY AND NOT DURATION, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT OF PICKING THIS PAIR NOW. The textbook offset for an equity-duration shock is nominal duration (TLT/IEF), and that offset is structurally impaired in the current regime: Oxford Economics' 2026 work argues the stock-bond correlation returns to POSITIVE territory in 2026 and that the pre-Covid regime of weakly pro-cyclical inflation does not sustainably return, with continued supply shocks, geopolitical risk and more activist fiscal policy pushing term premia higher. When the correlation is positive, a rates leg does not offset an equity-duration shock -- it SHARES it, because both legs are then being driven by the same inflation/term-premium factor. So the hedge for long-duration equity has to come from the asset class that is the SOURCE of that factor rather than another victim of it. This is also why the pair is admissible under the primitive's asset-class rule for a real reason and not merely a bookkeeping one: QQQ resolves to equity, USO to commodity. WHY USO RATHER THAN DBC. DBC is a diversified basket whose energy exposure is diluted by metals and agriculture and whose optimised roll rule deliberately blunts the front of the curve; the inflation-transmission channel argued above is specifically CRUDE, so the pure front-month WTI instrument is the sharper expression of the stated mechanism, not a broader-is-better diversifier claim. A SECONDARY, PARTIAL AND DELIBERATELY NOT OVERSOLD POINT ON THE USO LEG. USO holds and rolls front-month WTI, so its price series -- which is what engine.load_prices() sees -- mechanically embeds roll yield. The WTI curve is currently backwardated (CME Group's 2026 'Implications of WTI Oil Futures In Backwardation Amid the Supply Crunch'; a gently backwardated Brent strip running from roughly USD 79/bbl for Oct-2026 down toward USD 69-70/bbl by 2029 as of early August 2026), which means a LONG USO position is roll-yield-supported while held. I am not counting this as carry, because this primitive takes both signs via sign(): the same structure that pays the long side charges the short side symmetrically. It is a reason the long half of the leg is cheaper to hold, not an edge. PARAMETERS AND WHY. lookback_a=200 for QQQ: the equity-duration re-rating this argument describes is a multi-quarter repricing of a discount rate, and ~200 trading days is the horizon over which the AI-capex investment cycle driving that duration actually turns (PIMCO, 'AI Financing Needs Do Not Override Cyclical Drivers of Yield', argues AI financing needs do not override the cyclical drivers of yields -- i.e. the rate channel stays the operative one). lookback_b=40 for USO: roughly two months, the horizon over which crude's inventory/term-structure state actually re-prices, and deliberately much shorter than the equity leg so the two legs are not the same trend measured twice -- which is also what should let them clear this primitive's calibration-window correlation cap on merit rather than by luck. freq=W: the faster leg is 40 days, so weekly rebalancing gives ~8 rebalances per lookback while paying far less turnover than daily, and gate 1's null is duty-cycle-matched so the candidate is charged for exactly the trading it does. THE HONEST CASE AGAINST, STATED IN FULL RATHER THAN APPENDED AS A CAVEAT. (1) THIS LAB'S OWN RECORD IS THE STRONGEST EVIDENCE AGAINST IT: both legs are trend legs, trend is DEAD across every family tested here, and rp_single_asset_trend_USO_126 is already in graveyard.csv as DEAD (OOS Sharpe 0.512) -- the crude trend leg specifically has already failed on its own. The only genuinely new thing being tested is whether combining two dead trend legs across these particular asset classes clears gate 2, and DOCTRINE v1.14 warns explicitly that two zero-edge signals can look like they earn their place through variance reduction alone rather than economic logic. My prior is DEAD. (2) NOT INDEPENDENT OF AN EXISTING PROPOSAL: rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_SPY_DBC_252_63 was proposed 2026-08-12 on an adjacent mechanism (broad equity hedged with a broad commodity basket), and QQQ's constituents are a large share of SPY's capitalisation, so this is a sharper restatement of that hypothesis on both legs, not a second independent draw. If the mechanism is real, both should work; if this one clears and that one does not, the difference is likelier instrument construction than economics. (3) THE CITED STRUCTURAL CONDITION IS WEAKENING IN REAL TIME: front-month crude sold off sharply on 2026-08-04 (WTI down nearly 7%), flattening the forward curve, and the 'supply crunch' framing of the CME piece is materially less pronounced now than a month ago. I am citing backwardation as present but gentle, not as a tight-market claim. (4) THE POSITIVE-STOCK-BOND-CORRELATION PREMISE IS A FORECAST, not an observed constant -- Oxford Economics argues it, and if the correlation reverts to negative the entire 'why not duration' argument for choosing a commodity hedge leg loses its force. DISTINCTNESS. QQQ has never appeared in any of the 51 prior pipeline_ideas.csv rows under any primitive, and no asset_class_trend_hedge proposal has ever used QQQ or USO as a leg (prior pairs: SPY/GLD, TLT/DBC, SPY/IEF, VNQ/DBC, TLT/GLD, TLT/UUP, VNQ/UUP, SPY/DBC). No graveyard.csv row uses this pair. BREADTH NOTE, STATED PLAINLY: asset_class_trend_hedge stands at 8 of 51 prior rows, against curve_carry's 7 -- so this does not improve primitive-level coverage by one count. It was chosen anyway because curve_carry's sole parameter is a lookback and 21/42/63/84/126/189/252 are already taken (three of them already DEAD), leaving only gap-filling draws with little research value, whereas this proposal closes a real TICKER-level coverage hole (QQQ, previously zero uses across the entire ledger) on a mechanism today's research actually supports. Per the stated preference-not-a-rule. FINALLY, A VOCABULARY GAP THIS RUN SURFACED AND DID NOT TRY TO FORCE: the cleanest reading of the crude backwardation evidence is 'be long a commodity while its own futures curve is backwardated, flat otherwise' -- a term-structure carry signal that no primitive in PRIMITIVES can express, since none of them condition on anything outside spot price action (curve_carry is the lone exception and is hard-wired to the FRED Treasury curve and the TLT/IEF pair). This is already recorded as a known follow-up in DOCTRINE v1.14, which notes structural carry generalisation is blocked on engine.load_prices() being spot ETF closes only with no futures term structure -- so I am flagging it here rather than opening a duplicate note, and I did not bend it into this proposal.

Citation: Stock-bond correlation regime (the 'why the hedge leg is not duration' premise): Oxford Economics, 'Stock bond correlation will become positive again in 2026', https://www.oxfordeconomics.com/resource/stock-bond-correlation-will-become-positive-again-in-2026/ -- argues the stock-bond correlation returns to positive territory in 2026, that the pre-Covid regime of weakly pro-cyclical inflation does not sustainably return, and that continued supply shocks, geopolitical risk and more activist fiscal policy keep term premia marching higher, which challenges allocation approaches relying on long-duration government bonds as ballast. Corroborating regime framing: Morningstar, 'What Higher Inflation Means for Stock/Bond Correlations', https://www.morningstar.com/portfolios/what-higher-inflation-means-stock-bond-correlations and Vanguard, 'The stock/bond correlation: increasing amid inflation' (inflation surprises and monetary-policy shocks produce POSITIVE stock-bond correlation, whereas demand shocks and flight-to-quality produce negative). Crude term structure (the secondary roll-yield point, cited as present but gentle): CME Group, 'Implications of WTI Oil Futures In Backwardation Amid the Supply Crunch' (2026), https://www.cmegroup.com/insights/economic-research/2026/implications-of-wti-oil-futures-in-backwardation-amid-the-supply-crunch.html ; and, as the deliberately-weighted counterweight, CMB News / Commodity Board, 'Crude Oil Sell-Off Flattens Brent and WTI Curves in Early August 2026', https://commodity-board.com/brent-curve-flattens-as-front-month-crude-slides-below-usd-80 -- front-month WTI fell nearly 7% on 2026-08-04, flattening the forward curve, with the Brent strip running from roughly USD 79/bbl (Oct-2026) toward USD 69-70/bbl (2029), i.e. clear but gentle backwardation rather than an acute supply crunch. Broader commodity structure: IG, 'Commodities outlook 2026: gold, silver & oil price forecasts', https://www.ig.com/en/news-and-trade-ideas/commodities-market-outlook-for-2026-251212 (oil facing a supply-driven downtrend on weakening demand). Long-duration equity / rate-sensitivity channel (the QQQ leg): PIMCO, 'AI Financing Needs Do Not Override Cyclical Drivers of Yield', https://www.pimco.com/us/en/insights/ai-financing-needs-do-not-override-cyclical-drivers-of-yield -- AI-related financing demand does not override the cyclical drivers of yields, so the real-rate channel remains the operative valuation variable; and Kalkine, 'Interest Rates and the NASDAQ 2026: How Fed Policy Is Impacting Tech Stocks', https://www.kalkine.com/news/premium/interest-rates-and-the-nasdaq-2026-how-fed-policy-is-impacting-tech-stocks (the Nasdaq-100's long-duration growth profile is unusually rate-sensitive; the 10-year real yield is among the most influential variables for its valuation multiple). Curve context for the term-premium premise: the 2s10s spread exited the 2022-2024 inversion and remains positively sloped as of August 2026 (RecessionPulse, 'Yield Curve (2s10s) Spread', https://recessionpulse.com/indicators/yield-curve-2s10s ; FRED T10Y2Y, https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/T10Y2Y). NOTE ON WHAT IS DELIBERATELY ABSENT: no source here is cited for how any strategy, factor or fund has recently performed; every citation above is a statement about structure (correlation regime, curve shape, transmission channel), per this lab's pre-registration discipline.


### `rp_single_asset_trend_EFA_252` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-14, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=EFA, lookback=252. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: STRUCTURAL, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM. Nothing below asserts that trend-following, international equity, or any live strategy has recently performed well; the inputs are official-sector reserve-composition data and the mechanical construction of the instrument. THE MECHANISM. EFA is an UNHEDGED USD share class over developed ex-North-America equities, so the price series engine.load_prices() actually sees decomposes into two additive return sources: the local-currency equity return, and a pure FX translation term (EUR/JPY/GBP/AUD vs USD). The hypothesis is specifically about the SECOND term. The dollar's share of allocated global FX reserves fell below 57% for the first time since 1995 -- 56.9% in Q3 2025, down from a 72% peak in 2001 -- and central banks have now bought more than 1,000 tonnes of gold in each of three consecutive years as the reallocation counterparty. That is an official-sector flow with two properties ordinary FX flow does not have: it is price-INSENSITIVE (a reserve manager rebalancing a mandated currency weight does not stop because the dollar got cheaper, which is what normally arbitrages a drift away) and it is administratively SLOW (reserve composition targets move on multi-quarter committee cycles and are reported quarterly through IMF COFER). A price-insensitive, slow, one-directional flow is the textbook generator of DRIFT rather than mean reversion. So the testable claim is that EFA's USD-denominated series carries a persistent additive component that a trend-sign signal can hold, and that this makes single-ticker trend behave differently on an unhedged foreign-currency claim than on the domestic assets where this lab has already killed it. WHY EFA AND NOT EEM. Both are unhedged, but EEM's currency basket is dominated by managed and partially-pegged regimes whose central banks actively suppress exactly the drift this argument depends on; EAFE's are freely floating majors, which are the currencies reserve managers actually hold, so the flow described above lands in EFA's FX term directly rather than being absorbed by an intervention desk. WHY NOT UUP DIRECTLY. UUP is the pure expression of the dollar leg and has already been proposed (rp_single_asset_trend_UUP_63, 2026-08-06). EFA is a deliberately different bet: it pairs the same FX drift with a positive-expected-return equity claim underneath it, so the signal is not asked to earn its keep from FX alone. PARAMETERS AND WHY. lookback=252 (top of the pre-registered range, ~12 months) because the flow's own clock is the binding constraint -- COFER is quarterly, the reserve-share decline being cited spans 2025 into 2026, and a shorter window would measure the equity leg's noise rather than the currency leg's drift. I am choosing this horizon from the mechanism's timescale, not shopping it. freq=M because a signal built from a multi-quarter structural flow has no business rebalancing faster than monthly; gate 1's null is duty-cycle-matched so the candidate is charged for exactly the trading it does, and monthly is where a 252-day signal's turnover is honest. THE HONEST CASE AGAINST, STATED IN FULL. (1) 252/monthly IS the canonical TSMOM specification this lab has already killed. tsmom_12m and tsmom_ensemble are both DEAD in graveyard.csv, engine.py pins LOOKBACK=252 with an explicit 'do NOT tune this', and seven of the eight prior single_asset_trend proposals are already DEAD (GLD 126, SLV 63, VNQ 126, EEM 189, USO 126, TLT 126, and DBC 126 pending). The ONLY genuinely new thing here is the unhedged-FX-composite channel; if that channel is not real, this is a restatement of a dead hypothesis on a fifteenth ticker and should die identically. My prior is DEAD. (2) THE MECHANISM IS SLOWER THAN THE OOS WINDOW CAN RESOLVE. Reserve share moved roughly 15 percentage points over 25 years; even if the drift is entirely real, its per-year contribution is small relative to EAFE equity volatility, so the FX term may simply be undetectable at the sample size gate 1 has -- an argument that the effect could be real AND the verdict correctly DEAD. (3) THE DRIFT IS NOT MONOTONIC. The dollar index strengthened over the twelve months into June 2026 on rate expectations even while reserve share kept falling, which is the direct counterexample: tactical FX and structural reserve diversification are different clocks, and a trend signal reads the tactical one. The cited sources make exactly this caveat themselves and I am not arguing past it. (4) A LONG-ONLY VERSION OF THIS IS ALREADY A SURVIVOR HERE. Diversified buy-and-hold is one of only two things alive in this lab and already owns international equity beta; for this candidate to add anything, the trend SIGN has to carry information beyond simply being long, which gate 2's diversification test is well placed to reject. DISTINCTNESS. EFA has never been proposed under single_asset_trend, appears in only 2 of the 52 prior pipeline_ideas.csv rows (both as a hedge leg), and no graveyard.csv row uses it. BREADTH NOTE, STATED PLAINLY. single_asset_trend stands at 8 of 52 prior rows against curve_carry's 7, so this does not improve primitive-level coverage by one count. curve_carry's sole parameter is a lookback and 21/42/63/84/126/189/252 are already taken (three already DEAD), leaving only gap-filling draws; today's research pointed at an FX/reserve-composition mechanism that curve_carry -- hard-wired to the FRED Treasury slope and the TLT/IEF pair -- cannot express at all. Per the stated preference-not-a-rule. Today's other three proposals used static_spread_carry, pair_zscore and asset_class_trend_hedge, so this is a fourth distinct primitive for the day.

Citation: Reserve-composition structure (the primary claim): informedclearly, 'De-Dollarization 2026: Dollar Reserve Share Below 57% for First Time Since 1995', https://informedclearly.com/en/economy/49805/de-dollarization-dollar-reserve-share-2026 -- USD share of allocated global FX reserves at 56.9% in Q3 2025 and falling further in subsequent quarters, versus a 72% peak in 2001; underlying series is IMF COFER, https://data.imf.org/en/datasets/IMF.STA:COFER (quarterly, which is the reporting cadence the 252-day lookback is chosen against). Official-sector gold demand as the reallocation counterparty and the explicit tactical-vs-structural caveat: Plus500, 'U.S. Dollar Safe-Haven Status in 2026: De-dollarisation Explained for Traders', https://us.plus500.com/en/newsandmarketinsights/de-dollarisation-explained -- central banks bought over 1,000 tonnes of gold in 2024, a third consecutive year above that threshold; the same source states plainly that de-dollarization is a gradual diversification rather than a collapse, that the dollar index STRENGTHENED over the twelve months into June 2026 on rate expectations, and that a short-term rally does not disprove the structural shift -- cited here as the counterweight in point (3) above, not suppressed. Instrument construction (why the FX term is additive and unhedged in the series the engine sees): iShares, 'Currency Hedged MSCI EAFE ETF (HEFA)', https://www.ishares.com/us/products/259622/ -- HEFA and the unhedged EAFE exposure hold the same underlying developed-market portfolio, the only structural difference being currency exposure, which is precisely the decomposition this proposal rests on; magnitude of the currency term documented in ETF Trends, 'Currency: The Hidden Alpha in International ETFs', https://www.etftrends.com/model-portfolio-content-hub/currency-hidden-alpha-international-etfs/ (MSCI EAFE returned ~24% in local terms versus ~31% for a USD investor, i.e. a ~7 percentage point currency component -- cited to size the FX channel, not as a performance recommendation). NOTE ON WHAT IS DELIBERATELY ABSENT: no source above is cited for how any strategy, factor or fund has recently performed; every citation is a statement about structure (reserve composition, official-sector flow, instrument construction), per this lab's pre-registration discipline.


### `rp_cross_sectional_rank_momentum_105_1` (primitive `cross_sectional_rank`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-14, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: metric=momentum, lookback=105, k=1. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: PRIMITIVE CHOICE, and why not curve_carry. cross_sectional_rank has 8 of pipeline_ideas.csv's 54 prior proposals against static_spread_carry's 12, and its THREE-dimensional (metric x lookback x k) space is by far the least-covered per draw in the vocabulary. curve_carry is nominally the least-used (7) but is a ONE-parameter primitive whose pre-registered (20, 252) range has already been sampled at 21/42/63/84/126/189/252 across six separate term-premium rationales -- an eighth draw there fills a gap in an already-mined space rather than adding information, so the under-representation preference is better served here. STRUCTURAL SETUP -- about DISPERSION MAGNITUDE, and direction-free. This primitive's mechanism requires exactly one precondition to have anything to harvest: the trailing-return spread between the top-K and the bottom-K of the UNIVERSE must be wide enough that the long and short legs do not cancel. That precondition is currently at an extreme, and at the cross-ASSET level this 15-ETF universe actually ranks over, not merely inside equities. Through 2026-07-31 the broad commodity complex sits ~+39% YTD against US equities ~+10.5% and a bond complex far behind, with the commodity block itself internally split -- crude ~+87% YTD on the Strait of Hormuz blockage while GOLD LAGS on a firmer dollar. A ~29-point gap between the best and worst asset-class blocks, plus a split INSIDE the leading block, is the wide and non-degenerate rank ordering this primitive needs; in a compressed-dispersion regime the identical signal trades costs for noise. WHY THE ORDERING SHOULD PERSIST RATHER THAN CHURN -- the real hypothesis under test. The dominant driver is a PHYSICAL supply constraint (a blocked shipping lane), not a sentiment rotation. A constraint of that kind resolves on a logistics/geopolitical clock measured in quarters and re-prices the affected assets against everything else for as long as it binds, so the rank order it produces is slow-moving. That is a claim about the PERSISTENCE OF THE RANKING, not a forecast that any particular ticker keeps rising: both legs' membership and sign are computed mechanically from trailing returns and I assert no view on which assets occupy which end. PARAMETERS. metric=momentum because the persistence argument above IS a momentum claim and no other metric in the set expresses it -- the named mechanism is the reason, never any strategy's record. lookback=105 (~5 months) spans the 2026 shock without reaching back into the materially different 2025 regime, and is untested; the three prior momentum draws sit at 63/126/252, all monthly. k=1 is the point of this proposal and has NEVER been drawn (prior k values: 2, 3, 4): today's dispersion is concentrated at the EXTREMES rather than spread evenly across the 15 names -- one asset near +87%, another commodity lagging outright -- so a wide K dilutes the book into the thin middle of the ranking where the spread is smallest. k=1 is the configuration that actually harvests extreme dispersion, and it sits at the boundary of the pre-registered (1, 7) range. freq=W rather than the M every prior momentum draw used: a supply constraint can lift abruptly, and a monthly book stays stale for up to a month at exactly the point the rank order can turn over. HONEST LIMITATION, stated rather than papered over. The most-cited 2026 dispersion evidence -- S&P 500 cross-sectional volatility more than five standard deviations above its long-term average, 10 of 11 sectors below their long-run correlation averages -- is SINGLE-STOCK dispersion inside the equity block and does NOT transfer to a 15-ETF cross-asset universe; it is corroborating regime context only. The load-bearing fact here is the cross-ASSET return spread above. I make no claim about how any live strategy, fund or factor has recently performed. Three prior cross_sectional_rank momentum draws are DEAD in graveyard.csv -- under the doctrine that is neither evidence for nor against this one, and is noted only so the shape is not mistaken for unexplored.

Citation: Cross-asset YTD dispersion through 2026-07-31 (commodities ~+39% led by crude ~+87% on the Strait of Hormuz blockage; US equities ~+10.5%; gold lagging on a firmer dollar): https://seekingalpha.com/article/4929511-year-to-date-investment-performance-for-asset-classes-and-portfolios-through-july-31-2026 -- this is the LOAD-BEARING citation. Corroborating regime context ONLY, equity-internal and explicitly not cross-asset: S&P 500 cross-sectional volatility >5 standard deviations above its long-term average, 10 of 11 sectors below long-run correlation averages, Mag-7 vs S&P 500 Equal Weight 100-day correlation -0.27 -- Nationwide Financial, 'Market dispersion is back: why stock selection matters more now', https://www.nationwide.com/financial-professionals/blog/markets-economy/articles/market-dispersion-is-back-why-stock-selection-matters-more-now ; S&P Dow Jones Indices (Indexology), '2026 Is the Year of the Stock Picker?', 2026-01-13, https://www.indexologyblog.com/2026/01/13/2026-is-the-year-of-the-stock-picker/ ; dispersion-regime baselines (15-25% dispersion, 0.40-0.50 average pairwise correlation in calm periods) -- Morningstar Indexes, 'Dispersion as a Gauge of Market Regime', https://indexes.morningstar.com/insights/analysis/bltca007ed69ad2ef48/dispersion-as-a-gauge-of-market-regime .


### `rp_curve_carry_147` (primitive `curve_carry`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-14, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: lookback=147. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: STRUCTURAL, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM. Nothing below asserts that curve trades, trend-following, or any live strategy has recently performed well. Every input is a level or a policy fact -- Treasury yields, the FRED 2s10s slope, the FOMC's July vote -- never a return. THE MECHANISM, and what is genuinely new about it. curve_carry is the ONLY primitive in this vocabulary that reads a real macro series rather than a price: it trend-follows the FRED DGS10-DGS2 slope and expresses the sign as a DV01-neutral TLT/IEF position. The hypothesis it tests is therefore narrow and checkable -- does the Treasury slope move in PERSISTENT regimes, or does it chop? Today's setup is the sharpest statement of that question the OOS window has offered, because the slope's two ends are now driven by two DIFFERENT forces on two DIFFERENT clocks, pulling in OPPOSITE directions. (a) THE LONG END IS STEEPENING on term-premium / inflation-risk compensation: the 10-year moved from under 4.40% in late June to 4.65% by 2026-08-10, and the reporting attributes it to investors repricing longer-run inflation risk and demanding more compensation for holding longer-term bonds -- a supply/credibility channel, not a growth channel. (b) THE FRONT END IS FLATTENING on a hawkish policy path: the 2-year sits at 4.19%, near its high for the year, after the Fed held at 3.50-3.75% for a fifth consecutive meeting in July with THREE dissents preferring a 25bp increase, and fixed-income futures now center on a September HIKE -- a reversal of the cut expectations priced in early 2026. The net 2s10s is +46bp as of 2026-08-10, having re-steepened from a +27bp low on 2026-06-19. That spread is the exact observable this primitive trades. WHY THIS ARGUES FOR A LONG LOOKBACK, which is the entire content of the parameter choice. The slope traversed roughly 19bp in eight weeks -- a fast, news-driven move (the hawkish repricing dates to mid-June) sitting on top of a slow structural drift (the term-premium normalization out of the 27-month July-2022-to-late-2024 inversion). A short lookback measures the news clock and gets whipsawed by exactly this kind of eight-week round trip; a lookback long enough to span a full policy cycle measures the drift instead. 147 trading days is ~7 months, roughly five FOMC meetings -- the shortest horizon that contains a complete decision cycle rather than a single repricing episode. I am choosing it from the mechanism's timescale, and it is the untested horizon that does that: the pre-registered (20, 252) range is already sampled at 21/42/63/84/126/189/252, and 147 is the midpoint of the 126-189 gap. THE HONEST CASE AGAINST, STATED FIRST AND IN FULL, because it is strong. (1) MY PRIOR IS DEAD, and the evidence is specifically about this primitive. Of the seven curve_carry draws already in this ledger, only three ever cleared harness.prelim_screen() -- 126/W, 189/M and 252/M -- and ALL THREE are DEAD in graveyard.csv, with OOS Sharpes of 0.257, 0.112 and 0.046 against a 60/40 benchmark Sharpe of 0.863. The four short draws (21/42/63/84) never even reached pipeline_preregistered.csv. 147/M is a horizon BETWEEN two dead neighbours, on the same rebalance clock as one of them. If slope trend-following is simply not a real effect, this candidate should and will die identically, and nothing in today's macro setup changes that. (2) THE STRUCTURAL STORY IS ABOUT NOW; THE VERDICT IS ABOUT 2018-2026. A regime description dated August 2026 cannot make a signal work across an eight-year OOS window. All today's research legitimately does is motivate WHICH HORIZON to test -- it is not, and must not be read as, evidence the candidate will pass. The primitive is scored on the whole window, not on whether it happens to be positioned correctly this month. (3) THE PARAMETER IS PARTLY GAP-FILLING, and two of the last three proposals in this ledger explicitly declined curve_carry for that reason. That critique is right about the parameter and wrong as a permanent rule: honoring it every run freezes curve_carry at 7 proposals forever while the other five primitives keep advancing, which is the opposite of the breadth this routine is meant to explore. So I am taking it, and labelling the weakness rather than dressing 147 up as a novel insight. (4) THE POSITION IS SMALL BY CONSTRUCTION. The three dead draws' OOS max drawdowns were -0.8% to -1.4% -- a DV01-neutral TLT/IEF spread is a low-variance object, so even a real slope effect may be too small to clear a gate-1 bar computed against a duty-cycle-matched null. (5) TWO OPPOSING DRIVERS IS AN ARGUMENT FOR CHOP, NOT TREND. Read honestly, (a) and (b) above describe a slope pinned between a steepening force and a flattening force, which is a mean-reverting setup rather than a trending one. I state it because it is the single strongest reason to expect DEAD: this proposal tests whether one force dominates long enough to trend, and the null is that they cancel. PRIMITIVE-CHOICE HONESTY. curve_carry is the least-proposed primitive in this ledger (7 of 55 prior rows, against 9 each for pair_zscore, cross_sectional_rank, single_asset_trend and asset_class_trend_hedge, and 12 for static_spread_carry), and it is the only one whose mechanism is defined on the exact series today's research is about. No other primitive can express a slope bet: pair_zscore on TLT/IEF (already proposed 2026-08-07) reads the PRICE ratio, not the real curve, and is a reversion rather than a trend claim; static_spread_carry on TLT/IEF (2026-08-04) is always-on and reads no macro data at all. Today's six earlier proposals used static_spread_carry, pair_zscore, asset_class_trend_hedge, single_asset_trend and cross_sectional_rank -- every primitive EXCEPT this one -- so this seventh slot is the day's contribution to coverage. freq=M because the slope's drivers update on the FOMC/CPI calendar (~monthly) and a 147-day signal has no business rebalancing faster; the duty-cycle-matched null charges the candidate for exactly the trading it does. DISTINCTNESS CHECKED MECHANICALLY, not asserted: grep for rp_curve_carry_147 returns zero rows in pipeline_ideas.csv, graveyard.csv and pipeline_preregistered.csv.

Citation: YIELD LEVELS AND CURVE SLOPE (load-bearing): 10-year at 4.65% and 2-year at 4.19% as of 2026-08-10, i.e. 2s10s = +46bp -- https://www.sofrrate.com/treasury-rates . The underlying primary series are FRED DGS10 (https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/DGS10), DGS2 (https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/DGS2) and T10Y2Y (https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/T10Y2Y) -- the SAME DGS10-DGS2 pair src/tradefabe/rates.py feeds this primitive -- plus US Treasury's Daily Par Yield Curve Rates (https://home.treasury.gov/resource-center/data-chart-center/interest-rates/TextView?type=daily_treasury_yield_curve&field_tdr_date_value=2026). Re-steepening from a +27bp low on 2026-06-19, and the 27-month July-2022-to-late-2024 inversion: https://centralbank.watch/tools/yield-curve/us-yield-curve/ . Corroborating slope reading (+35bp) and dormant bond volatility (MOVE 74.7): Janus Henderson, 'High yield bonds: can tight credit spreads persist?', https://www.janushenderson.com/en-us/investor/article/high-yield-bonds-can-tight-credit-spreads-persist/ -- cited ONLY for those two levels, not for any credit or performance claim. LONG-END DRIVER (term premium / inflation-risk repricing, not growth): CNBC, '2-year Treasury yield keeps going higher after spiking on hawkish start to Warsh's Fed', 2026-06-18, https://www.cnbc.com/2026/06/18/treasury-yields-investors-warsh-fed-interest-rates.html -- the 10-year stepping up from under 4.4% in late June, and the explicit statement that investors repriced longer-run inflation risk and demanded more compensation for holding longer-term bonds. FRONT-END DRIVER (policy path): CNBC, 'Fed meeting recap: July 2026', https://www.cnbc.com/2026/07/29/fed-meeting-today-live-updates.html , and TradingEconomics' US Fed Funds Rate page https://tradingeconomics.com/united-states/interest-rate -- target range held at 3.50-3.75% for a fifth consecutive meeting, three dissents preferring a 25bp increase. A September hike as the market-centered outcome: Forbes, 'September Rate Hike Becomes Most Likely Outcome According To Markets', 2026-07-29, https://www.forbes.com/sites/simonmoore/2026/07/29/september-rate-hike-becomes-most-likely-outcome-according-to-markets/ ; Chase, 'Will the Fed Hike Rates in September? A 25-Basis-Point Move Is Now Expected', https://www.chase.com/personal/investments/learning-and-insights/article/september-2026-rate-hike-now-expected-amid-energy-shocks . NOT TAKEN FROM THE WEB: the three DEAD curve_carry verdicts (OOS Sharpe 0.257/0.112/0.046, max drawdowns -0.8% to -1.4%, benchmark Sharpe 0.863), the seven already-sampled lookbacks, and which of them cleared the prelim screen are all read directly from this repo's graveyard.csv, pipeline_ideas.csv and pipeline_preregistered.csv. NOTE ON WHAT IS DELIBERATELY ABSENT: no source above is cited for how any strategy, fund or factor has recently performed; every citation is a level, a policy fact, or a series/instrument definition, per this lab's pre-registration discipline.


### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_USO_IEF_63_168` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-14, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=USO, ticker_b=IEF, lookback_a=63, lookback_b=168. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: STRUCTURAL, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM. Nothing below says that trend-following, commodity strategies, or any live fund has recently performed well. Every input is a physical quantity, a price level, a policy fact or a published correlation study -- never a strategy return. THE MECHANISM. asset_class_trend_hedge holds two INDEPENDENT trend legs, each long or short by the sign of its own trailing return, and the primitive's own requirement is that the two legs come from different asset classes AND that a specific mechanism, not a correlation search, explains why they offset. Mine: the two legs sit on OPPOSITE SIDES OF THE INFLATION AXIS, and their trends are set by two different physical/institutional clocks. Leg A (USO, commodity) is driven by barrels -- OPEC+ quota decisions and Middle East transit risk, which move on a meeting-and-headline clock of weeks to a quarter. Leg B (IEF, rates, 7-10y) is driven by the Fed's policy path, which moves on an FOMC/CPI clock of six-to-twelve months and only responds to oil with a long pass-through lag. The OFFSET claim is specifically this: whichever way the shock runs, the signed legs end up on opposite sides of it. In a SUPPLY/INFLATION shock, oil trends up and duration trends down, so the candidate is long USO and short IEF. In a GROWTH/DEMAND shock, oil trends down and duration rallies, so it flips to short USO and long IEF. Neither state is a levered bet on one factor; the pair is always straddling the inflation axis rather than doubling down on it. That is also why I expect the signals to decorrelate mechanically rather than merely economically -- the legs measure different clocks (63 vs 168 days) on different drivers, which is what a low trend-sign correlation IS. WHY NOW, AND WHY THIS MATTERS FOR THE PARAMETER CHOICE. The current setup is the cleanest live statement of the two-clock structure the OOS window has offered. (a) The BARREL CLOCK is running fast and is supply-side, not demand-side: the market is short roughly 1.8 mb/d this quarter on the Iran/Strait of Hormuz disruption, with WTI near $84 on 2026-08-12 after closing at $82.13 on 2026-08-10, and prices re-rating on individual diplomatic setbacks -- a headline-driven, quarter-scale regime, which is what a 63-day lookback measures. (b) The POLICY CLOCK is running slow and in the opposite direction: the Fed held at 3.50-3.75% for a fifth consecutive meeting in July 2026 with THREE dissents preferring a 25bp increase, and a September HIKE is now the market-centered outcome, explicitly framed as a response to energy-driven inflation. A policy turn from cuts to hikes is a multi-meeting object; 168 trading days is ~8 months, ~6 FOMC meetings, the shortest horizon that contains a complete turn rather than one repricing. I am choosing both lookbacks from their mechanisms' timescales, not from a grid. (c) The regime this describes -- an energy-led inflation shock -- is exactly the one the stock-bond correlation literature identifies as the state where duration stops hedging and stocks and bonds fall together, i.e. where a rates leg is NOT interchangeable with an equity leg. That is the specific reason to pair a commodity leg with a RATES leg here rather than the equity/commodity pairings this ledger already holds. THE HONEST CASE AGAINST, STATED IN FULL. (1) MY PRIOR IS DEAD. Every predictive strategy this lab has tested is dead against pre-registered kill rules, and that includes the closest neighbours to this one: single_asset_trend on USO at 126 days and on TLT at 126 days are both already proposed here, and the two existing commodity-touching asset_class_trend_hedge draws (TLT/DBC 252/60, QQQ/USO 200/40) test the same family of claim. If trend on either leg is noise, combining two noisy legs produces a noisier candidate, not a better one. (2) USO IS NOT CLEAN OIL EXPOSURE. It holds front-month futures and pays the roll; across most of the 2018-2026 OOS window the curve was in contango, and the 2020 episode mangled the fund's structure outright. A long leg bleeds roll in contango even when spot oil is flat, and the current backwardation that would help it is a feature of TODAY, not of the window the verdict is computed on. This is the single most likely reason the candidate dies. (3) THE STRUCTURAL STORY IS ABOUT NOW; THE VERDICT IS ABOUT 2018-2026. One month of Hormuz risk premium cannot make a signal work across eight years. The research legitimately motivates WHICH pair and WHICH horizons to test and nothing more; it is not evidence the candidate passes. (4) THE DECORRELATION GUARD MAY REJECT IT, and I would rather say so than pretend otherwise. On 2007-2017 calibration data IEF's 168-day trend sign was positive through most of a secular bond bull market, which dampens its correlation with anything; but oil's two big crashes (2008, 2014-16) coincided with flight-to-quality bond rallies, which pushes the trend-sign correlation negative. If |corr| exceeds the 0.3 cap the screen should and will kill this, and that is the guard working, not a bug in the idea. (5) TWO LEGS, TWO CHANCES TO WHIPSAW. A sign-flip signal near a flat trend churns, and freq=W charges turnover on both legs; the duty-cycle-matched null prices exactly that trading. PRIMITIVE-CHOICE HONESTY. asset_class_trend_hedge stands at 9 of the 56 prior rows in this ledger, tied with pair_zscore, cross_sectional_rank and single_asset_trend, against 12 for static_spread_carry and 8 for curve_carry. curve_carry is nominally the least-used, but it was taken by the immediately preceding run (rp_curve_carry_147, 2026-08-13) and its one-dimensional (20, 252) space is now sampled at eight points, so a ninth draw would be gap-filling rather than exploration. The commodity-vs-rates corner of asset_class_trend_hedge is genuinely under-explored by contrast: its only prior rates-leg-plus-commodity draw is TLT/DBC, and TLT is a term-premium instrument against a broad commodity basket, whereas IEF is a policy-path instrument against a single, supply-shocked energy leg -- a different claim on both sides, not a reparametrization. This is today's FIRST proposal (slot 1 of 10; zero rows in this ledger carry a 2026-08-14 UTC date), so it is not steering around anything proposed earlier today. freq=W because the faster leg's driver updates on a headline/OPEC clock of weeks and the slower leg's on the FOMC calendar; daily rebalancing would pay costs for noise the 63-day signal cannot resolve. DISTINCTNESS CHECKED MECHANICALLY, not asserted: grep for USO_IEF returns zero rows in pipeline_ideas.csv, graveyard.csv and pipeline_preregistered.csv.

Citation: OIL SUPPLY SHOCK AND PRICE LEVELS (load-bearing for leg A's clock): CNBC, 'Oil prices today: Uncertainty over U.S.-Iran Strait of Hormuz deal', 2026-08-10, https://www.cnbc.com/2026/08/10/oil-prices-today-brent-wti-hormuz-trump-iran.html -- WTI closing at $82.13 on 2026-08-10 and re-rating on Hormuz diplomacy; TradingEconomics crude oil series https://tradingeconomics.com/commodity/crude-oil for the ~$83-84 level on 2026-08-12 and the ~1.8 mb/d quarterly shortfall. The opposing supply-side force is cited too, not suppressed: OPEC+ raised August output by 188 kb/d and the IEA's own surplus arithmetic points at a large 2026 oversupply -- IEA Oil Market Report, August 2026, https://www.iea.org/reports/oil-market-report-august-2026 (page blocked by this environment's egress proxy; cited from its search-indexed summary, so treat the exact surplus figure as indicative) and https://www.kavout.com/market-lens/opec-production-boost-strips-oil-s-geopolitical-cushion-accelerating-2026-supply-glut . That the two forces disagree is the point of a TREND leg rather than a directional view: the primitive reads whichever one is currently winning, it does not assume one. POLICY PATH (load-bearing for leg B's clock): CNBC, 'Divided Fed holds interest rates steady, but three members voted to hike', 2026-07-29, https://www.cnbc.com/2026/07/29/fed-rate-decision-july-2026.html -- the hold at 3.50-3.75% and the three dissents; Chase, 'Will the Fed Hike Rates in September? A 25-Basis-Point Move Is Now Expected', https://www.chase.com/personal/investments/learning-and-insights/article/september-2026-rate-hike-now-expected-amid-energy-shocks -- a September hike as the expected outcome, explicitly attributed to energy-driven inflation, which is the oil-to-policy transmission this proposal's two-clock argument depends on. Underlying primary series for the rates leg: FRED DGS10 https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/DGS10 and the 5-year breakeven T5YIE https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/T5YIE . Instrument definition for leg B: iShares 7-10 Year Treasury Bond ETF (IEF) https://www.ishares.com/us/products/239456/ishares-710-year-treasury-bond-etf . CORRELATION-REGIME EVIDENCE (why the hedging leg must be RATES, and why that hedge is conditional): J.P. Morgan, 'Beyond bonds: How to protect against inflation-led shocks', https://www.jpmorgan.com/insights/markets-and-economy/top-market-takeaways/tmt-beyond-bonds-how-to-protect-against-inflation-led-shocks -- bonds hedge growth shocks but fall with equities in inflation shocks; and the Financial Analysts Journal empirical study of the stock-bond correlation, https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/full/10.1080/0015198X.2024.2317333 , for the finding that the correlation's SIGN is set by the shock type (demand/flight-to-quality negative, monetary-policy/inflation-surprise positive). Cited for the regime mechanism only -- neither source is cited for any strategy's performance.
