### `rp_single_asset_trend_IWM_168` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-14, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=IWM, lookback=168. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: STRUCTURAL, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM. Nothing below asserts that trend-following, small-cap exposure, or any live strategy has recently performed well. Every input is a policy fact, a rate level, or a balance-sheet/maturity-schedule fact -- never a return. THE MECHANISM. Small-cap equity is the one equity exposure in this UNIVERSE whose earnings are mechanically levered to the front end of the curve, and the front end is now repricing HAWKISH for the first time in this cycle. (a) POLICY: the FOMC held at 3.50-3.75% on 2026-07-29 for a fifth consecutive meeting, but the vote was 9-3 with Hammack, Kashkari and Logan all dissenting IN FAVOUR OF A 25bp HIKE -- the first time since September 2016 that three policymakers dissented with a unified direction. Futures now imply roughly a 65% chance of a September hike, and BofA's own Q2-2026 guidance is explicitly built on a forward curve containing one 25bp September hike, so the hike is in the curve, not a forecast of mine. The stated drivers are energy/supply-chain shocks tied to the Iran conflict and doubt about the Fed's inflation credibility -- a cost-push channel, not a demand channel. (b) TRANSMISSION: small caps carry far more floating-rate debt than large caps (roughly a third of Russell 2000 constituents; market commentary puts it at ~32% of R2K debt versus ~6% for the S&P 500), so a front-end move hits their interest expense directly rather than through a discount rate. SOFR sits near 4.3%; Fitch estimates each 25bp of SOFR adds ~$3.5bn of annual interest expense across the leveraged-loan universe. US speculative-grade borrowers must refinance ~$397bn in 2026 (about half floating-rate loans and revolvers), with small-cap-specific commentary putting the 2026 wall at ~$368bn refinancing near 6.5% against 1-2% legacy coupons, i.e. 150-200bp above the coupons being retired. WHY THIS IS A TREND HYPOTHESIS AND NOT A MEAN-REVERSION ONE -- the whole content of the primitive choice. The shock is already public, but it does NOT arrive at announcement: it lands borrower-by-borrower on each firm's own refinancing date and quarterly SOFR reset, and only becomes visible in reported interest expense at that firm's next earnings print. That is precisely Hong & Stein's (1999) gradual-information-diffusion condition -- a known fundamental impairment released on a mechanical calendar over several quarters -- which is the only respectable reason to expect a price series' own trailing sign to persist rather than revert. A pair or z-score primitive would assume the divergence closes; this thesis says it accretes on a refinancing schedule, so time-series trend is the right shape and mean-reversion is the wrong one. NOTE THE SIGN IS NOT ASSUMED: single_asset_trend is symmetric, so if the September hike does not land and the front end unwinds, the same signal takes the other side -- the claim under test is that the diffusion is SLOW, not that small caps fall. WHY lookback=168 (~8 months). The transmission clock is the quarterly SOFR reset plus the quarterly earnings disclosure that reveals it; 168 trading days spans roughly three such cycles, long enough for the accretion to accumulate across multiple reporting dates and long enough not to be whipsawed by a single FOMC headline (the September meeting itself), which a 20-60d lookback would chase. WHY freq=M: the underlying channel updates at quarterly earnings, so a monthly rebalance samples it several times over without paying daily turnover to track rate noise. NOVELTY: IWM has never been proposed for single_asset_trend by this pipeline (existing single_asset_trend proposals cover GLD, SLV, VNQ, UUP, EEM, USO, TLT, DBC, EFA), and no rp_single_asset_trend_IWM_* spec exists in pipeline_ideas.csv or graveyard.csv. PRIOR: family-level trend and cross-sectional momentum are DEAD in this lab; this proposal expects to fail the same gates and is offered because the mechanism is specific and checkable, not because trend deserves another look.

Citation: FOMC July 2026 hold and 9-3 vote with three hike dissents (Hammack/Kashkari/Logan): CNBC, 'Fed rate decision July 2026: Divided Fed holds interest rates steady', 2026-07-29, https://www.cnbc.com/2026/07/29/fed-rate-decision-july-2026.html . September hike expectation (~65% futures-implied; J.P. Morgan Wealth Management penciling +25bp): Chase Learning & Insights, 'Will the Fed Hike Rates in September?', 2026-08. Small-cap floating-rate share (~32% of R2K debt vs ~6% for the S&P 500), the ~$368bn 2026 small-cap maturity wall and BofA's ~2%-of-R2K-operating-earnings-per-25bp estimate: BingX Learn, 'Russell 2000 Forecast 2026: Small-Cap Breakout or Floating-Rate Debt Trap?' (secondary market commentary -- cited as reported, not as a primary source). Leveraged-loan refinancing wall (~$397bn US speculative-grade due 2026, ~half floating-rate) and Fitch's ~$3.5bn-per-25bp-of-SOFR interest-expense estimate with SOFR near 4.3%: Crystal Funds, 'Floating Rate Debt in a High Rate World', https://www.crystalfunds.com/insights/floating-rate-debt-in-high-rate-world . Mechanism for slow diffusion of known fundamental news: Hong & Stein (1999), 'A Unified Theory of Underreaction, Momentum Trading and Overreaction in Asset Markets', Journal of Finance 54(6); time-series-momentum formulation: Moskowitz, Ooi & Pedersen (2012), 'Time Series Momentum', Journal of Financial Economics 104(2).


### `rp_pair_zscore_EEM_EFA_84_2p0_4p5` (primitive `pair_zscore`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-15, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=EEM, ticker_b=EFA, z_window=84, z_entry=2.0, z_stop=4.5. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: EEM and EFA are the same trade in economic terms -- non-US equity beta funded and reported in dollars -- so their log-spread is close to a pure relative-valuation series rather than two independent risk assets, which is the precondition a z-score mean-reversion primitive needs. The current structural setup is a wedge in the CURRENCY-TRANSLATION term, not in fundamentals: the dollar's 2026 move has been concentrated against the developed-market currencies EFA actually holds (EUR/JPY/GBP -- DXY near the top of its 52-week range, then falling past 99.6 toward a two-month low after July retail sales dropped and both CPI and PPI softened), while the broad and emerging-market dollar indices have barely moved (EM index +0.06% over 30 days, -0.25% on the week) -- DXY excludes CNY and the other major EM currencies entirely. A dollar regime that repriced one leg's FX translation and left the other's flat pushes the EEM/EFA log-spread away from its mean for a reason unrelated to either region's earnings, and FX translation wedges are the component of a cross-region equity spread with the clearest reason to be mean-reverting. Reinforcing, not load-bearing: EM sits at ~14-15x forward earnings against ~24x for MSCI World, so there is no fundamental repricing story that would make a stretched spread the new level. z_window=84 (about four months) defines 'unusual' relative to the CURRENT dollar regime rather than averaging across the 2025 regime shift; z_entry=2.0 with z_stop=4.5 fades a genuine dislocation while exiting rather than doubling down if the spread keeps running, which is what a real regime break would look like. Weekly rebalance because an FX-translation wedge resolves over weeks -- daily would pay turnover for noise. Stated honestly: every predictive strategy this lab has tested is DEAD, cross-region mean reversion included, and a plausible mechanism is not evidence of an edge -- this is a candidate for the gate, not a claim it will pass.

Citation: TradingEconomics, US Dollar (Aug 2026): DXY fell past 99.6, approaching a two-month low of 95.53 on Aug 7 after July retail sales dropped and CPI/PPI softened -- https://tradingeconomics.com/united-states/currency ; S&P Global / iBoxx USD and EUR Emerging Markets Indices review: DXY sits near the top of its 52-week range while the broad and emerging-market dollar indices have barely moved (EM index +0.06% over 30 days, -0.25% on the week), and DXY excludes major EM currencies including CNY -- https://www.spglobal.com/spdji/en/commentary/article/iboxx-usd-and-eur-emerging-markets-indices-2025-review ; J.P. Morgan, After The Rate Cut: Investing Beyond U.S. Markets -- Europe and emerging markets trade at about 15x NTM earnings, a ~40% discount to US equities -- https://www.jpmorgan.com/insights/markets-and-economy/top-market-takeaways/tmt-after-the-rate-cut-investing-beyond-us-markets ; Vested Finance, Developed vs. Emerging Markets 2026: MSCI EM P/E 18.8 vs MSCI World 24.13 (Feb 2026) -- https://vestedfinance.com/blog/global-funds/developed-vs-emerging-markets-a-global-funds-guide/


### `rp_single_asset_trend_HYG_84` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-15, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=HYG, lookback=84. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: Structural setup, not a performance claim. HY index OAS entered August 2026 at 271-284bp -- the richest decile of its own history against a long-run median near 450bp -- with IG at ~81bp and the HY/IG ratio near 3.5x, i.e. at its long-run average, so the tightness is market-wide rather than one quality tier's distortion. On HYG's short spread duration (~3-4y) that configuration makes the asset's payoff structurally asymmetric right now rather than merely expensive: remaining compression toward any plausible floor is worth roughly a point of price, while a move back toward the historical median is several times that in the other direction. The asymmetry is not only arithmetic -- widening is already concentrated in the single-B/CCC tiers beneath a stable index level, the bifurcation pattern that also preceded the 2015-16 energy and early-2020 episodes. single_asset_trend is the vocabulary's mechanical way to hold a bounded-upside / unbounded-downside carry asset only while it is not repricing: the trailing-return sign accrues the ~6% index yield in quiet regimes and goes flat/short once a widening episode is underway. lookback=84 (~one quarter of trading days) because credit widening episodes historically develop over roughly a quarter -- long enough to ignore HYG's own NAV premium/discount noise, short enough to exit inside an episode rather than after it; W rebalance for the same reason, since a monthly clock can lag the exit by up to a month while a daily one churns a carry asset against its bid-ask. Explicitly NOT a claim that trend-following works -- this lab's own record says it does not; the claim is only about the current shape of this one asset's return distribution. HYG has not been proposed under any primitive before, and single_asset_trend is among the least-used primitives in the ledger.

Citation: Convex, 'HY Credit Spread (OAS): 271 bps (Aug 12, 2026)' -- HY OAS 271bp, IG 81bp, BBB 100bp, HY/IG ~3.5x, richest decile vs a ~450bp long-run median; policy backdrop Fed effective funds 3.63%, MOVE 74.7, 2s10s ~+35bp -- https://convextrade.com/metrics/bamlh0a0hym2 ; Equity Capital Market, 'Junk Bonds in 2026: What Widening Credit Spreads Are Signaling' -- single-B/CCC spread widening beneath a stable index level, the late-cycle bifurcation also seen in 2015-16 and the weeks into 2020 -- https://ecmsource.com/junk-bond-credit-spreads-2026-corporate-debt/ ; Yahoo Finance, '6.7% yield with short duration gives HYG an edge over riskier bond funds' -- HYG's short duration and 30-day SEC yield above 6% -- https://finance.yahoo.com/markets/options/articles/6-7-yield-short-duration-133033431.html ; Charles Schwab, '2026 Corporate Credit Outlook' -- https://www.schwab.com/learn/story/corporate-bond-outlook


### `rp_static_spread_carry_GLD_DBC_a` (primitive `static_spread_carry`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-15, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=GLD, ticker_b=DBC, long_leg=a. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: Structural roll-carry differential between a physically-backed and a futures-backed commodity ETF, not a directional commodity view. GLD holds allocated bullion and pays no roll cost at all; DBC is a futures-based basket whose largest weight block is energy, and the WTI curve has flipped into sustained contango for much of 2026 with the IEA projecting record oversupply and OPEC itself revising from a supply deficit to a Q3 supply surplus. Invesco's Optimum Yield roll reduces but explicitly does not eliminate that drag -- in a sustained contango even the best roll rule opens a gap between spot performance and what the fund delivers. On the long leg, gold carries a structural, price-insensitive official-sector bid no other index commodity has (PBOC bought ~20t in July 2026 and kept buying through the price pullback; Singapore, the Czech Republic and Kazakhstan also added). So the spread is always-on and fixed by construction: collect the absence of negative roll carry plus a reserve-demand floor on one leg against a basket structurally paying negative roll on the other. Same commodity asset class on both legs, so this is a carry/structure bet rather than a cross-asset directional one. Monthly rebalance, matching every other static_spread_carry proposal and the 60/40 benchmark's own clock, keeps turnover cost minimal for a signal that never changes sign.

Citation: gCaptain, 'Oil Contango Deepens for 2026 as Trade Tensions Stoke Glut Fears' (https://gcaptain.com/oil-contango-deepens-for-2026-as-trade-tensions-stoke-glut-fears/) and iTiger, 'Crude Futures Curve Indicates Supply Glut Most Pronounced in U.S. Market' (https://www.itiger.com/hans/news/1146232013) -- WTI timespreads in contango through 2026, OPEC revising to a Q3 supply surplus, IEA projecting record oversupply. 24/7 Wall St., 'The Commodity ETF That Solved K-1 Complexity...' (https://247wallst.com/investing/2026/04/20/the-commodity-etf-that-solved-k-1-complexity-and-delivered-89-in-five-years/) -- DBC's Optimum Yield roll reduces but does not eliminate contango cost; a sustained contango still opens a spot-vs-fund gap. GoldPrice.com, 'Macro Drivers Behind the July-August 2026 Gold Rally' (https://goldprice.com/news/macro-drivers-behind-the-julyaugust-2026-gold-rally-databacked-insights-for-institutional-investors) and SSGA August 2026 Monthly Gold Monitor (https://www.ssga.com/library-content/products/fund-docs/etfs/us/insights-investment-ideas/monthly-gold-monitor.pdf) -- continued official-sector accumulation (PBOC ~20t in July 2026) through the pullback, a structural demand floor specific to gold.


### `rp_cross_sectional_rank_low_vol_126_5` (primitive `cross_sectional_rank`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-15, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: metric=low_vol, lookback=126, k=5. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: Cross-asset diversification by asset-class LABEL is structurally degrading right now, which is the setup this candidate is built for. Oxford Economics' 2026 call is that the stock-bond correlation returns to POSITIVE this year, driven by continued supply shocks, geopolitical risk and higher macro volatility from more activist fiscal policy pushing term premia higher -- explicitly framed as a problem for allocations that rely on long duration as equity ballast. CFR documents the same shift one level up: VIX and MOVE, which before ~2013 co-moved only in acute crises, now co-move as the norm, so an equity shock and a rates shock increasingly arrive together. March 2026 is the realized instance -- a correlation shock in which the traditional hedges failed simultaneously (gold about -18% intramonth, 10y Treasuries more than -4% over the same stretch), and the move was described as pervasive precisely because rates, FX and commodities were emerging from unusually low vol. The Fed holding at 3.50-3.75% with markets repricing from cuts toward possible hikes keeps that shared macro-vol driver live rather than resolved. MECHANISM: if a single macro-volatility factor is what moves every bucket together, then the asset-class label carries less cross-sectional information and each asset's OWN realized volatility carries more -- it is the direct measure of exposure to the factor that is actually doing the moving. Ranking the whole UNIVERSE by trailing realized vol and going long the 5 lowest / short the 5 highest is a label-blind expression of that ordering, dollar-neutral by construction against the common factor rather than hedged via an equity-vs-rates relationship the research above says is breaking. PARAMETERS: k=5 (of 15 tickers) deliberately broad -- the claim is about a factor ordering across the whole cross-section, not about a few extreme names, and every k proposed by this pipeline so far has been 1-4, so the broad end of the pre-registered range is untested. lookback=126 (~6 months) is long enough for a stable realized-vol estimate yet short enough to re-rank within the multi-month cadence at which the sources describe this regime moving; the two prior low_vol proposals sit at 63 and 252, so 126 fills the middle of the range. freq=W because realized-vol rankings are far more persistent than return rankings -- daily rebalancing would pay turnover for a signal that barely changes -- while monthly is slower than the regime shifts cited. HONEST PRIOR: this lab has killed cross-sectional momentum and every other predictive strategy tested, and low-vol is a well-known, heavily-mined anomaly; the argument here is not that low-vol is undiscovered, it is that the specific correlation regime makes a vol-ordered cross-section the structurally coherent construction to test now. It faces the same DSR/CPCV gate as everything else and should be expected to die there.

Citation: Oxford Economics, 'Stock bond correlation will become positive again in 2026' (https://www.oxfordeconomics.com/resource/stock-bond-correlation-will-become-positive-again-in-2026/); Council on Foreign Relations, 'Rising Stock-Bond Volatility Correlation Is Undermining Global Risk Models' (https://www.cfr.org/articles/rising-stock-bond-volatility-correlation-is-undermining-global-risk-models); Resonanz Capital, 'After the Correlation Shock: How March 2026 Broke -- and Reshaped -- a Popular Vol Trade' (https://resonanzcapital.com/insights/after-the-correlation-shock-how-march-2026-broke-and-reshaped-a-popular-vol-trade); NSP Group, 'Market Volatility Surge: A Perfect Storm in March 2026' (https://nspgroup.com/insights/investment/market-volatility-march-2026/); Federal Reserve FOMC July 29 2026 hold at 3.50-3.75% via TradingEconomics (https://tradingeconomics.com/united-states/interest-rate). Accessed 2026-08-15.


### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_LQD_DBC_126_63` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-15, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=LQD, ticker_b=DBC, lookback_a=126, lookback_b=63. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: Regime argument for pairing a duration leg against an inflation-beta leg rather than the usual equity-vs-bond hedge. (1) IG credit spreads are at multi-decade tights (index OAS ~74bp, roughly the 1st percentile of a 20-year lookback; BBB OAS ~100bp near 25-year tights), so LQD's return variance right now is dominated by rate duration rather than by spread -- it acts as a long-duration leg with little spread left to tighten into. (2) The curve is bear-steepening on term premium rather than on growth: 2026-08-14 marks 2y 4.17%, 10y 4.68% (2s10s ~+51bp) with the 30y ~5.26%, and the stated driver is fiscal supply plus inflation volatility. The specific offsetting mechanism: an inflation/term-premium shock pushes duration trends down and broad commodity trends up, so the two legs' trailing-return signs should carry opposite sign under the dominant macro shock -- a rate-sensitivity vs inflation-beta offset, not a correlation search. (3) The equity-vs-bond form of this hedge is structurally weaker in exactly this regime, since the stock-bond correlation is expected to turn positive again in 2026 for the same inflation-shock reason. Lookbacks: 126d on the rates leg (duration trends are slow and policy-anchored), 63d on the commodity leg (commodity trends turn faster on supply shocks).

Citation: Curve level/slope: 'Treasury Yields Snapshot: August 14, 2026', etftrends.com (https://www.etftrends.com/fixed-income-content-hub/treasury-yields-snapshot-august-14-2026/) and the centralbank.watch US yield-curve tool (https://centralbank.watch/tools/yield-curve/us-yield-curve/) -- 2y 4.17-4.18%, 10y 4.68-4.70%, 30y 5.26% as of 2026-08-14. IG spread tightness: Q3-2026 corporate bond outlooks surveyed via web search report index OAS ~74bp at ~1st percentile over 20 years (Breckinridge Q3 2026 Corporate Bond Market Outlook, https://www.breckinridge.com/insights/q3-2026-corporate-bond-market-outlook) and BBB OAS ~100bp near 25-year tights (Schwab 2026 Corporate Credit Outlook, https://www.schwab.com/learn/story/corporate-bond-outlook); direct page fetches were blocked by this environment's egress proxy, so those two figures are as reported in search summaries rather than read off the source pages. Stock-bond correlation regime: Oxford Economics, 'Stock bond correlation will become positive again in 2026' (https://www.oxfordeconomics.com/resource/stock-bond-correlation-will-become-positive-again-in-2026/). Mechanism background: Campbell, Pflueger & Viceira, 'Macroeconomic Drivers of Bond and Equity Risks', Journal of Political Economy 128(8), 2020.


### `rp_cross_sectional_rank_momentum_168_5` (primitive `cross_sectional_rank`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-15, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: metric=momentum, lookback=168, k=5. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: The SF Fed's August 2026 Economic Letter reports that the stock-bond correlation has flipped and the stock-oil correlation changed accordingly, which it reads as the perceived source of macro risk shifting from demand shocks to supply shocks, with elevated oil prices and inflation now the prominent priced risk. That is a claim about the whole cross-section, not a two-leg pair: under a demand-driven regime a mixed asset-class universe collapses toward a single risk-on/risk-off factor, so relative ranks churn; under a persistent supply-shock factor real assets (DBC, USO, GLD, SLV) and nominal assets (TLT, IEF, LQD, HYG) separate durably, which is the dispersion a cross-sectional momentum rank needs to have anything to bite on. Corroborating structure: the curve has exited the longest inversion in the FRED series and re-steepened to roughly +35 to +52bp with the steepening attributed to term premium and fiscal supply rather than growth, and the IMF (Feb 2026) finds stock-bond diversification now offers less protection in selloffs -- both consistent with a nominal-vs-real split rather than a risk-on/risk-off one. A 168-day (~8 month) lookback spans the post-flip window without averaging deep into the prior demand-driven regime; k=5 of 15 tickers per side expresses an asset-class-bucket separation rather than name selection; monthly rebalance matches a slow structural factor. Not a performance claim -- no assertion is made about how cross-sectional momentum has recently performed; the four cross_sectional_rank momentum specs this pipeline has already tested are in graveyard.csv.

Citation: FRBSF Economic Letter, 'Financial Markets, Oil Prices, and Supply-Side Risks', August 2026 (frbsf.org/research-and-insights/publications/economic-letter/2026/08/financial-markets-oil-prices-and-supply-side-risks/); IMF Blog, 'Stock-Bond Diversification Offers Less Protection From Market Selloffs', 18 Feb 2026; Asness/Israelov/Liew-style cross-asset momentum convention, cf. Asness, Moskowitz & Pedersen (2013) 'Value and Momentum Everywhere', Journal of Finance 68(3).


### `rp_curve_carry_210` (primitive `curve_carry`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-15, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: lookback=210. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: The 2s10s curve has exited the deepest inversion in the modern series (a -108bp trough) and sits at roughly +35 to +52bp in mid-August 2026 (2y 4.15-4.18% on 13 Aug, 10y ~4.70%), and the driver is structural at the LONG end rather than cyclical at the front. The NY Fed's ACM 10-year term premium is positive for the first time since 2023 (~+0.51% June 2026, +0.83% in the 15 May daily snapshot) on three slow-moving forces: the cumulative duration returned to the market by QT since June 2022 (>$2tn), widened federal deficits expanding net Treasury supply, and an upward revision to r-star. Meanwhile the front end is pinned and contested, not trending: the 29 July 2026 FOMC HELD at 3.50-3.75% on a 9-3 vote with Hammack, Kashkari and Logan dissenting toward a HIKE on persistent inflation, so 2y at ~4.15% sits above the policy rate rather than pricing a clean easing path. That asymmetry -- a persistent, multi-year supply/term-premium drift steepening the curve from the long end against a two-sided, meeting-to-meeting front end -- is exactly the condition under which curve_carry's mechanism (trend-follow the DGS10-DGS2 slope with a DV01-neutral TLT/IEF position) has a durable signal to track rather than whipsaw, and its DV01 construction is what isolates the slope from the level move the term-premium repricing also produces. Parameters: a 210-day (~10 month) slope lookback measures the change over roughly the length of the current re-steepening leg -- long enough to be dominated by the structural drift rather than by policy-meeting jitter (currently large and two-sided), short enough not to average back across the inversion-to-steepening turn the way the 252-day window does at this point in the cycle; it is also the one unsampled rung of the 21-day grid this pipeline has already tested (21/42/63/84/105/126/147/168/189/252). Weekly rebalance because a 10-month slope trend changes far too slowly to justify daily turnover, while monthly would hold a stale DV01 position through the 16 September FOMC and its updated projections; no weekly curve_carry beyond a 126-day lookback has been proposed. Bond volatility is dormant (MOVE 74.7), which is the reason to read the slope over quarters rather than weeks, not a claim about returns. Explicitly NOT a performance claim: nothing here asserts how curve or trend strategies have recently done -- the ten curve_carry specs this pipeline has already tested are in graveyard.csv and pipeline_ideas.csv.

Citation: FOMC statement and Implementation Note, 29 July 2026 (federalreserve.gov/newsevents/pressreleases/monetary20260729a1.htm; target range held at 3.50-3.75%, 9-3 vote, three dissents preferring a 25bp increase), reported CNBC 29 Jul 2026 'Fed rate decision July 2026: Divided Fed holds interest rates steady'; 2y/10y levels via Convex metrics DGS2 (4.15%, 13 Aug 2026) and centralbank.watch US yield curve tool (2s10s ~+52bp, 14 Aug 2026); NY Fed ACM 10-year term premium via CEIC (0.513%, Jun 2026) and eco3min.fr ACMTP10 decomposition (+0.83% daily, 15 May 2026; QT duration return, fiscal supply and r-star revision as the three drivers); MOVE index 74.7 per the August 2026 credit/rates market structure review. Mechanism reference: Adrian, Crump & Moench (2013), 'Pricing the Term Structure with Linear Regressions', Journal of Financial Economics 110(1).


### `rp_pair_zscore_IWM_SPY_105_2p5_5p0` (primitive `pair_zscore`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-15, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=IWM, ticker_b=SPY, z_window=105, z_entry=2.5, z_stop=5.0. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: pair_zscore needs a spread whose DRIVER oscillates rather than drifts, and the small-cap-vs-large-cap relative is currently tied to the one part of the curve that is pinned and two-sided. The structural asymmetry is documented and large: roughly 32% of Russell 2000 debt is floating-rate against about 6% for the S&P 500 (WisdomTree's independent cut of the same gap puts it at ~40% of S&P 600 constituents carrying floating-rate debt versus ~10% of S&P 500 constituents), and small caps face a ~$368bn 2026 maturity wall rolling from 1-2% coupons into ~6.5%. That makes the IWM-vs-SPY log spread an unusually direct expression of the FRONT end of the curve -- SOFR is 3.64% and the funds target 3.50-3.75%. WHY THE DRIVER IS RANGE-BOUND RIGHT NOW, NOT TRENDING: the 29 July 2026 FOMC held on a 9-3 vote with Hammack, Kashkari and Logan all dissenting toward a HIKE -- the first time since September 2016 that three policymakers dissented in a unified direction, and the most divided decision since 2016. A committee split between holding and hiking, with inflation above target for five years on one side and a July payrolls print of -23,000 on the other, is a policy rate that gets repriced back and forth on each data release rather than one following a delivered path. Repeated two-sided repricing of the front end mechanically drives repeated two-sided moves in a relative whose sensitivity to the front end is ~5x on one leg versus the other, which is the dislocate-and-close pattern a z-score fade is built for. Separately, the part of the curve that IS trending -- the long end, on term premium and fiscal supply -- hits both indices' discount rates in the same direction, so it largely differences out of the spread rather than imparting drift to it. PARAMETERS: z_window=105 (~5 months) spans roughly three FOMC cycles, so the z-score is normalized against the current hold-versus-hike regime rather than against the delivered-cuts period that preceded it; no pair_zscore proposal has used 105 (the ledger has 40/45/60/63/84/90/120). z_entry=2.5 is deliberately at the strict end of the pre-registered [1.5, 3.0] range -- every prior proposal here sits at 1.75-2.25 -- because this spread, unlike a hard-anchored one, does carry real multi-year drift from mega-cap concentration, and a low entry would repeatedly fight that drift; only genuinely large dislocations relative to the trailing window are worth fading. z_stop=5.0 is correspondingly wide so the position is exited on a regime break, not on ordinary noise past a tight entry. freq=W because policy expectations reprice on a meeting-and-release cadence, not intraday; daily would pay turnover on a signal that changes at data frequency. HONEST PRIOR: this lab has killed every predictive strategy tested, mean-reversion pairs included (family N, and eleven prior rp_pair_zscore specs). The level evidence also cuts against pure stationarity -- small caps sit far below and large caps far above their long-term trends, which is a divergence a rolling z-score partly absorbs into its own mean but does not erase. This is a claim about the CURRENT driver's two-sidedness, not a claim that the spread is cointegrated, and it should be expected to face the full DSR/CPCV gate on that basis. NOT a performance claim: nothing here asserts how small caps, large caps, or any mean-reversion strategy has recently performed.

Citation: FOMC July 2026 decision and dissents: CNBC, 'Fed rate decision July 2026: Divided Fed holds interest rates steady' (https://www.cnbc.com/2026/07/29/fed-rate-decision-july-2026.html); Bloomberg, 'Fed Holds Rates Steady as Three Officials Dissent in Favor of a Hike', 29 Jul 2026 (https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2026-07-29/fed-holds-rates-steady-three-officials-dissent-favoring-a-hike); Forbes, 'Three Dissents And A Hold: What The Fed's July Meeting Actually Told Us', 11 Aug 2026 (https://www.forbes.com/sites/jasonkirsch/2026/08/11/three-dissents-and-a-hold-what-the-feds-july-meeting-actually-told-us/) -- 9-3 hold at 3.50-3.75%, three dissents preferring a 25bp hike, most divided since 2016. Floating-rate debt asymmetry and the 2026 maturity wall: BingX Learn, 'Russell 2000 Forecast 2026: Small-Cap Breakout or Floating-Rate Debt Trap?' (https://bingx.com/en/learn/article/russell-2000-rty-outlook-and-price-prediction-is-russell-2000-a-good-investment) -- ~32% of Russell 2000 debt floating vs ~6% for the S&P 500, ~$368bn of 2026 maturities repricing from 1-2% to ~6.5%; corroborated independently by WisdomTree, 'The Small-Cap Rotation Is Focused on Fixed vs. Floating Debt Exposure' (https://www.wisdomtree.com/us/insights/blog/the-small-cap-rotation-is-focused-on-fixed-vs-floating-debt-exposure) -- ~40% of S&P 600 vs ~10% of S&P 500 constituents carrying floating-rate debt. Front-end levels: SOFR 3.64% and funds target 3.50-3.75% per FRED SOFR (https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/SOFR) and primerates.com Fed funds/SOFR dashboard, 15 Aug 2026 (https://primerates.com/primerate/fed-prime-rate/); July 2026 nonfarm payrolls -23,000 with unemployment 4.1%, as reported in the RiverFront small-cap note (https://www.riverfrontig.com/insights/us-small-cap-lower-rates-to-the-rescue/), which is also the source for the small-cap -23.4%-below-trend / large-cap +37%-above-trend level figures cited as a caveat in the rationale. Direct page fetches to several of these hosts were blocked by this environment's egress proxy, so figures are as reported in search result summaries rather than read off the source pages. Mechanism background: standard duration-of-liabilities argument for rate-sensitivity differences across market-cap tiers.


### `rp_single_asset_trend_LQD_231` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-15, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=LQD, lookback=231. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: LQD is normally a hybrid instrument -- a credit-spread carry component plus a duration component -- and the two historically partly OFFSET, since spreads tend to tighten in the same risk-on conditions that push Treasury yields up. That offset is what makes IG total returns choppy rather than directional, and choppy is the regime a trend signal loses in. Right now the offset is structurally exhausted at one end: US IG option-adjusted spreads are roughly +70-74bp against a long-run average near 132bp, the tightest in about 20 years, so the spread leg has very little room left to tighten and absorb a yield move, while it has the entire historical range available to widen. With the cushion gone, LQD's price becomes a near-pure long-duration expression (ICE BofA US Corporate index effective duration ~6.8y, weighted-average maturity 10.6y). WHY THE DURATION DRIVER IS A DRIFT AND NOT AN OSCILLATION RIGHT NOW: the long end is currently being set by a supply-and-buyer-composition process, not by the policy rate. Barclays puts private (price-sensitive) investors at ~73% of the Treasury market against ~50% a decade ago, so the marginal absorber of net issuance now demands term premium rather than holding for policy or reserve reasons; the 10y term premium has rebounded from 1.20% (11 Aug 2025) to 1.33% (10 Aug 2026) and the 30y has held above 5% for 41 consecutive sessions, the longest such streak since 2007, with the 10y at 4.70% and 30y at 5.26%. The corporate side pushes the same way: gross IG supply was $721bn in 1Q26 (+12% y/y) against full-year estimates of ~$2.25tn gross / ~$1.0tn net, which would exceed the $2.1tn gross record set in 2020. Buyer-composition shift and multi-year deficit/issuance paths are slow-moving stock variables -- they grind in one direction over quarters rather than repricing back and forth on each data release, which is the drift-type driver a trend signal is built for and the opposite of the policy-anchored front end. The asymmetry also matters for SIGN: at a ~70bp cushion a duration shock and a spread shock now add rather than cancel, so LQD's downside is the fatter side, and a signal that can go flat or short expresses that where an always-on long cannot. PARAMETERS: ticker=LQD -- no prior rp_single_asset_trend proposal has used it (the ledger has GLD, SLV, VNQ, UUP, EEM, USO, TLT, DBC, EFA, IWM, HYG), and it is the UNIVERSE ticker most directly exposed to the specific joint spread-plus-term-premium mechanism above; TLT/IEF are pure Treasury duration with no spread-cushion argument and HYG's spread leg is far too large for the 'cushion exhausted' claim to hold. lookback=231 (~11 months) spans four consecutive quarterly Treasury refunding announcements plus a full corporate issuance calendar, so the signal is measured over the horizon on which the supply/term-premium stock variable actually moves, rather than over a single quarter of it; it is inside the pre-registered [20, 252] range and unused by any prior proposal. freq=W rather than M because the specific risk here is a joint duration-and-spread shock into a thin cushion -- monthly rebalancing leaves the vol-targeted position size up to a month stale exactly when the return distribution's fat side realizes -- and W is under-represented in this ledger relative to M (25 vs 38). HONEST PRIOR: this lab has killed every trend strategy it has ever tested, including tsmom_12m, green_line_200d, the whole hourly family, and eleven prior rp_single_asset_trend specs. This proposal is a claim about what is currently DRIVING one specific instrument's price -- a slow structural supply process rather than an oscillating policy rate -- not a claim that trend following works, and it should face the full DSR/CPCV gate on exactly that basis. NOT a performance claim: nothing here asserts how LQD, credit, duration, or any trend strategy has recently performed, only what the current level of spreads and the current composition of Treasury demand imply structurally.

Citation: IG spread levels: Morningstar 2026 outlook and Q2 2026 index data as reported via Charles Schwab, '2026 Corporate Credit Outlook' (https://www.schwab.com/learn/story/corporate-bond-outlook) and Breckinridge Capital Advisors, 'Q3 2026 Corporate Bond Market Outlook' (https://www.breckinridge.com/insights/q3-2026-corporate-bond-market-outlook) -- US IG OAS ~+70-74bp vs a ~132bp long-run average, tightest in ~20 years, described as fully valued; underlying series is FRED BAMLC0A0CM (https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/BAMLC0A0CM). IG index duration/maturity and 2026 issuance: Breckinridge Q2/Q3 2026 outlooks and 'The Changing Seasons in Investment-Grade Corporate Bond Supply-Demand Technicals', US Bancorp Asset Management (https://www.usbancorpassetmanagement.com/index/our-insights/quick-insights-blog/TheChangingSeasonsinInvestment-GradeCorporateBondSupply-DemandTechnicals.html) -- ICE BofA US Corporate index effective duration ~6.8y / WAM 10.6y; gross IG supply $721bn in 1Q26 (+12% y/y); full-year estimates ~$2.25tn gross / ~$1.0tn net vs the $2.1tn 2020 gross record. Treasury buyer composition and term premium: Barclays research as reported by Bloomberg, 'Barclays Sees Picky Bond Buyers Keeping Yields at Multiyear Peak', 11 Aug 2026 (https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2026-08-11/barclays-sees-picky-bond-buyers-keeping-yields-at-multiyear-peak) and BigGo Finance, 'Structural Shift in Treasury Buyers: Barclays Warns Long-End Yields May Stay Elevated' (https://finance.biggo.com/news/8cc70ac3-8f6f-4571-9187-732932773b9f) -- private investors ~73% of the Treasury market vs ~50% a decade ago; term premium potentially returning toward pre-GFC levels. Term premium and long-end levels: Marketplace, 'Why term premiums on U.S. Treasurys have been rising', 4 Aug 2026 (https://www.marketplace.org/story/2026/08/04/why-term-premiums-on-us-treasurys-have-been-rising) and StreetStats term-premium/yield dashboards (https://streetstats.finance/rates/term-premiums, https://streetstats.finance/rates/treasuries) -- 10y term premium 1.20% (11 Aug 2025) -> 1.33% (10 Aug 2026), 30y above 5% for 41 consecutive sessions (longest since 2007), 10y 4.70%, 30y 5.26%. A direct page fetch to fred.stlouisfed.org was blocked by this environment's egress proxy, so figures are as reported in search-result summaries of the sources above rather than read off the primary series. Mechanism background: standard decomposition of corporate bond return into Treasury-duration and credit-spread components, and the convexity of a spread already near its historical floor.


### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_HYG_GLD_63_252` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-16, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=HYG, ticker_b=GLD, lookback_a=63, lookback_b=252. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: HYG and GLD are driven by structurally distinct flows, which is the specific reason the two trend legs should offset rather than duplicate each other. HYG's return is compensation for the corporate default cycle, and that cushion is unusually thin: US high-yield OAS sits at ~271-281bp as of mid-August 2026, the richest decile of its history against a long-run median near 450bp, with IG at 81bp and bond vol dormant (MOVE 74.7). GLD's marginal bid is official-sector reserve diversification -- 244 tonnes of net central-bank buying in Q1 2026, with the USD's share of global reserves down from ~71% (1999) to ~56-57% -- a flow that is price- and credit-cycle-insensitive by construction. Pairing a fast 63d (credit-cycle-speed) trend leg on HYG with a slow 252d (structural-flow-speed) leg on GLD bets that the credit leg's drawdown states are not the gold leg's, since the sovereign reserve-allocation decision does not run off the US corporate default cycle.

Citation: Offsetting mechanism: corporate default-cycle risk premium (HYG) vs sovereign reserve-diversification demand (GLD) -- two independent drivers, not merely two uncorrelated series. Sources: ICE BofA US High Yield Index OAS, FRED series BAMLH0A0HYM2 (~271bp, 2026-08-12); ICE BofA US Corporate (IG) OAS ~81bp, same date; World Gold Council, Gold Market Commentary 2026 / central-bank net purchases 244t Q1 2026 (gold.org); IMF COFER, USD share of allocated FX reserves ~56-57%.


### `rp_static_spread_carry_IEF_DBC_a` (primitive `static_spread_carry`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-16, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=IEF, ticker_b=DBC, long_leg=a. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: MECHANISM: a two-sided ROLL/CARRY claim, not a directional or mean-reversion view -- which is what static_spread_carry is the pre-registered primitive for. Both legs are structural statements about the shape of a futures/yield curve right now, not claims about anything's recent performance. LONG LEG (IEF, 7-10y Treasuries): the belly of the Treasury curve has flipped from negative to POSITIVE roll-down carry. As of 2026-08-14 the 5y sits at 4.37% and the 10y at 4.70% -- roughly 33bp of positive slope across the exact maturity band IEF holds -- so a note held in IEF now ages DOWN a rising curve and picks up price as it rolls, the opposite of the 2022-2025 regime when the same segment sat under an inverted curve and rolled UP into higher yields. The 2s10s spread is positive (having exited its -108bp trough) though still narrow, with the front end inverted and the belly flat -- a late-cycle shape in which the 5-10y band is the specific part of the curve that earns roll, while the front end does not. SHORT LEG (DBC, broad front-loaded commodity futures): commodity futures sit in contango most of the time, and front-month concentration is the most expensive place on the curve to hold, with negative roll yield the single biggest long-run drag on futures-based commodity returns -- which is precisely why the newer broad-commodity products (COMT, CMDY, constant-maturity indices) exist to spread exposure along the curve, and DBC does not. That generic drag is unusually binding right now because DBC's largest sleeve is energy: the IEA's July 2026 Oil Market Report projects a surplus of just over 3.7 mb/d for 2026, a record annual average glut driven by non-OPEC+ supply growth (US, Brazil, Canada, Guyana, Argentina) against below-trend demand, and the WTI curve has accordingly held a contango structure for much of 2026. Oversupply IS contango, mechanically -- storage has to be paid for -- so the short leg is being paid to sit in the part of the curve that is structurally leaking roll. WHY THIS SHAPE: the two carry components are independent in origin (one is the term structure of interest rates, the other the term structure of physical commodity storage), so this is a spread between two separately-argued risk premia rather than a single bet dressed as two. Freq M, matching the 14 of 15 prior static_spread_carry proposals: the position is always-on and never changes sign, so monthly rebalancing pays the least turnover cost for the same exposure. HONEST CAVEATS: (1) DBC's index does apply an optimum-yield roll rule that mutes, but does not remove, front-month contango drag -- the leg is a weaker version of the pure claim USO would express; (2) IEF's roll-down carry is small in absolute terms (tens of bp) next to its duration risk, so realized returns will be dominated by rate level moves, not by the carry this rationale argues for -- the vol-targeted sizing partly but not fully addresses this; (3) neither leg's carry is observable in engine.load_prices()'s spot ETF closes, so the gate tests whether the claimed structure shows up in total returns, not the carry itself. Novel spec: IEF/DBC has never been proposed under any primitive.

Citation: IEA Oil Market Report, July 2026 (https://www.iea.org/reports/oil-market-report-july-2026) -- 2026 surplus of just over 3.7 mb/d, record annual average glut; WTI curve in contango for much of 2026. Invesco DB Commodity Index Tracking Fund (DBC) product page (https://www.invesco.com/us/en/solutions/invesco-etfs/commodity-investing.html) and Fidelity, 'Commodity ETFs: Contango/Backwardation' (https://www.fidelity.com/learning-center/investment-products/etf/commodity-etfs-contango-backwardation) -- front-month concentration is the most expensive curve position; negative roll yield is the largest long-run drag on futures-based commodity returns. VanEck, 'Commodity Strategies Diverge as Roll Yield Takes Over' (https://www.vaneck.com/us/en/blogs/natural-resources/commodity-strategies-diverge-as-roll-yield-takes-over/) and iShares COMT/CMDY roll-selection product pages -- confirming roll selection, not spot exposure, is the differentiating structural variable. US Treasury curve levels as of 2026-08-14 (5y 4.37%, 10y 4.70%; 2s10s positive but narrow, front end inverted, belly flat) via StreetStats (https://streetstats.finance/rates/treasuries) and FRED T10Y2Y (https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/T10Y2Y).


### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_EFA_UUP_252_63` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-19, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=EFA, ticker_b=UUP, lookback_a=252, lookback_b=63. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: MECHANISM. asset_class_trend_hedge is the pre-registered primitive for two independent trailing-return-sign legs, from two DIFFERENT asset classes, held as one candidate, and it requires the citation to name the SPECIFIC mechanism expected to make the two legs offset -- not merely that they have been uncorrelated. Here the offsetting mechanism is an accounting identity, not a correlation observation: EFA is an UNHEDGED foreign-equity ETF, so its USD total return decomposes exactly into (local-currency equity return) + (FX translation of the underlying currencies into USD). Roughly 70% of EAFE exposure is denominated in yen, euro, sterling or Swiss franc, and UUP tracks the US Dollar Index basket, whose weights are dominated by those same currencies (EUR ~57.6%, JPY ~13.6%, GBP ~11.9%, with CAD/SEK/CHF the remainder). The FX term inside EFA's USD return and the thing UUP prices are therefore the SAME underlying variable with opposite sign -- a shared factor by construction of the two products, which is why a trend leg on UUP is a hedge of a component EFA actually contains rather than a second bet that happens to zig when the first zags. ASSET-CLASS GUARD: EFA is equity, UUP is currency in tradefabe.pipeline.ASSET_CLASS -- different buckets, derived from the tickers, not asserted here. HOW LARGE THAT FX COMPONENT IS RIGHT NOW, structurally: it is currently the dominant term rather than a rounding error. In 2025 the dollar's move accounted for about 75% of the gap between MSCI EAFE and the S&P 500 and about 51% of the gap for MSCI ACWI ex-USA (a DECOMPOSITION of an index's return into its local and FX components -- stated here as evidence that the FX term is large, not as a claim that any strategy is working). Over the prior decade the identity ran the other way: MSCI EAFE Currency Hedged compounded ~11.5%/yr against ~8.4%/yr unhedged, i.e. the FX term subtracted roughly 3 points a year for ten years before mostly reversing in 2025. A component that can swing a multi-percent annual wedge in either direction is exactly the kind of exposure worth carrying an explicit, separately-timed leg against. WHY NOW, structurally (state variables, not performance): DXY sits near 99.4-99.5 in mid-August 2026, its lowest since June 2026, having fallen for three consecutive sessions on softer US inflation, weaker retail sales and consumer sentiment; it began August near 99.9, and the six-month forecast range from major banks is roughly 96-102, i.e. genuinely two-sided. The reason it is two-sided rather than one-way is a policy split that is a matter of record: the FOMC held at 3.50-3.75% on 2026-07-29 for a fifth consecutive meeting, 9-3, with three policymakers dissenting FOR a hike -- so the dollar has one force (a hawkish minority, still-elevated US rates) supporting it and another (cooling US data, documented capital outflows -- roughly $18bn from Treasuries and $22bn from US equities in January 2026 -- rotating toward the Eurozone and EM) pulling it down. That is the configuration in which a currency leg carries real, independent variance rather than sitting still: neither a pinned dollar nor a one-way trend, but a contested one. WHY THESE LOOKBACKS, AND WHY THEY ARE DELIBERATELY MISMATCHED. The single largest failure mode of this primitive is that two legs on the SAME clock, driven by one shared factor, collapse into one levered bet on that factor -- which is precisely what DOCTRINE.md v1.14's calibration-window correlation cap (|corr| <= 0.3 between the two legs' own trend signs, 2007-2017 data alone) exists to reject. Mismatching the horizons is the structural answer to that, not a parameter search: lookback_a = 252 (one year) is the horizon on which the capital-rotation story above actually operates -- cross-border allocation shifts are annual-scale decisions, and a year is the standard window over which an equity leg's own trend is measured; lookback_b = 63 (one quarter) is the horizon on which the DOLLAR's information arrives, because the policy split that currently drives it reprices on the FOMC's quarterly SEP/dot-plot cadence (the next is 2026-09-16) rather than annually. So the equity leg reads a year of capital rotation while the currency leg reads a quarter of policy repricing: the same underlying factor, sampled on two different clocks, which is what makes the pair a hedge rather than a doubling. Both values are inside the pre-registered (20, 252) range for each leg. WHY freq = M. Both legs are sign(trailing return) signals that flip a handful of times a year at these horizons, so rebalance frequency governs how often vol-target sizing refreshes, not what is held. Monthly samples the faster (63-day) leg about three times per lookback -- enough to track it -- while paying the least turnover of the three available frequencies; weekly would pay ~4x the rebalance cost for sizing refreshes a quarterly signal never asks for. It also matches the freq the three prior UUP-hedged proposals here used. HONEST DISCLOSURES, stated before any result. (1) The EFA leg at exactly this lookback has ALREADY been tested here standalone and is DEAD: rp_single_asset_trend_EFA_252, OOS Sharpe -0.146. The UUP leg's nearest standalone relative (rp_single_asset_trend_UUP_63) never reached a verdict. So this candidate's entire claim rests on the COMPOSITION, and DOCTRINE v1.14 names that exact situation as its central worry -- two zero-edge signals can appear to earn their place through variance reduction alone. If this clears the gates, that is a reason for suspicion and a close look at gate 2's diversifier clause, not enthusiasm; the two mechanical guards (asset-class difference, calibration correlation cap) are the only things standing between this shape and that artifact, and neither can verify that the offset is economically meaningful rather than statistically convenient. (2) The identity argued above is exact for EFA's FX component but only approximate against UUP: the DXY basket is ~58% euro while EAFE is more evenly spread across yen, euro, sterling and franc, and DXY carries no exposure to several EAFE currencies at all, so the hedge is a partial one by construction. (3) The 'why now' configuration -- DXY near 99.5 off a multi-year decline, a 9-3 Fed hold with hawkish dissents, documented outflows from US assets -- describes mid-2026 and essentially none of the 2018-2024 portion of the OOS window, so whatever verdict comes back is mostly evidence about the shape, not about the current setup. (4) This lab's honest prior on any predictive candidate is DEAD; every one tested so far has been. NOTHING above is a claim that any strategy, live or backtested, has performed well recently: every input is an index level, a rate decision, a flow figure, a currency weight, or a return decomposition into mechanical components. Novel spec: EFA/UUP has never been proposed here under any primitive, and no prior asset_class_trend_hedge proposal uses EFA on either leg.

### `rp_pair_zscore_VNQ_SPY_63_2p0_4p0` (primitive `pair_zscore`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-20, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=VNQ, ticker_b=SPY, z_window=63, z_entry=2.0, z_stop=4.0. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM. pair_zscore is mean-reversion of the z-scored log-spread log(VNQ) - log(SPY): long VNQ / short SPY when the spread is unusually low, the reverse when it is unusually high, flat near the mean. The common-factor basis for treating that spread as a residual is an index-construction fact, not a correlation observation: listed REITs are equity claims on US real cash flows and were literally components of the S&P 500 until GICS split real estate out into its own sector in September 2016. The two legs are priced by a shared domestic-cash-flow factor and a shared discount rate by construction; the spread is what is left after that. WHY NOW, structurally. The condition that has broken this pair since 2022 is that VNQ stopped trading as equity and started trading as a levered duration proxy -- a second factor SPY does not carry, which pushes the residual persistently in one direction instead of back toward its mean. That second factor is measurably receding: the 'Rates Up, REITs Down' regime has weakened, with REIT-rate correlations falling sharply as fundamentals, capital allocation and valuation catalysts increasingly drive performance. The cleanest evidence is a natural experiment already in this year's data: the US 10-year yield rose from 3.9% in February 2026 to as high as 4.7% in May 2026 -- an 80bp move that the 2022-2025 relationship would have transmitted nearly one-for-one into REIT underperformance -- without REITs behaving as a pure rates trade; the 10-year sits at 4.645% in mid-August 2026. With the duration factor's loading falling, VNQ's residual against SPY is again mostly the shared equity-cash-flow factor, which is the regime in which a spread z-score is a mean-reversion signal rather than a trend-fighting one. MEASURABLE DISLOCATION ANCHOR (a level, not a performance claim). Bank of America marks REITs at 92% of net asset value against a 97% historical average, alongside estimated funds-from-operations growth of 7.3% for 2026 and 8.3% for 2027 -- a listed-versus-appraised gap roughly 5 points wider than normal while the underlying cash flows are growing. That is a state variable describing where the spread currently sits, and it sits on the side (VNQ cheap versus its own assets) that this primitive's long leg would take. WHY z_window = 63. Deliberately near the SHORT end of the pre-registered (20, 120) range, chosen against this primitive's known weakness rather than around it. pair_zscore has no cointegration or ADF filter of its own -- its own description in PRIMITIVES says so -- so its worst failure mode is a spread that trends: a long window keeps calling a multi-year repricing 'extreme' and holds the losing leg the whole way down. A 63-day (one-quarter) window re-anchors 'normal' every quarter, matching REITs' own quarterly reporting and appraisal cadence, so the signal fires on a genuine quarter-scale dislocation instead of fighting a multi-year regime shift. z_entry 2.0 with z_stop 4.0 is the plain 2-sigma entry and 4-sigma abort used across this vocabulary; the stop is what converts 'the relationship broke' from an unbounded loss into a flat position, which matters more than usual for a pair whose common factor is exactly what this proposal argues is being restored. WHY freq = W. The spread's information arrives on a weekly-to-quarterly cadence -- rate moves, earnings, transaction comps -- not intraday. Weekly rebalancing samples a 63-day z-score about thirteen times per window, enough to track the state machine's entries and exits, while paying roughly a fifth of daily rebalancing's turnover cost on two liquid but not costless ETFs. HONEST DISCLOSURES, stated before any result. (1) The competing reading of VNQ has already been tested here and is DEAD: rp_pair_zscore_VNQ_IEF_120_2p0_4p0 (VNQ as duration, OOS Sharpe -0.096) and rp_single_asset_trend_VNQ_126 (OOS Sharpe -0.293). Disclosed as context, NOT as the reason for this proposal -- the motivation above rests on the REIT-rate correlation regime and the NAV-discount level, which stand on their own, and this is a different hypothesis about which factor prices VNQ, not a re-run of the one that died. (2) Family N's hand-picked pairs_cointegration study is DEAD (OOS Sharpe 0.002) across six economically-linked pairs, and every prior rp_ pair_zscore verdict in this graveyard is DEAD as well. This lab's honest prior on this shape is DEAD. (3) The 'why now' describes 2026 and essentially none of 2018-2024, so whatever the OOS gate returns is evidence about the SHAPE across the whole window, not about the current setup. (4) NOTHING above is a claim that any strategy, live or backtested, has performed well: every input is a yield level, an index-membership fact, a price-to-NAV ratio, a cash-flow growth estimate, or a description of a correlation regime. Novel spec: VNQ/SPY has never been proposed here under any primitive, and the factory's five generated families (trend, short-term reversal, calendar, low-vol cross-section, breakout) contain no pairwise-spread template at all, so this mechanism is among the least-covered in the ledger.

Citation: REIT-rate correlation regime and the Feb-May 2026 10-year natural experiment (10y 3.9% in February to as high as 4.7% in May 2026 without REITs trading as a pure rates trade; 'Rates Up, REITs Down' weakening as REIT-rate correlations fall sharply): Janus Henderson, 'REIT halftime report: Off to a strong start to 2026' (https://www.janushenderson.com/en-us/investor/article/reit-halftime-report-off-to-a-strong-start-to-2026/). 10-year Treasury at 4.645% in mid-August 2026: ts2.tech, 'U.S. real estate shares climb as 10-year yield drops under 4.65%' (https://ts2.tech/en/u-s-reit-stocks-lead-as-10-year-yield-slides-below-4-65/). Bank of America valuation of REITs at 92% of NAV versus a 97% historical average, with estimated FFO growth of 7.3% (2026) and 8.3% (2027): Seeking Alpha, 'REITs: Cheap, Unloved, And Finally Showing Life' (https://seekingalpha.com/article/4915931-reits-cheap-unloved-finally-showing-life). Structural repricing of commercial real estate to a permanently higher cost of capital, and the persistent public-private cap-rate gap (112bp in Q3 2025) that the listed discount reflects: Nareit, 'Commercial Real Estate and REITs: Dual Divergences Set Stage for REIT Rebound in 2026' (https://www.reit.com/news/blog/market-commentary/dual-divergences-reit-growth-outlook-2026) and Commercial Property Executive, 'Public vs. Private: Where Will Cap Rates Clear in 2026?' (https://www.commercialsearch.com/news/public-vs-private-where-will-cap-rates-clear-in-2026/). Real estate's separation from the S&P 500's financials sector into its own GICS sector in September 2016 -- the index-construction basis for the shared-factor argument -- is the published S&P Dow Jones Indices / MSCI GICS structure change. Background on REITs' documented interest-rate sensitivity being regime-dependent rather than constant: Shulman, 'Recent Evidence on the Interest Rate Sensitivity of REITs', UCLA Ziman Center Economic Letter (https://www.anderson.ucla.edu/documents/areas/ctr/ziman/UCLA_Economic_Letter_Shulman_01-16-15x.pdf). Prior verdicts disclosed in the rationale (rp_pair_zscore_VNQ_IEF_120_2p0_4p0, rp_single_asset_trend_VNQ_126, pairs_cointegration) are this repo's own graveyard.csv, not an external source.
