### `rp_cross_sectional_rank_momentum_84_4` (primitive `cross_sectional_rank`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-20, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: metric=momentum, lookback=84, k=4. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: Cross-asset correlation regime, not a performance claim: correlations across the major asset classes are reported to be among the lowest in recent memory in 2026, and the commodity complex has visibly decoupled internally (precious metals moving on record central-bank accumulation -- 288.9t bought in Q2 2026, a quarterly record -- while the energy leg faces an OPEC-projected Q3 supply surplus and an IEA record-oversupply 2026 forecast, leaving WTI's belly in contango). The equity-side dispersion picture is the same shape from the options market: 3-month S&P implied correlation ~11 against a ~41 long-run mean. A long-top-K / short-bottom-K rank over this cross-asset ETF basket is structurally paid only by the top-minus-bottom spread; a high-correlation regime collapses that spread toward zero and leaves nothing but turnover cost, while a dispersed cross-section is the condition under which the mechanism can pay at all. Momentum rather than reversal at this horizon because the drivers doing the dispersing -- multi-year central-bank gold demand, a fifth consecutive silver supply deficit, an OPEC+ barrel-return schedule, a re-steepened 2s10s at +35bp -- are slow-moving supply/policy facts that persist over quarters rather than mean-reverting over them; the short horizons where reversal is the defensible read are already covered by earlier proposals (20/21/42d). lookback=84 (~4 months) spans the current divergence without reaching back into the prior regime; k=4 of a 15-ticker universe holds both dispersed tails while staying diversified. This proposes a test of whether that dispersion is harvestable out-of-sample, not a claim that it is -- cross-sectional momentum is already DEAD in this lab's graveyard and this variant carries no lower bar.

Citation: Asness, Moskowitz & Pedersen, 'Value and Momentum Everywhere', Journal of Finance 68(3), 2013 -- the cross-asset rank-momentum mechanism, and its finding that the payoff is a cross-sectional spread rather than a directional bet. Current structural context, accessed 2026-08-19: cross-asset correlations 'some of the lowest in recent memory' and a narrow, internally-divergent commodity rally (investing.com, '10 Charts Point to Commodities Becoming a Major Macro Theme in 2026'); central banks bought 288.9 tonnes of gold in Q2 2026, a quarterly record up 62% y/y, with a fifth consecutive year of silver structural supply deficit (CME Group, 'Precious Metals Outlook 2026'; J.P. Morgan silver price research); OPEC's reversal to a Q3 'supply surplus' forecast and the IEA's record-oversupply 2026 call, with WTI's curve showing a backwardated front and contango belly (CME Group energy research; commoditycontext.com, 'Oil's Wrinkle In Time'); 2s10s re-steepened to +35bp with HY OAS at 271bp as of 2026-08-13 (FRED BAMLH0A0HYM2 via convextrade.com); 3-month S&P 500 implied correlation 11.1 vs a ~41 long-run mean (Cboe COR3M via thetrading.tools). Figures for the last several are as reported in search-result summaries where this environment's egress proxy blocked a direct page fetch. No source is cited for, and no claim is made about, any strategy's realized performance.


### `rp_single_asset_trend_IEF_147` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-20, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=IEF, lookback=147. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: MECHANISM. single_asset_trend takes the sign of IEF's own trailing return over a 147-day lookback, so it only earns anything if moves in intermediate (7-10y) duration are autocorrelated at that horizon rather than range-bound. The structural reason to expect that right now is a change in how the Fed's path gets communicated, not any claim about how trend has been performing. The Fed is on hold at 3.50-3.75% with the new chair standing up five task forces reviewing how decisions are made and communicated, explicitly with LESS emphasis on forward guidance. Forward guidance is precisely the device that makes the expectations component of intermediate yields jump to a new level in one pre-announced step and then sit still; remove it and the path has to be discovered incrementally, data release by data release, which turns one guided repricing into a sequence of small same-signed adjustments. That is an autocorrelation argument about the belly's dominant driver. WHY IEF, NOT TLT. The 7-10y sector is where the expectations component dominates and where the term-premium and convexity volatility that whipsaws the long end is smallest -- 2Y 4.15%, 7Y ~4.50%, 10Y 4.63% (13 Aug 2026), with the 10y term premium at +0.87% (Jul 2026) after the 2011-2022 negative regime, so the level is now being set by slow structural duration-demand flows (bank capital treatment of duration, changed foreign holder composition, coupon-vs-bill issuance skew) rather than by a pinned front end. WHY 147d/M. 2026's own repricing swing -- from cuts priced in January to hikes being priced as possible now -- has run roughly seven months, so a ~147-trading-day lookback spans one full policy-repricing swing instead of a fraction of it, and monthly rebalancing matches drivers that turn over on refunding-announcement and FOMC timescales rather than paying spread on daily noise. HONEST PRIOR: every trend variant tested in this lab is DEAD, and rp_single_asset_trend_TLT_126 is already pre-registered on the long end; this is a distinct sector with a distinct driver, and it should be judged by the same gates with no discount.

Citation: Fed on hold at 3.50-3.75% and the new chair's five task forces reviewing decision-making and communication with less focus on forward guidance: Morgan Stanley, 'Fed Rate Pause: What It Could Mean for Fixed Income Market' (2026); Morningstar, 'What's Next for the Fed in 2026?'. Levels: 2Y 4.15%; 5Y 4.37% and 7Y 4.50% late Jul 2026 (Schwab, 2026 Taxable Fixed Income Mid-Year Outlook); 10Y 4.63% on 13 Aug 2026 (FRED DGS10). Term premium +0.87% Jul 2026: FRED THREEFYTP10 (Kim-Wright). Duration-demand structure: Apollo Academy, 'Understanding demand for Treasuries' (Jan 2026). Mechanism being tested: Moskowitz, Ooi & Pedersen (2012), 'Time Series Momentum', Journal of Financial Economics 104(2).


### `rp_single_asset_trend_SLV_189` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-20, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=SLV, lookback=189. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: STRUCTURAL, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM -- nothing below says trend-following, precious metals or silver has been working lately; no price level, return figure or strategy performance claim is used as motivation, and this lab's own ledger already records rp_single_asset_trend_SLV_63 (W) as DEAD at OOS Sharpe -0.39 (2026-08-08). THE MECHANISM (why a TREND primitive at all, in a lab where every trend verdict is DEAD): the theory of storage says the inventory state, not the price, decides which time-series behaviour a commodity should exhibit. With ample above-ground stocks a price shock is absorbed by destocking and the price snaps back -- mean-reverting. As stocks approach a stockout boundary that buffer is gone, the local supply curve turns inelastic, and competitive-storage models (Deaton-Laroque 1992) predict positive serial dependence in the price path instead of fast reversion; Gorton-Hayashi-Rouwenhorst (2013) is the empirical statement of inventory-as-state-variable behind commodity risk premia. That is a claim about WHICH process should hold, derived from a measurable physical state, and it is the entire reason this primitive is chosen. WHY SILVER'S INVENTORY STATE IS UNUSUAL RIGHT NOW: the market is in its sixth consecutive annual physical deficit. Estimates of the 2026 gap itself disagree materially -- the Silver Institute's headline 2026 forecast is ~215 Moz while the World Silver Survey 2026 projects 46.3 Moz -- so I lean on the CUMULATIVE figure both agree on: roughly 762 Moz drawn from above-ground stocks since 2021. Visible vaults point the same way: LBMA London holdings ~870 Moz in Feb 2026 against 894.4 Moz at end-2025 and about 20% below the 2021 record, and COMEX registered stocks down more than 75% to ~79.9 Moz. The October 2025 silver lease-rate spike to ~39% annualised (since normalised to ~2-3%) is direct evidence the free float is thin enough to price. WHY THE SUPPLY SIDE CANNOT UNDO IT QUICKLY -- the inelasticity half of the argument: most mined silver is a BYPRODUCT of copper/lead/zinc/gold, so output responds to those metals' economics rather than silver's own price, and new primary capacity takes years; total 2026 supply is forecast DOWN ~2%. Demand-side substitution is real and already running -- solar thrifting cut per-panel silver use ~19%, taking PV demand from 186.6 Moz to ~151 Moz -- and the deficit still widens because supply falls faster. Both adjustment channels are therefore slow, which is exactly the condition under which the price path is a drawn-out repricing rather than an oscillation. PARAMETERS: lookback 189 trading days (~9 months) and freq M are set by the driver's own clock, not by search -- the physical balance is published annually and mine capacity moves on multi-year lead times, so the state variable updates far more slowly than a short-horizon signal could track, and monthly rebalancing keeps turnover matched to it. HAZARD I AM DECLARING EXPLICITLY: proposing a longer lookback on a ticker whose shorter lookback already died can look like parameter-shopping on a result. The horizon here is argued from the fundamental's update frequency and would have been argued the same way before that verdict existed; segregated n_tested plus DSR should price the extra look regardless. If the screen kills this, that is the screen doing its job.

Citation: Deaton & Laroque (1992), 'On the Behaviour of Commodity Prices', Review of Economic Studies 59(1):1-23 -- competitive storage implies serial dependence when stocks are low. Gorton, Hayashi & Rouwenhorst (2013), 'The Fundamentals of Commodity Futures Returns', Review of Finance 17(1):35-105 -- inventory as the state variable. Silver Institute, 'Global Silver Investment to Remain Strong in 2026 Against the Backdrop of a Sixth Consecutive Annual Market Deficit' (2026), https://silverinstitute.org/global-silver-investment-to-remain-strong-in-2026-against-the-backdrop-of-a-sixth-consecutive-annual-market-deficit/ -- figures taken from secondary coverage of that release, as silverinstitute.org was blocked by this run's network egress proxy and could not be read directly; the ~215 Moz 2026 forecast and the sixth-consecutive-deficit framing are also reported at https://www.canadianminingreport.com/blog/silver-may-see-wild-volatility-in-2026-instead-of-steady-gains-warn-experts-amid-sixth-deficit . World Silver Survey 2026 figures (46.3 Moz 2026 deficit projection; ~762 Moz drawn from above-ground stocks since 2021), LBMA vault holdings and the Oct-2025 lease-rate spike as reported at https://www.bullionstar.com/blogs/bullionstar/silver-enters-2026-in-a-state-of-structural-breakdown/ and https://www.fxstreet.com/analysis/londons-silver-is-running-out-of-room-202604231905 . Solar thrifting (-19% per-panel use, PV demand 186.6 -> ~151 Moz): https://goldsilver.com/industry-news/article/silver-supply-deficit-solar-thrifting/


### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_DBC_IEF_189_63` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-21, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=DBC, ticker_b=IEF, lookback_a=189, lookback_b=63. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: Structural inflation-vs-duration hedge sized to whichever regime asserts itself next. Setup: 10Y TIPS real yields have printed 2.37% (Jul 2026), well above the 10-yr average; the 10Y-2Y curve has re-steepened to about +0.51% (2026-08-14, ~58th percentile) and continues to widen; and the Fed under Chair Warsh has held at 3.50-3.75% for a fifth straight meeting with 9 of 18 SEP participants penciling further tightening -- a persistent long-end repricing driven by supply/energy inflation (Middle East tensions, non-OPEC+ oil supply dynamics, sixth consecutive annual physical silver deficit) rather than demand strength. The mechanism connecting the two legs' own trailing-return trend signals: a broad supply-driven inflation shock lifts DBC (~55% energy, ~15% agriculture, ~20% metals) while reprising IEF (7-10Y intermediate Treasuries) lower as the term premium and nominal yields absorb the inflation risk premium -- trend_DBC positive, trend_IEF negative. In the opposing regime (demand collapse or clear disinflation), commodity trends roll over while IEF rallies on flight-to-quality and rate-cut pricing -- trend_DBC negative, trend_IEF positive. Both directions of the inflation cycle drive the two leg trends to OPPOSITE signs, which is exactly the offset structure the primitive is meant to express, and it does not depend on picking the correct direction of the next move -- only on the inflation channel being the dominant driver of both legs, which the current data supports. Independent-clock parameterization: lookback_a=189 (~9 months) matches the multi-quarter clock at which physical commodity balances reprice (OPEC cadence, inventory turnover, harvest cycles); lookback_b=63 (~1 quarter) matches the FOMC/CPI cycle at which intermediate Treasuries reprice. freq=M is set by the slower leg. This is a MECHANISM claim only -- every trend and cross-asset primitive in this lab is DEAD to date; the DSR/CPCV gate and the mechanical calibration-window |corr|<=0.3 guard decide, not this rationale.

Citation: 10Y TIPS real yield 2.37% (Jul 2026): FRED DFII10 https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/DFII10 . 10Y-2Y at +0.51% and widening, ~58th percentile as of 2026-08-14: FRED T10Y2Y https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/T10Y2Y . Broad commodity supply picture (non-OPEC+ supply growth against below-trend demand; contango-driving oversupply): IEA Oil Market Report July 2026 https://www.iea.org/reports/oil-market-report-july-2026 . DBC index composition (energy-heavy broad commodity basket): Invesco DB Commodity Index Tracking Fund product page https://www.invesco.com/us/en/solutions/invesco-etfs/commodity-investing.html . IEF effective duration ~7-8yr: iShares 7-10 Year Treasury Bond ETF product page https://www.ishares.com/us/products/239456/ishares-710-year-treasury-bond-etf . Commodities as a distinct macro factor from nominal duration: Gorton & Rouwenhorst (2006), 'Facts and Fantasies about Commodity Futures', Financial Analysts Journal 62(2):47-68. Inflation risk premium in the long end: Ang, Bekaert & Wei (2008), 'The Term Structure of Real Rates and Expected Inflation', Journal of Finance 63(2):797-849.


### `rp_cross_sectional_rank_momentum_189_3` (primitive `cross_sectional_rank`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-21, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: metric=momentum, lookback=189, k=3. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: STRUCTURAL, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM: this is not proposed because cross-sectional momentum 'has been working' -- five momentum and five reversal cross_sectional_rank rows already went through this pipeline, all DEAD, and that prior stands. The structural claim is about the CURRENT DRIVER REGIME across this universe's asset classes, which cross-sectional momentum needs to be differentiated (not single-factor-correlated) to have a mechanism at all. As of 2026-08-20, five of this universe's asset classes are each being priced off a distinct, identifiable driver rather than one shared macro factor: (1) oil (USO) is in backwardation on Strait-of-Hormuz supply risk, WTI mid-$80s / Brent ~$91-92, a physical-scarcity story; (2) gold (GLD) sits near its 2026 highs on monetary/safe-haven demand, and 2026 term-structure research (Journal of Futures Markets) finds gold's curve slope responds insignificantly-to-negatively to crude's own slope shocks, i.e. mechanically decoupled from oil's driver, most pronounced in stress; (3) US high-yield credit (HYG) OAS sits near 271bp, close to all-time tights, a benign-default/idiosyncratic-risk-appetite story orthogonal to either commodity; (4) US equities (SPY/QQQ/IWM) are in a regime the CBOE itself is flagging directly -- COR1M (S&P 500 implied correlation) fell to multi-year lows in 2026 as VIXEQ (dispersion) rose, i.e. macro-driven co-movement is structurally breaking down in favor of idiosyncratic, asset-specific catalysts, the market's own real-time correlation read, not a return claim; (5) the Fed (rates/duration complex) is in a genuinely uncertain regime under a new chair (Kevin Warsh since May 2026) who held at 3.50-3.75% on July 29 while DXY broke below 100 on a weak jobs print, with the ECB/BoC diverging toward hikes -- policy divergence, not a directional trend call either way. The MECHANISM: cross-sectional momentum's edge requires each asset's own trailing return to reflect its own persistent driver rather than a single global risk factor that would erase cross-sectional differentiation; the condition above -- multiple identifiably distinct, currently-active drivers plus the market's own correlation gauge (COR1M) confirming low realized co-movement -- is the structural precondition for that mechanism to have anything to rank, independent of whether any of it has recently paid off. 189-day lookback (~9 months) and monthly rebalance match this primitive's existing longer-horizon rows (126d/252d -> M) rather than its short-horizon (63-105d -> W) ones, since none of the five drivers above are event-of-the-week phenomena.

Citation: CBOE Index Insights (May 2026), COR1M/VIXEQ; Investing.com 'Oil Trading in August 2026: Backwardation, Crack Spreads and Hormuz Risk' (2026-08); Journal of Futures Markets, Luisi (2026), 'On the Comovement of Contango and Backwardation Across Futures Commodity Markets'; ICE BofA US High Yield OAS (FRED BAMLH0A0HYM2), Aug 2026; FOMC July 29 2026 statement; DXY level per Aug 2026 FX coverage (e.g. cryptonomist.ch 2026-08-19, tradingeconomics.com).

Not a claim about QQQ's recent price performance -- QQQ has no single_asset_trend proposal in this pipeline to date (its only prior appearance is a since-DEAD asset_class_trend_hedge combo vs USO), and single_asset_trend is this pipeline's least-proposed primitive, so this also adds genuine breadth on both axes.

### `rp_cross_sectional_rank_low_vol_84_3` (primitive `cross_sectional_rank`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-21, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: metric=low_vol, lookback=84, k=3. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: STRUCTURAL, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM -- three low_vol cross_sectional_rank rows already went through this pipeline (low_vol_63_3, low_vol_126_5, low_vol_252_2, the last DEAD in graveyard.csv) and this is not proposed because low-vol investing 'has been working'. The structural claim is specific to what makes a TRAILING-REALIZED-VOLATILITY ranking meaningful right now, distinct from the momentum-based dispersion argument this pipeline used on 2026-08-20 for cross_sectional_rank_momentum_189_3: that one argued return DRIVERS are currently idiosyncratic; this one argues VOLATILITY ITSELF is currently idiosyncratic, which is what low_vol's own rolling-std-of-returns signal actually measures. As of 2026-08-14, average 60-day pairwise correlation among S&P 500 constituents sits in the bottom 1st percentile of observations since 2005 -- cross-sector pair correlation in the 1st percentile, same-sector in the 3rd -- while cross-sector contribution to total index dispersion fell further below its long-term average in July per S&P Dow Jones Indices' own dispersion/correlation dashboard. Simultaneously, CBOE's own vol-of-single-stocks gauge VIXEQ closed at 49.98 on 2026-07-09, the 98th percentile of its history since 2014 inception, while the index-level VIX closed the same day at 15.84 -- a near-record split between calm aggregate volatility and elevated, unusually dispersed single-name volatility (Penn Mutual and JPMorgan Asset Management both flag the same split independently in their August 2026 commentary). THE MECHANISM: low_vol ranks purely on each name's own trailing realized volatility, which only differentiates names if that volatility is genuinely idiosyncratic rather than each name simply inheriting a shared, correlated volatility regime (in which case every name's trailing std would move together and the cross-sectional sort would carry little information). The current split -- correlation at a two-decade low, single-stock vol dispersion at a multi-year high, while the index itself stays calm -- is precisely the condition under which trailing realized vol is measuring name-specific risk rather than a compressed common factor, which is the structural precondition for this primitive's ranking to have anything real to sort on, independent of whether the resulting long-low-vol/short-high-vol spread has recently paid off. PARAMETERS: lookback=84 (~4 months, one earnings-plus-guidance cycle) is picked because the cited dispersion regime is a mid-2026 development (July/August readings, not a multi-year standing feature) -- a window this short is needed to actually measure the current elevated idiosyncratic-vol regime rather than average it away against a calmer trailing year, and it is distinct from this pipeline's existing 63/126/252 low_vol lookbacks. k=3 matches this primitive's typical basket width. freq=W because the correlation/dispersion readings cited are already computed and published on a rolling near-daily to weekly cadence (the S&P dashboard and CBOE gauges update continuously), so a weekly rebalance can track the regime without paying daily turnover against a signal that itself only shifts materially over days to weeks. The pre-registered DSR/CPCV gate decides ALIVE/DEAD, not this rationale -- and given every low_vol_xsec draw from the factory to date has come back DEAD, this candidate may well be DEAD too; the point of a fresh structural setup is to give the gate a genuinely current window to test, not to predict its outcome.

Citation: S&P Dow Jones Indices, 'Dispersion, Volatility & Correlation Dashboard' and 'INDEX INVESTMENT STRATEGY Dashboard: U.S. Sector', data through 2026-07-31/2026-08-14, https://www.spglobal.com/spdji/en/documents/performance-reports/dashboard-dispersion-volatility-correlation.pdf and https://www.spglobal.com/spdji/en/documents/performance-reports/dashboard-us-sector.pdf (60-day pairwise correlation in the bottom 1st percentile since 2005 as of 2026-08-14; cross-sector correlation 1st percentile, same-sector 3rd percentile; cross-sector dispersion contribution below long-term average in July 2026). CBOE, 'The Impact of Dispersion on Market Expectations and Volatility', https://www.cboe.com/insights/posts/the-impact-of-dispersion-on-market-expectations-and-volatility (VIXEQ 49.98 vs VIX 15.84 on 2026-07-09, VIXEQ at the 98th percentile since its 2014 inception). Penn Mutual Asset Management, 'The S&P 500 Index Is Calm. Its Stocks Aren't.' (2026-08-20), https://www.pennmutualam.com/market-insights-news/blogs/chart-of-the-week/2026-08-20-the-sp-500-index-is-calm-its-stocks-aren-t . J.P. ### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_DBC_UUP_168_63` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-22, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=DBC, ticker_b=UUP, lookback_a=168, lookback_b=63. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: STRUCTURAL, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM -- this is not proposed because commodities or the dollar-short trade have "been working"; DBC and UUP have never been paired in this primitive before (16 prior asset_class_trend_hedge rows cover SPY/GLD, TLT/DBC, SPY/IEF, VNQ/DBC, TLT/GLD, TLT/UUP, VNQ/UUP, SPY/DBC, QQQ/USO, USO/IEF, EEM/UUP, LQD/DBC, HYG/GLD, EFA/UUP, IWM/TLT, DBC/IEF -- none is this pair), and single_asset_trend/curve_carry/cross_sectional_rank each already had a fresh proposal earlier today, so this keeps the vocabulary exploring breadth rather than repeating a shape already used today. THE CURRENT STRUCTURAL CONDITION: the US current account deficit widened to 26.8B in Q1 2026 from 21.1B in Q4 2025, and IIF/Bloomberg reporting (2026-05-06) finds foreign demand for US Treasuries stalling as investors diversify -- Japanese and European buyers shifting toward their own sovereign debt rather than absorbing US issuance at the prior pace. DXY itself sits defensive in a 99.50-100.00 range, near two-month lows. THE MECHANISM, stated as economics rather than an observed co-movement: commentary on this dollar move (BBH, ABN AMRO) is explicit that the standard rate-differential story is incomplete -- the Fed has barely cut, and the Treasury yield premium over Bunds/JGBs remains intact -- so the residual explanation is structural: whether the real return on dollar assets compensates for currency and inflation risk embedded in a fiscal path that structurally resists consolidation, given persistent twin (fiscal + current-account) deficits. That is a slow-moving, multi-quarter repricing of the currency itself, not a rate-cycle trade, and it is exactly the kind of driver single_asset_trend signals on trailing price are built to catch once it is underway. DBC is the natural counterpart because a currency the world is structurally willing to hold less of, against unchanged real physical demand for the energy/metals/ag basket DBC tracks, mechanically shows up as USD-denominated commodity prices trending up (commodities are priced and settled in dollars, so a cheapening unit of account re-rates them nominally higher for unchanged real terms) -- and the reverse holds too: DBC/UUP is symmetric, this is a claim about which regime the trend legs are currently entering, not a directional call locked into one side. The inverse relationship itself is not asserted from nothing: the 2014-2015 episode, cited here only as evidence the mechanical link exists (not as a recent-performance claim), was a strong dollar squeezing WTI -59% and gold -8% in the opposite direction. PARAMETERS: lookback_a (DBC)=168 (~8 months) spans the period since the Q1 2026 current-account release through today, matching the multi-quarter pace of the underlying fiscal/current-account story rather than a fast commodity-specific catalyst. lookback_b (UUP)=63 (~3 months) is shorter because the currency leg also carries a faster-moving cyclical component (near-term Fed-cut repricing) layered on the structural story, so a shorter trailing window is needed to let the trend signal track the currencys own more responsive price action rather than averaging a quarter-old regime shift against a slower window built for the commodity leg. freq=W matches this primitives typical cadence for a multi-month structural thesis without paying daily-rebalance turnover. DECLARED HAZARDS: (1) DBC is a diversified basket including energy, whose price is also driven by its own supply/demand shocks (e.g. OPEC+ spare capacity, independently cited in this pipelines 2026-08-20 gold/oil proposal) unrelated to the dollar -- a large idiosyncratic oil move could dominate the commodity leg and swamp the currency-driven component this thesis is actually about. (2) A current-account/fiscal repricing is a structural thesis precisely because it is slow; if it does not resolve within the OOS window, this trade simply looks like noise around two independently-trending series with no coherent link, which the pre-registered DSR/CPCV gate -- not this rationale -- will decide.

Citation: US current account deficit 26.8B Q1 2026 vs 21.1B Q4 2025: https://www.global-vtrader.com/en/live-updates/us-current-account-deficit-widens-to-226-8bn-reviving-dollar-weakness-and-options-hedging-focus/ and https://cryptobriefing.com/us-current-account-deficit-widens-q1-2026/ . Foreign demand for US Treasuries stalling as investors diversify, IIF (Bloomberg, 2026-05-06): https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2026-05-06/foreign-demand-for-treasury-debt-is-stalling-trade-group-says . DXY defensive in a 99.50-100.00 range near two-month lows, and dollar weakness as a structural (not just rate-differential) story given fiscal path resisting consolidation and reliance on foreign demand to fund persistent current-account deficits: https://www.bbh.com/us/en/insights/blog/mind-on-the-markets/Drivers-for-the-Week-of-August-17-2026.html and https://www.abnamro.com/research/en/our-research/fx-outlook-2026-more-dollar-weakness-ahead . 2014-2015 strong-dollar / commodity-squeeze episode (WTI -59%, gold -8%) as evidence of the mechanical dollar-commodity link: https://convextrade.com/outlook/dollar . ### `rp_single_asset_trend_USO_42` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-22, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=USO, lookback=42. Rebalance freq: D.

Rationale: WTI's forward curve has been in extreme backwardation since Aug 2026, the opposite of the deep contango (~$20/bbl Aug26-vs-early-2030s) an earlier pipeline proposal on this same ticker (rp_single_asset_trend_USO_126, proposed 2026-08-07) built its trend thesis on -- that oversupply-driven regime has since been overturned by a live geopolitical supply shock, not by any performance claim about the strategy itself. Since the Iran-Israel-US war escalated on Feb 28 2026, Strait of Hormuz throughput -- the chokepoint for roughly a fifth of global oil supply -- has repeatedly whipsawed with the conflict's on/off phases: steep backwardation through the war's opening months, a brief flip back toward contango in late June as flows recovered and a US-Iran memorandum (signed 17 Jun 2026) lifted the US naval blockade, then a fresh escalation in early August when Washington reimposed the blockade after renewed attacks. Hormuz transits have since collapsed to roughly 1-5% of the pre-war ~97/day baseline (Lloyd's List Intelligence, 19 Aug 2026), and the WTI curve is back in backwardation -- a +$3.54 prompt spread per CME Group's analysis, severe enough that prices held firm even when the EIA's report for the week ending 14 Aug 2026 showed a surprise 4.4-million-barrel inventory BUILD. That decoupling is the mechanism: the physical-scarcity risk premium from a live, still-unresolved blockade is currently dominating the headline supply/demand print, not the other way around, and a naval blockade is a slow-moving state variable -- escalation/de-escalation phases lasting weeks to months, not a single-day event -- so a trailing-return trend signal is structurally suited to track which regime the market is currently pricing, unlike a static or mean-reverting construction. Lookback 42 (~2 months) is deliberately shorter than the existing USO proposal's 126-day window: it spans back to roughly the early-August blockade reimposition without also averaging in the late-June contango interlude the same war already produced once this year, so the signal reads the CURRENT escalation phase rather than blending two opposite regimes together. USO, not DBC, for the same reason the prior proposal gave: USO's return is dominated by front-month WTI roll mechanics that carry the curve's shape directly, while DBC's broad multi-commodity basket dilutes it. Stated plainly: this reuses the same primitive and ticker as an existing, still-unverdicted pipeline proposal, but the underlying economic driver has fully reversed since that proposal was written (supply-glut contango vs an active war/blockade backwardation), so this is a fresh structural thesis rather than a re-tuning of the old one -- and if the blockade resolves again as it briefly did in late June, this construction's own signal would flip with it rather than requiring a new proposal.

Citation: Lloyd's List Intelligence, 'Strait of Hormuz Brief: 19 August, 2026' (transits down to ~1-5% of pre-war baseline): https://www.lloydslistintelligence.com/resources/blog/strait-of-hormuz-brief-19-august-2026 | CME Group, 'Implications of WTI Oil Futures In Backwardation Amid the Supply Crunch' (2026; +$3.54 prompt-spread backwardation): https://www.cmegroup.com/insights/economic-research/2026/implications-of-wti-oil-futures-in-backwardation-amid-the-supply-crunch.html | Investing.com, 'Oil Trading in August 2026: Backwardation, Crack Spreads and Hormuz Risk Explained' (naval blockade reimposed early Aug 2026 after renewed attacks; 14 Aug 2026 EIA build of 4.4Mb despite firm prices): https://www.investing.com/analysis/oil-trading-in-august-2026-backwardation-crack-spreads-and-hormuz-risk-explained-200686148


### `rp_static_spread_carry_EEM_UUP_a` (primitive `static_spread_carry`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-22, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=EEM, ticker_b=UUP, long_leg=a. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: STRUCTURAL, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM -- no assertion is made about how EEM, UUP, emerging markets or any live/paper strategy has recently performed; static_spread_carry is this primitive's fixed, always-on structural risk-premium bet, so the case must be for a persistent mechanism, not a reverting or trending one. THE CURRENT STRUCTURAL CONDITION: the US Dollar Index has fallen to four-year lows in the mid-90s in 2026, a move multiple sources tie to structural rather than purely cyclical drivers -- capital rotating away from historically concentrated US equity allocations (the same AI-driven mega-cap concentration this pipeline has cited in other proposals today), a widening US current account deficit (26.8B Q1 2026 vs 21.1B Q4 2025) and stalling foreign demand for Treasuries (IIF/Bloomberg, 2026-05-06) reflecting persistent twin fiscal and current-account deficits that resist near-term consolidation. THE MECHANISM linking that to EM equities specifically, not just to the dollar itself: a structurally softer dollar relieves EM sovereign and corporate dollar-denominated debt-servicing costs, improves EM terms of trade for commodity-exporting members of the index, and mechanically lowers the US-dollar hurdle rate foreign capital measures EM local-currency returns against -- a standing transmission channel, not a one-off correlation, and one multiple independent sources (J.P. Morgan, T. Rowe Price, LSEG, Mondrian) currently describe as a reinforcing, multi-pillar structural case for 2026 (weaker-dollar tailwind plus EM's own demographic and domestic-consumption growth advantage over developed markets) rather than a cyclical rebound story. PRIMITIVE CHOICE: this same EEM/UUP pair already has one pending, unverdicted proposal in this ledger (rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_EEM_UUP_147_42), but under a genuinely different mechanism -- two independent trend-following legs betting on which direction each ticker is currently moving -- versus this candidate's claim that the EM-over-dollar tilt is a standing structural premium worth holding regardless of short-term trend, which is what long_leg='a' (long EEM, short UUP) as a fixed position actually tests. static_spread_carry has not yet been proposed at all today (five other proposals today used curve_carry, single_asset_trend x2, cross_sectional_rank and asset_class_trend_hedge), and this EEM/UUP combination has never been tested under this primitive, keeping this proposal additive to the ledger's breadth on both axes. freq=M matches every other static_spread_carry proposal in this ledger -- a structural, always-on premise calls for infrequent rebalancing, not a trend or reversion cadence. DECLARED HAZARD: EEM is a diversified basket spanning economies with heterogeneous dollar-sensitivity and idiosyncratic country risk (e.g. local political or credit events) unrelated to the DXY-level thesis this proposal actually rests on, which could dominate the EM leg independent of the dollar-weakness mechanism. The pre-registered DSR/CPCV gate, not this rationale, decides ALIVE/DEAD.

### `rp_pair_zscore_VNQ_LQD_84_2p0_4p0` (primitive `pair_zscore`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-22, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=VNQ, ticker_b=LQD, z_window=84, z_entry=2.0, z_stop=4.0. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: STRUCTURAL, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM -- pair_zscore is this pipeline's least-used primitive (16 prior rows vs 17-19 for the others) and has NOT been proposed yet today (today's six proposals used curve_carry, single_asset_trend x2, cross_sectional_rank, asset_class_trend_hedge and static_spread_carry), so this adds genuine breadth on the under-represented axis rather than defaulting to a heavier-used shape. VNQ/LQD has never been tested under any primitive in this ledger -- the closest existing pair_zscore rows are VNQ/IEF (tested, DEAD) and LQD/TLT and LQD/IEF (both DEAD) -- so this is a fresh pair, not a rerun. MECHANISM: Nareit's own mid-2026 commentary documents a public-private real estate valuation divergence that has now persisted for 17 quarters, the longest such gap since the early 2000s -- as of Q1 2026 the public REIT-implied cap rate (~5.89%) sat 169bp over the 10-year Treasury while the NCREIF ODCE private appraisal cap rate sat only 24bp over it, a roughly 145bp gap Nareit and others frame as a MEAN-REVERSION setup: private appraisals are eventually marked to market, so the gap closes rather than persisting indefinitely. VNQ (the public, mark-to-market side of that gap) is the tradeable leg; the natural traded counterpart is not Treasuries (VNQ/IEF already tested this pure-duration match and came back DEAD) but LQD, since the REIT literature itself frames the 'is real estate cheap or rich' question by comparing REIT implied cap rates against INVESTMENT-GRADE corporate bond yields/spreads, not just risk-free duration -- REIT cash flows are priced as a long-duration, credit-like income stream. IG credit's own spread (ICE BofA US Corporate OAS, ~77-81bp as of late July/mid-August 2026) sits in the bottom decile of post-financial-crisis readings, meaning the corporate-credit side of that comparison is itself unusually rich right now. That combination -- REITs historically cheap on an implied-cap-rate basis, IG credit historically rich on a spread basis -- is the current structural condition motivating a VNQ/LQD log-spread mean-reversion test, distinct from the already-failed pure-Treasury-duration version of this idea (VNQ/IEF). PARAMETERS: z_window=84 (~4 months) spans slightly more than one full NCREIF ODCE quarterly appraisal print, letting the z-score reflect an updated private-side data point rather than averaging across stale appraisal vintages; z_entry=2.0/z_stop=4.0 match this primitive's most common calibration elsewhere in the ledger. freq=W matches this primitive's typical cadence for a multi-quarter structural setup. DECLARED HAZARD: the public-private gap is explicitly described as unusually PERSISTENT (17 quarters, not a typical few-quarter cycle), so there is no guarantee it resolves within any given OOS window -- if it doesn't, this looks like two credit/rate-sensitive tickers drifting apart rather than reverting, which the pre-registered DSR/CPCV gate, not this rationale, decides.

Citation: Nareit, "Commercial Real Estate and REITs: Dual Divergences Set Stage for REIT Rebound in 2026", https://www.reit.com/news/blog/market-commentary/dual-divergences-reit-growth-outlook-2026 (public REIT implied cap rate 169bp over 10Y Treasury vs NCREIF ODCE appraisal cap rate 24bp over 10Y Treasury, Q1 2026; gap persisting 17 quarters, longest since the early 2000s; framed as a valuation gap that closes as private marks catch up to public pricing). Seeking Alpha, "Public Vs. Private Real Estate: Mean Reversion Opportunity?", https://seekingalpha.com/article/4612878-public-versus-private-real-estate-mean-reversion-opportunity . Convex, "IG Credit Spread (OAS): 80 bps (Jul 30, 2026)", https://convextrade.com/metrics/bamlc0a0cm (ICE BofA US Corporate Index OAS ~77-81bp, bottom decile of post-financial-crisis readings).


### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_HYG_UUP_63_126` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-22, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=HYG, ticker_b=UUP, lookback_a=63, lookback_b=126. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: Two independent structural repricings this month point the same direction for a HYG/UUP offsetting-trend pair, via the well-documented risk-off dollar bid ("dollar smile") mechanism rather than any track-record claim about either leg. First, US high-yield credit is priced for perfection: OAS sat at 271bp on 2026-08-12 (Convex), in the richest decile of the last 25 years against a long-run median near 450bp, with intermediate Treasuries near 4.3% -- an acknowledged asymmetric setup ("far more room to widen than to compress") because spread duration near 3.2yr means a move back to a merely-average-tight 380bp erases roughly five months of carry. Second, the rate-cut path priced into HYG/UUP has itself been repriced hawkish: CME FedWatch puts ~64% odds on a hold at the Sept 15-16 FOMC (the fifth straight hold under Chair Warsh, median 2026 dot at 3.8%) with elevated odds of a hike by year-end, pushing the cutting cycle -- and the dollar-carry compression that would come with it -- further out than markets expected a quarter ago. Credit priced for perfection plus a policy path with less room to ease than hoped is the setup under which a risk-off credit air-pocket and a dollar bid tend to arrive together: HYG (bucketed "rates" here) sells off on spread widening while UUP (bucketed "currency") catches a flight-to-safety bid, the offsetting pair this primitive is built to hold. lookback_a=63 (~1 quarter) matches how fast credit spreads actually move once they turn; lookback_b=126 (~6 months) matches the slower policy-repricing clock driving the dollar leg. freq=W avoids paying daily whipsaw cost on what is a quarters-long macro repricing, not a day-trade.

Citation: Jen, S. (2001), "The 'Dollar Smile' Hypothesis," Morgan Stanley -- USD strengthens both in broad risk-off flight-to-safety and in idiosyncratic USD strength, the offsetting mechanism against a credit risk-off leg; HY OAS from Convex (convextrade.com, accessed 2026-08-12 read); Fed hold odds from CME FedWatch as reported 2026-08.


### `rp_single_asset_trend_SPY_105` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-22, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=SPY, lookback=105. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: STRUCTURAL, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM: nothing here asserts that SPY, momentum, or any live strategy has recently performed well -- single_asset_trend has never used SPY (15 prior proposals cover GLD, SLV, VNQ, UUP, EEM, USO, TLT, DBC, EFA, IWM, HYG, LQD, IEF, QQQ -- SPY is the one UNIVERSE ticker this primitive has never touched). THE MECHANISM: SPY's marginal buyer is now structurally price-insensitive on two independent channels. (1) CORPORATE BUYBACKS: Goldman Sachs puts 2026 S&P 500 repurchases at roughly $1.4tn executed against ~$1.55tn authorized, up from $1.1tn in 2025; the count of companies running an active daily repurchase program has grown from roughly 10 two years ago to 50-60 now, i.e. the flow has broadened across issuers rather than concentrating in a few names that a broad-index signal could net out. (2) PASSIVE/RETIREMENT FLOW: index funds and ETFs now hold over half of US equity fund assets (up from ~19% in 2010), and the inelastic-markets literature (Gabaix & Koijen, NBER w28967) estimates $1 of net flow into the aggregate market moves its value by roughly $3-8, because the marginal holders -- index funds, pension funds, 401k target-date allocations -- rebalance to fixed mandates rather than react to price, so demand shocks clear mostly through price rather than quantity. Both channels are stock-flow-driven and quarter-paced -- buyback authorizations are set at quarterly earnings and executed over the following one to two quarters; 401k/pension contributions land biweekly-to-monthly on payroll and plan-rebalancing schedules -- so the resulting price pressure accretes gradually rather than repricing at a single announcement, the same gradual-diffusion condition (Hong & Stein 1999) that makes a trailing-return-sign trend the right primitive rather than a mean-reversion one: nothing here assumes the flow reverses. PARAMETERS: lookback=105 (~5 months) spans roughly one to two buyback-execution cycles and several 401k contribution/rebalancing dates, long enough to sample the structural flow's own cadence without chasing single-week noise; 105 is inside the pre-registered [20,252] range and unused by any prior single_asset_trend proposal (63,84,126x4,147,168,189x2,231,252,42 used so far). freq=M matches every prior single_asset_trend proposal with a lookback at or above 100 days, sampling the flow periodically rather than paying turnover tracking daily noise on a driver that itself only updates on a quarterly/monthly cadence. HONEST PRIOR: this lab has killed every trend strategy tested, including all 15 prior single_asset_trend proposals and the whole hourly/tsmom family; SPY itself is this lab's OWN diversified buy-and-hold survivor, which if anything argues its price is closer to efficient than any other UNIVERSE ticker, not less -- this proposal is offered because the flow mechanism is specific and checkable on its own terms, not because SPY trend is expected to win. NOT a performance claim: nothing here asserts how SPY, buybacks, passive investing, or any trend strategy has recently returned.

Citation: Gabaix, X. & Koijen, R.S.J. (2021), 'In Search of the Origins of Financial Fluctuations: The Inelastic Markets Hypothesis', NBER Working Paper 28967 (nber.org/papers/w28967) -- $1 of net equity flow moves aggregate market value ~$3-8; Hong, H. & Stein, J.C. (1999), 'A Unified Theory of Underreaction, Momentum Trading, and Overreaction in Asset Markets', Journal of Finance -- gradual information/flow diffusion as a trend-persistence mechanism; Goldman Sachs 2026 buyback estimate ~$1.4tn executed / ~$1.55tn authorized (finance.yahoo.com/markets/stocks/articles/goldman-sachs-sees-1-4-143200767.html); active daily repurchase program count 10 -> 50-60 (247wallst.com/investing/2026/06/26/corporate-america-is-quietly-buying-back-its-own-stock-at-a-record-pace-and-its-not-just-the-mag-7/); passive/index share of US equity fund assets >50%, up from ~19% in 2010 (morningstar.com/financial-advisors/passive-investing-is-driving-decline-active-fund-alpha-heres-what-that-means-investors).


### `rp_single_asset_trend_EFA_126` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-22, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=EFA, lookback=126. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: STRUCTURAL, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM -- nothing below asserts that trend-following, international equity, or any live/paper strategy has recently performed well; every input is a budget figure, an earnings estimate, or a fiscal-calendar fact. THE MECHANISM, and why it is DIFFERENT from this ledger's existing EFA proposals: both prior EFA rows here (rp_single_asset_trend_EFA_252, DEAD OOS Sharpe -0.146, and rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_EFA_UUP_252_63, pending) rest on the SAME channel -- EFA's unhedged FX-translation term from dollar-reserve diversification. This proposal is deliberately about the OTHER additive term in EFA's return: the LOCAL-CURRENCY equity return, driven this time by a real fiscal impulse to European corporate earnings, not a currency flow. Germany's 2026 federal budget, adopted mid-2025 and now executing, commits a record EUR126.7bn in investment (vs EUR74.5bn in 2024) as part of a roughly EUR1tn multi-year public-investment package equivalent to ~23% of German GDP, with EUR108.2bn of 2026 spending on defense alone -- exceeding the UK and France combined -- and NATO-wide European defense spending rising toward 2.8% of GDP in 2026 en route to 3.5% by 2029. Sell-side and buy-side estimates now tie a SPECIFIC earnings effect to this: European defense and German infrastructure spending are separately estimated to add roughly 2 and 1 percentage points of incremental EPS growth per year respectively, and STOXX Europe 600 EPS grew an estimated 14% in H1 2026 with a 15% full-year forecast, up from consensus expectations of 11% CAGR through 2029 at the start of the year to 13% now -- a magnitude and direction of earnings revision reporting-season data can actually confirm, unlike a multi-decade FX-reserve drift. WHY A TREND SIGNAL: fiscal execution is inherently a multi-quarter process -- budget authorization, procurement contracting, and disbursement each lag the prior step by months -- so the earnings effect should accrete and get re-confirmed release by release across 2026 rather than repricing on a single print, which is the same gradual-diffusion condition (per Hong & Stein 1999, already used in this ledger's own SPY buyback/passive-flow proposal today for an analogous reason) that makes a trailing-sign trend the appropriate primitive rather than a reversal bet. PARAMETERS: lookback=126 (~6 months) is chosen to span the H1 2026 reporting season (roughly Feb-Aug 2026) during which the STOXX 600 EPS-growth acceleration actually became visible and consensus 2026-2029 estimates were revised up -- a deliberately SHORTER window than the already-used 252-day EFA lookback, which was sized against COFER's quarterly reserve-composition clock, a completely different and slower mechanism; 126 is inside the pre-registered [20,252] range and unused by any prior EFA proposal. freq=M matches this primitive's convention for lookbacks >=100 days elsewhere in this ledger (rebalancing on the same cadence the underlying driver itself updates -- quarterly earnings and budget releases -- without paying weekly/daily turnover tracking noise). HONEST PRIORS: this lab's prior on any predictive candidate is DEAD -- 8 of 9 prior single_asset_trend proposals with a verdict are DEAD (the sole EFA one included), tsmom_12m/tsmom_ensemble/the whole trend family are DEAD, and diversified buy-and-hold already owns this same international-equity beta as one of this lab's two survivors, so gate 2's diversification test is well-placed to reject this even if gate 1 passes. DECLARED HAZARD: EFA is a diversified basket across many European and Asia-Pacific economies, and Japan/Australia/other non-European constituents have no exposure to the German/EU fiscal story at all, diluting the signal this thesis actually rests on; a broader Europe-only vehicle would express it more cleanly but is outside tradefabe.engine.UNIVERSE. The pre-registered DSR/CPCV gate, not this rationale, decides ALIVE/DEAD. DISTINCTNESS: EFA has been proposed twice before (both FX-mechanism, one DEAD, one pending) and never at lookback=126 or under this earnings-growth mechanism; single_asset_trend itself was already used today for QQQ, USO and SPY, but on an AI-capex, oil-supply-shock and buyback/passive-flow mechanism respectively -- this is a fourth, unrelated structural driver.

Citation: AIM Congress, "Germany's 2026 draft budget unveils record EUR126.7B investment, EUR174.3B borrowing to boost infrastructure, defence" (aimcongress.com/articles/germanys-2026-draft-budget-unveils-record-1267b-investment-1743b-borrowing-to-boost-infrastructure-defence) -- EUR126.7bn 2026 investment vs EUR74.5bn 2024, EUR108.2bn 2026 defense budget exceeding UK (~EUR72bn) and France (~EUR60bn) combined, EUR174.3bn 2026 borrowing. Deutsche Bank, "Germany update: breaking from the brake" (flow.db.com/Topics/macro-and-markets/germany-update-breaking-from-the-brake) and Defence Finance Monitor, "Germany's defence spending surge" (defencefinancemonitor.substack.com/p/germanys-defence-spending-surge-and) -- EUR1tn multi-year public-investment package (~23% of German GDP), NATO European defense spending rising to 2.8% of GDP in 2026 en route to 3.5% by 2029. Citigroup, "European Equity Strategy: European Fiscal Stimulus Revisited" (citigroup.com/global/insights/european-equity-strategy-european-fiscal-stimulus-revisited) and Cambridge Associates, "Germany's Fiscal Boost Remains a Tailwind" (cambridgeassociates.com/insight/germanys-fiscal-boost) -- European defense and German infrastructure spending estimated to add ~2pp and ~1pp of incremental EPS growth per year respectively; STOXX Europe 600 EPS +14% H1 2026, +15% full-year forecast; German equities' consensus EPS CAGR through 2029 revised from 11% to 13%; 63% of surveyed European fund managers name the German fiscal package as the main growth-acceleration catalyst. This ledger's own graveyard.csv (rp_single_asset_trend_EFA_252, DEAD, OOS Sharpe -0.146) and pipeline_ideas.csv (rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_EFA_UUP_252_63) are cited only to establish this proposal's mechanism is distinct from those, not as performance claims. Hong, H. & Stein, J.C. (1999), "A Unified Theory of Underreaction, Momentum Trading, and Overreaction in Asset Markets," Journal of Finance -- gradual information diffusion as the trend-persistence mechanism this proposal invokes for a fiscal-execution driver.


### `rp_cross_sectional_rank_momentum_70_3` (primitive `cross_sectional_rank`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-22, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: metric=momentum, lookback=70, k=3. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: cross_sectional_rank is the least-proposed primitive in pipeline_ideas.csv (16 uses vs 17-19 for the other five) as of 2026-08-22, so actively looked for a current structural setup to motivate it. Multiple independent 2026 macro threads point to the same broad, synchronized rotation across the UNIVERSE rather than a single-pair story: (1) the Fed delivered three consecutive 0.25% cuts in late 2025, held at 3.50-3.75% on 2026-07-29 (a 9-3 split vote), and is still in an active easing posture -- exactly the kind of cycle stage that differentiates assets by rate-sensitivity/duration rather than moving them together; (2) credit conditions are historically loose -- HY OAS ~271-281bp and IG ~81bp both sit in the richest decile of their own history (vs a ~450bp HY long-run median), MOVE is dormant at 74.7, and the 2s10s curve has re-steepened to +35bp -- a backdrop that structurally favors higher-beta, more-levered exposures over defensive ones; (3) the dollar is in a distinct weakening trend, DXY down from ~99.9 to 98.10 over August (-1.7%, worst week in three months) with most major banks projecting the mid-90s by year end, which structurally supports commodity-linked (GLD/SLV/DBC) and non-US (EFA/EEM) legs of the UNIVERSE against USD-linked/defensive legs; and (4) small caps are the clearest expression of the rate-cut sensitivity channel -- IWM's larger floating-rate debt load makes it structurally more levered to a cutting cycle than SPY, consistent with the average +700bp 12-month Russell-2000-vs-S&P-500 outperformance across the five Fed cutting cycles since 1990. None of this is a claim that any single strategy has 'been working' -- it's a structural argument that a synchronized, multi-cause easing/dollar-weakness/loose-credit regime should widen cross-sectional dispersion in trailing returns across the full 15-ticker UNIVERSE (rate-sensitive, high-beta and commodity/non-US legs trending up; defensive and USD-linked legs lagging) -- precisely the dispersion a long-top-K/short-bottom-K momentum rank is built to capture. lookback=70 (~14 weeks) is sized to the recent acceleration of this rotation (e.g. Russell 2000 +11% in July alone vs SPY <1%) rather than the longer 84-252-day windows already tested, which would still weight pre-rotation dynamics heavily; k=3 matches the modal choice among prior cross_sectional_rank proposals. Weekly rebalance keeps turnover reasonable while staying responsive to a rotation moving on a weeks-to-months cadence, not a multi-year one.

Citation: MTFX Group, 'US Dollar Forecast August 2026' (DXY ~99.9->98.10 in August, -1.7%, worst week in three months, most major banks projecting mid-90s by year end); Convex, HY Credit Spread (OAS) 271bp as of 2026-08-12; Seeking Alpha, 'Beyond August Volatility: Outlook From Our Investment Grade Experts' (HY OAS 281bp/richest decile vs ~450bp long-run median, IG 81bp, BBB 100bp, fed funds 3.63%, 2s10s +35bp, MOVE 74.7); EBC Financial Group, 'Russell 2000: Small Caps Steal the Spotlight in 2026's Great Rotation' (Russell 2000 +11% in July vs SPY <1%, average +700bp 12-month outperformance across five Fed cutting cycles since 1990, small-cap floating-rate debt sensitivity to cuts); reporting on the Fed's 2026-07-29 FOMC hold at 3.50-3.75% on a 9-3 split vote after three consecutive late-2025 cuts.


### `rp_single_asset_trend_UUP_126` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-23, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=UUP, lookback=126. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: The Fed has shifted decisively toward a cutting cycle since early August 2026: cooling core inflation, a contracting labor market, and softening retail sales have pushed September-hold odds down and cut odds up, while the Treasury has expanded its long-bond buyback program to contain borrowing costs -- both actions widening the case for a lower path of US real rates relative to other major economies. DXY has already moved from roughly 101.70 in late July 2026 to about 98.8 by 2026-08-21, a multi-month low, consistent with the well-documented FX mechanism where a shrinking rate advantage (uncovered-interest-parity unwind) produces sustained multi-quarter dollar depreciation once a cutting cycle is underway, not a one-off repricing. single_asset_trend on UUP (which tracks DXY) is built to capture that structural, policy-driven persistence rather than a short-term dip: a 126-day (~6-month) lookback is long enough to hold through a full cutting-cycle drift instead of chopping on weekly noise. Distinct from the pipeline's existing UUP draw (rp_single_asset_trend_UUP_63, a much shorter 63-day, weekly-freq window) and from rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_EFA_UUP_252_63, which pairs a 63-day UUP leg against an EFA equity-trend leg rather than trading the dollar's own trend signal standalone at this horizon.

Citation: Menkhoff, Sarno, Schmeling & Schrimpf (2012), 'Currency Momentum Strategies', Journal of Financial Economics 106(3) -- the FX trend/momentum mechanism tied to interest-rate-differential dynamics. Current data: TradingEconomics, 'United States Dollar' (tradingeconomics.com/united-states/currency) -- DXY ~98.8 on 2026-08-21 vs ~101.70 in late July 2026; Vantage Markets, 'US Dollar Index (DXY) Forecast: Worst Week in Three Months' (vantagemarkets.com/market-analysis/us-dollar-index-dxy-forecast-worst-week-august-3-7-2026); FXStreet, 'US Dollar Index softens ... as traders ramp up Fed rate cut bets' (fxstreet.com) -- Fed rate-cut repricing and cooling labor-market/inflation data cited as the drivers.


### `rp_cross_sectional_rank_low_vol_45_3` (primitive `cross_sectional_rank`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-23, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: metric=low_vol, lookback=45, k=3. Rebalance freq: D.

Rationale: cross_sectional_rank is the least-proposed primitive in pipeline_ideas.csv (17 uses vs 18-20 for the other five, prior to today's addition) as of 2026-08-22, and today's earlier cross_sectional_rank proposal already used the momentum metric -- this proposal uses low_vol instead, motivated by a DIFFERENT and more recent structural reading. STRUCTURAL, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM: nothing here argues any strategy has 'been working' -- every input is a volatility-index level or a documented market-structure mechanism. THE CURRENT STRUCTURAL CONDITION is a live divergence between AGGREGATE and CROSS-SECTIONAL volatility, not a level of either alone: the CBOE VIX (index-level, 30-day implied vol on the S&P 500) touched 14.2 on 2026-08-17, its lowest print of 2026, while the VIXEQ (the CBOE's single-stock-average implied-vol index) remains only modestly below its own 31.56 long-run average -- i.e. individual names are still pricing meaningfully more uncertainty than the index-level number would suggest, because single-name moves are currently offsetting rather than compounding at the index level. THE MECHANISM this motivates, stated as economics: Baker, Bradley & Wurgler (2011, 'Benchmarks as Limits to Arbitrage: Understanding the Low-Volatility Anomaly', Financial Analysts Journal) document that benchmark-relative mandates structurally bid up higher-beta/higher-vol names disproportionately in calm, low-index-vol regimes (chasing relative return within a fixed tracking-error budget), while depressing demand for low-vol names despite their comparable or better risk-adjusted return -- an anomaly that is a cross-sectional dispersion effect, not an index-timing one, so it specifically requires the CURRENT condition of muted index vol coexisting with non-trivial single-name vol dispersion, rather than either extreme alone (a genuinely low-dispersion market or a genuinely high-index-vol one would not motivate this trade the same way). A SECOND, INDEPENDENT structural point sharpens the asymmetry rather than driving the thesis alone: strategists are flagging that the market is entering the historically stormier mid-August-to-mid-October stretch from already-suppressed index vol and near-record equity levels, which raises the payoff of a defensive cross-sectional tilt toward the lowest-vol UNIVERSE names if a vol-normalization episode arrives, without requiring a directional index call either way. PARAMETERS: lookback=45 (~2.25 months) is sized to measure trailing realized vol over a window long enough to be a stable per-ticker vol estimate but short enough to reflect the CURRENT divergence rather than average it away with a stale regime -- distinct from the 63/84/126/252 lookbacks already tested for low_vol in this ledger, none of which specifically targets this recent a window. freq=D is also new for this metric (prior low_vol proposals used W three times and M once): daily rebalancing keeps the ranking responsive to a dispersion regime that the cited sources describe as actively shifting week to week, not a multi-month-stable state. k=3 matches the modal choice among prior cross_sectional_rank proposals, not searched to fit this window. HONEST DISCLOSURE: three of the four prior low_vol cross_sectional_rank proposals in this ledger have already verdicted DEAD (63/3, 252/2, 84/3; 126/5 still pending) -- this is not evidence the mechanism doesn't work at some window, only that a materially different, currently-live dated dispersion regime (aggregate-vol-at-2026-low while single-name vol stays elevated) has not been tested at this lookback/freq before. The pre-registered DSR/CPCV gate decides ALIVE/DEAD, not this rationale.

Citation: Baker, M., Bradley, B. & Wurgler, J. (2011), 'Benchmarks as Limits to Arbitrage: Understanding the Low-Volatility Anomaly', Financial Analysts Journal 67(1) -- the benchmark-relative-mandate mechanism behind the low-volatility anomaly cited here. CNBC, 'VIX: Wall Street's "fear gauge" hits 2026 low -- here's why' (2026-08-17, https://www.cnbc.com/2026/08/17/stock-market-volatility-vix-wall-street.html) -- VIX at 14.2, lowest 2026 print, and the entering-the-historically-stormy-Aug-Oct-window context. Penn Mutual Asset Management, 'The S&P 500 Index Is Calm. Its Stocks Aren't.' (2026-08-20, https://www.pennmutualam.com/market-insights-news/blogs/chart-of-the-week/2026-08-20-the-sp-500-index-is-calm-its-stocks-aren-t) -- VIXEQ single-stock implied vol remaining modestly elevated relative to its 31.56 long-run average even as index-level VIX sits at 2026 lows.

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-23, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=UUP, ticker_b=USO, lookback_a=189, lookback_b=42. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: asset_class_trend_hedge is tied for least-proposed primitive in pipeline_ideas.csv (19 uses, same as static_spread_carry vs 18 for pair_zscore/cross_sectional_rank/curve_carry) as of 2026-08-22, and the UUP/USO pairing specifically has not been proposed yet, so actively looked for a current structural setup to motivate an under-used leg combination rather than defaulting to an already-covered pair. Two independent, currently-reinforcing structural stories, each suited to its own lookback: (1) the dollar is in a fiscal-deficit-driven structural downtrend -- US general government deficit is projected near 7.4% of GDP in 2026 with debt/GDP rising toward 123% by 2028, and ActionForex flagged a mid-August 2026 "structural breakdown toward 90" for DXY, consistent with ABN AMRO's FX Outlook 2026 call for broad dollar weakness through H2 2026 -- a slow, multi-month narrative better captured by a long lookback than daily noise; (2) crude has its own supply-side tightening, unrelated to the dollar -- after most of 2026 in contango, WTI futures moved into backwardation by mid-August per Investing.com and CME Group, driven by Strait of Hormuz supply disruption -- a faster-moving signal suited to a short lookback. asset_class_trend_hedge's sign-of-trailing-return construction lets each leg trend on its own timescale; the checkable relationship this primitive requires is the well-established inverse USD/commodity-pricing mechanism (a structurally weaker dollar mechanically supports USD-denominated commodity prices), independent of and additive to oil's own supply-driven catalyst -- verified independently by the primitive's own calibration-window correlation check.
