### `rp_static_spread_carry_HYG_LQD_b` (primitive `static_spread_carry`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-23, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=HYG, ticker_b=LQD, long_leg=b. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: MECHANISM: static_spread_carry is a fixed, always-on long-short -- a structural risk-premium bet, not a directional forecast or a bet on near-term spread movement. The economic content lives entirely in WHICH side is chosen long at proposal time. HYG (broad high-yield corporate bond ETF) and LQD (broad investment-grade corporate bond ETF) are this lab's only two corporate-credit instruments, and this ledger already holds the classic direction: rp_static_spread_carry_HYG_LQD_a (proposed 2026-08-04, long HYG/short LQD), which harvests the standard credit risk premium -- compensation for bearing default, downgrade and liquidity risk that IG debt does not carry. This proposal takes the OPPOSITE leg -- long LQD / short HYG (long_leg='b') -- which is NOT a bet that the classic premium reverses or that spreads widen from here (that would be a momentum/mean-reversion claim about the spread itself, the right shape for pair_zscore or single_asset_trend, not this primitive). It is a DIFFERENT, also well-documented structural premium: the quality / betting-against-beta anomaly. Frazzini & Pedersen (2014, 'Betting Against Beta', Journal of Financial Economics 111(1)) and Asness, Frazzini & Pedersen (2019, 'Quality Minus Junk', Review of Accounting Studies 24) show that leverage-constrained investors who cannot use leverage to hit a return target instead reach for risk by overweighting high-beta, high-leverage, lower-quality assets -- bidding those assets' risk-adjusted returns down and structurally UNDERPRICING safer, higher-quality assets relative to their own risk. Harvesting that mispricing means being long the safer/higher-quality asset and short the more-leveraged, more business-cycle-correlated one, funded by giving up some of the junk yield pickup -- exactly long_leg='b' on this pair, since LQD issuers are investment-grade (lower leverage, more diversified, higher and more stable credit quality) and HYG issuers are junk-rated (higher leverage, higher effective beta to the credit and business cycle). WHY THIS SPECIFIC PREMIUM LOOKS UNUSUALLY WELL-COMPENSATED RIGHT NOW, not merely in general (the BAB/QMJ anomaly is a structural, permanent claim, but the CURRENT leverage backdrop is what makes today a good time to add exposure to it, and is the actual research finding this run is contributing): US AI-hyperscaler-linked debt issuance has exploded in 2026 -- roughly $220bn issued year-to-date through 2026-08-10, versus only ~$12.5bn over the same stretch of 2025 -- and high-yield bonds specifically tied to AI infrastructure projects reached $26.6bn in just the first four months of 2026, already more than double all of 2025's full-year total (Morgan Stanley sees a further $40-50bn of near-term financing demand in the pipeline). Crucially, the market is NOT re-rating this uniformly: since June 2026, high-yield data-center spreads have WIDENED as investors turn more cautious on execution-intensive, tenant-concentrated AI infrastructure credits, while investment-grade hyperscaler spreads have TIGHTENED modestly on the back of those issuers' scale and stronger capital-market access (Man Group's H2 2026 Credit Outlook; Penn Mutual's 2026-08-06 'AI Infrastructure Bonds' spread note). That bifurcation is itself evidence the market is starting to discriminate along exactly the leverage/quality axis this trade is built to harvest -- named risks are concentrated tenant exposure, construction execution and chip-refresh-cycle risk, and reliance on assumed AI revenue growth to cover long-dated lease obligations (Reuters/Yahoo, 'US corporate AI debt surge tests investor limits as fatigue emerges'), all of which sit specifically on leveraged, junk-rated issuers financing speculative buildout -- the BAB/QMJ mechanism's textbook setup. HONESTY CAVEATS, stated plainly. (1) HYG is a broad, diversified high-yield index; AI-datacenter-linked issuers are still a minority of its holdings today, so this is a market-wide quality-tilt bet MOTIVATED BY, not narrowly concentrated in, AI-linked credit risk -- I am not claiming HYG is an AI-debt proxy. (2) Both directions of the same HYG/LQD pair now sit in this ledger's pre-registration record; that is mechanically fine (two distinct, independently pre-registered economic hypotheses, not a parameter sweep of one), but a skeptical reader should be able to tell this isn't 'the obvious side was taken, so try the other one' -- the citation anchor (BAB/QMJ, a named academic anomaly distinct from the plain credit premium) and the specific current bifurcation condition (dated to June 2026 onward, not available to the 2026-08-04 proposal) are what make this a separate, independently-motivated claim rather than a coin flip on sign. (3) Leverage/quality anomalies have historically been partially arbitraged in easy-credit regimes, and this lab has never tested BAB/QMJ in any form before, so there is no prior-art track record here to lean on either way. (4) This is still fundamentally a carry trade funded by giving up HYG's higher running yield, so its worst-case scenario is a long, low-volatility bleed rather than a sharp drawdown -- gate 3's pain test is the one to watch. freq M matches every other static_spread_carry candidate in this ledger: a fixed spread's only turnover is the periodic rebalance back to target weights, so monthly is the natural cadence, not a tuned choice.

Citation: AI hyperscaler debt issuance and pipeline: Morgan Stanley financing-demand estimate and 2026 issuance figures via Reuters/Yahoo Finance, 'Analysis-US corporate AI debt surge tests investor limits as fatigue emerges' (finance.yahoo.com/markets/stocks/articles/analysis-us-corporate-ai-debt-100218069.html); AI infrastructure HY bond issuance ($26.6bn in first four months of 2026, >2x full-year 2025): White & Case Debt Explorer, 'High yield bond markets ride the data center boom' (debtexplorer.whitecase.com/leveraged-finance-commentary/high-yield-bond-markets-ride-the-data-center-boom). Credit spread bifurcation since June 2026 (HY data-center spreads widening, IG hyperscaler spreads tightening): Man Group, 'H2 2026 Credit Outlook: The AI Buildout -- Boom or Bubble?' (man.com/insights/2026-h2-credit-outlook); Penn Mutual Asset Management, 'AI Infrastructure Bonds: A Full Picture of Spreads', 2026-08-06 (pennmutualam.com/market-insights-news/blogs/chart-of-the-week/2026-08-06-ai-infrastructure-bonds-a-full-picture-of-spreads); Sage Advisory, 'AI Buildout Fuels Hyperscaler Bond Issuance and Credit Spread Volatility' (sageadvisory.com/article/hyperscaler-debt-deluge-the-new-driver-of-ig-spread-pressure). Academic anchor for the quality/betting-against-beta premium: Frazzini & Pedersen (2014), 'Betting Against Beta', Journal of Financial Economics 111(1), doi.org/10.1016/j.jfineco.2013.10.005; Asness, Frazzini & Pedersen (2019), 'Quality Minus Junk', Review of Accounting Studies 24, doi.org/10.1007/s11142-018-9470-2. In-repo prior art: pipeline_ideas.csv row rp_static_spread_carry_HYG_LQD_a, proposed 2026-08-04, the opposing (classic credit-premium) direction on the same pair.


### `rp_static_spread_carry_USO_DBC_b` (primitive `static_spread_carry`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-23, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=USO, ticker_b=DBC, long_leg=b. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: MECHANISM: static_spread_carry is a fixed, always-on long-short -- a structural risk-premium bet on the LEVEL of a curve/roll differential, not a forecast of oil's price. This proposal is the OPPOSITE side of rp_static_spread_carry_USO_DBC_a (proposed 2026-08-04, long USO/short DBC), and that prior proposal's own stated risk is exactly the condition that has now materialized: it built long USO/short DBC on the WTI curve's steep FRONT-of-curve backwardation following the Feb 2026 Middle East supply shock (Dec 2026 WTI as much as $40 below May/June delivery at the time), and it named the failure mode explicitly -- 'when the front of the curve flips to steep contango, front-month rolling produces the most negative roll yield.' By mid-August 2026 that flip has happened: the WTI futures curve has moved into contango, forward months trading at a premium to the front month, reflecting improved/ample supply conditions relative to demand rather than the acute shortage that drove backwardation earlier in the year. The two UNIVERSE tickers separate roll-yield exposure to that curve shape by construction, not by correlation, for the identical reason the prior proposal invoked in the other direction: USO's benchmark methodology rolls the near-month WTI contract into the next month on a fixed near-term schedule and is therefore fully exposed to whatever the front-of-curve slope currently is, while DBC tracks the DBIQ Optimum Yield index, whose rule searches out to 13 months of contract maturities and selects the point on the curve with the most favorable implied roll -- built specifically to reduce (not eliminate) the roll cost when the curve is in contango, rather than concentrate on it. A fixed, always-on long DBC / short USO therefore harvests the negative roll-yield differential now facing a purely front-month-rolling instrument versus a curve-optimizing one, hedging out most broad commodity-price beta (both legs are heavily oil-linked; DBC's energy weight is large), with no directional forecast on the price of oil itself. HONESTY CAVEATS. (1) This is explicitly the mirror image of an already-pre-registered claim on the same pair -- that is not disqualifying (two independently-dated, opposite-regime hypotheses on one pair is mechanically fine, same as this ledger's HYG/LQD a/b pair), but a skeptical reader should note the two proposals share one economic story (front-of-curve roll differential) applied to opposite regimes at two different dates, not two unrelated mechanisms; the check is whether the regime genuinely flipped, which the contango-vs-backwardation search results cited below support. (2) DBC's Optimum Yield methodology reduces contango drag, it does not eliminate it -- DBC's own roll can still take a loss when the whole curve is offered at a premium, so this is a bet on a smaller loss for DBC than for USO, not a bet that DBC is carry-free. (3) DBC is a diversified 14-commodity basket (energy, metals, agriculture) with meaningful non-energy weight, so this spread is not a clean, single-factor front-of-curve isolate the way a WTI calendar-spread future would be; some of DBC's return comes from commodities with no relationship to the WTI curve at all, which dilutes but does not remove the intended roll-differential exposure. (4) No claim is made about how either leg or either direction of this pair has recently performed -- the argument here is the mechanical curve shape and each instrument's known, prospectus-level roll methodology, not a track record. freq M matches every other static_spread_carry candidate in this ledger and the natural cadence of a fixed spread's only turnover: periodic rebalance back to target weights.

Citation: WTI term structure in contango, mid-August 2026 (contrast with earlier-2026 backwardation from Middle East supply disruption): investing.com, 'Oil Trading in August 2026: Backwardation, Crack Spreads and Hormuz Risk Explained' (m.investing.com/analysis/oil-trading-in-august-2026-backwardation-crack-spreads-and-hormuz-risk-explained-200686148); CME Group, 'Implications of WTI Oil Futures In Backwardation Amid the Supply Crunch' (cmegroup.com/insights/economic-research/2026/implications-of-wti-oil-futures-in-backwardation-amid-the-supply-crunch.html) for the earlier-2026 backwardation baseline this proposal contrasts against. USO roll methodology and contango cost mechanics: SEC EDGAR, United States Oil Fund LP prospectus (sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1327068/); Yahoo Finance/24-7 Wall St, 'USO's Front Month Oil Strategy Has Lagged Crude Oil Itself by Half Since 2014, And the Roll Cost Is the Reason' (finance.yahoo.com/markets/commodities/articles/uso-front-month-oil-strategy-173327031.html). DBC Optimum Yield methodology: Invesco, 'Invesco DB Commodity Index Tracking Fund' fund page (invesco.com/us-rest/contentdetail?contentId=cb3113e88ed90710VgnVCM1000006e36b50aRCRD); Yahoo Finance/24-7 Wall St, 'PDBC Promises Diversified Commodities Without K-1 Tax Forms, But the Workaround Hides a Long Term Roll Cost' (finance.yahoo.com/markets/commodities/articles/pdbc-promises-diversified-commodities-103332807.html). In-repo prior art: pipeline_ideas.csv row rp_static_spread_carry_USO_DBC_a, proposed 2026-08-04, the opposing (front-of-curve-backwardation) direction on the same pair, whose stated risk (a flip to contango) is the condition this proposal responds to.


### `rp_cross_sectional_rank_momentum_45_3` (primitive `cross_sectional_rank`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-24, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: metric=momentum, lookback=45, k=3. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: cross_sectional_rank is a fixed-metric long-top-K/short-bottom-K ranking across the full UNIVERSE (15 tickers spanning equity, rates, commodity, real_estate and currency buckets) -- its edge, if any, depends on the UNIVERSE currently having genuine cross-sectional dispersion in what drives each asset, not one shared macro factor moving everything together (in which case a long-short nets out the common factor and adds nothing). Metric/lookback/k are the only pre-registered free parameters, so the economically-motivated choices available today are WHICH metric captures the current dispersion regime and over WHAT window. CURRENT STRUCTURAL CONDITION: single-stock implied correlation is unusually LOW right now and still below its long-term average even after a recent rise -- Cboe's 1-Month Implied Correlation Index (COR1M) stood at 7.88 on 2026-08-12 and the 3-Month (COR3M) at 9.71; COR1M and COR6M had risen 6.9% and 17.2% respectively in July 2026 but 'both indices were still below long-term averages' per Cboe's own July 2026 Index Insights note. Low implied correlation is the textbook signature of an idiosyncratic-catalyst-driven ('stock-picker's') market rather than a macro-driven one, where a single risk-on/risk-off factor dominates and cross-sectional ranking just re-expresses that one factor. SEPARATELY, market breadth/concentration is genuinely shifting, not just correlation: the equal-weight S&P 500 is outperforming the cap-weighted index by roughly 1.7-3 points in 2026 (CNBC, 2026-08-23: 'Equal-weight S&P 500 is leading the 2026 market and its flagship trade just hit $100 billion'), the Mag 7 mega-caps were flat in H1 2026 versus +9.3% for the S&P 500 overall, and commentary attributes the equal-weight index's outperformance to 'declining market concentration, a rotation toward small-caps, and a persistent value tilt' (Capital Group, 'Fresh breadth? Market concentration in 3 charts'). A market where returns are no longer concentrated in one mega-cap-tech leadership group is structurally one where MORE of the UNIVERSE's own tickers can plausibly carry independent, own-asset trend information worth ranking, rather than every equity sleeve just tracking the same handful of names. WHY MOMENTUM, NOT REVERSAL OR LOW_VOL, and WHY freq=W, lookback=45: a low-correlation, broadening-breadth regime is one where idiosyncratic trends have room to persist without being drowned out or synchronized by a dominant common factor -- the mechanism this proposal is betting on is dispersion enabling persistent, asset-specific trend, not mean-reversion of a shared factor (which is the opposite regime: high correlation, one shared move overshooting and snapping back). Lookback=45 trading days (~9 weeks, matching freq=W) is sized to the regime's own recency: COR1M's rise dates specifically to July 2026, so a window reaching back roughly two months captures the dispersion regime as it has actually existed, without extending back into H1 2026's more concentrated, higher-correlation, mega-cap-dominated conditions this proposal is explicitly distinguishing itself from. k=3 (long top-3, short bottom-3 of 15) is this primitive's typical mid-range breadth, unchanged from many prior proposals -- k itself is not the variable this rationale is arguing for. HONESTY CAVEATS. (1) COR1M/COR3M measure implied correlation among S&P 500 SINGLE STOCKS, not literally the correlation structure of this primitive's own 15-ticker, multi-asset-class UNIVERSE -- the inference here is that a broad-market low-correlation, idiosyncratic-driven regime plausibly extends to how UNIVERSE's equity/rates/commodity/ currency components move relative to each other, not a direct measurement of UNIVERSE's own cross-sectional correlation today; a skeptical reader should weigh this as suggestive, not as a precise, on-target statistic. (2) The equal-weight-vs-cap-weight and Mag-7-breadth facts are cited ONLY as evidence of market STRUCTURE (how concentrated or broad current return-generation is) -- explicitly NOT as a claim that any momentum, cross-sectional, or other trading strategy has recently performed well; I am not aware of and am not relying on any such performance claim. (3) This exact (momentum, lookback=45, k=3) combination has not been proposed before in this ledger, but eight other momentum lookback/k combinations already have (63, 70, 84, 105, 126, 168, 189, 252) -- I make no claim about how any of them fared; this proposal stands on the dispersion-regime mechanism above, not on differentiation from prior attempts. (4) Implied correlation and market-breadth conditions are dated to early-to-mid August 2026 and can shift by the time this candidate reaches an actual OOS test; that lag is inherent to any point-in-time structural motivation and is the same caveat every prior proposal in this ledger carries.

Citation: Cboe implied correlation levels: Cboe, 'Index Insights: July 2026' (cboe.com/insights/posts/index-insights-july-2026); Investing.com, 'CBOE 1-Month Implied Correlation Historical Data (COR1M)' (investing.com/indices/cboe-1month-implied-correlation-historical-data), COR1M=7.88 on 2026-08-12; Investing.com, 'Implied Correlation Historical Data (COR3M)' (investing.com/indices/implied-correlation-historical-data), COR3M=9.71. Market breadth / concentration: CNBC, 'Equal-weight S&P 500 is leading the 2026 market and its flagship trade just hit $100 billion', 2026-08-23 (cnbc.com/2026/08/23/stocks-market-investing-sp500-spx.html); Capital Group, 'Fresh breadth? Market concentration in 3 charts' (capitalgroup.com/institutional/insights/articles/fresh-breadth-market-concentration-3-charts.html); ts2.tech, 'S&P 500 Equal Weight Ahead by 1.7 Points with Market Breadth in Focus' (ts2.tech/en/sp-500-equal-weight-leads-by-1-7-points-as-market-breadth-wobbles/).


### `rp_pair_zscore_HYG_LQD_75_2p75_5p5` (primitive `pair_zscore`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-24, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=HYG, ticker_b=LQD, z_window=75, z_entry=2.75, z_stop=5.5. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: STRUCTURAL, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM -- nothing below asserts any strategy has recently performed well; every input is a bond-market pricing level sourced from ICE BofA/FRED index data. THE MECHANISM: US high-yield credit spreads are historically stretched tight right now, not merely 'low' -- the ICE BofA US High Yield OAS (BAMLH0A0HYM2) sits at ~2.70% (271bps) as of mid-August 2026, which sources place in the richest decile of its own multi-decade history against a long-run median near 450bps, while investment-grade OAS (~80-81bps, BBB ~100bps) sits closer to its own typical range -- so the HY/IG ratio (~3.5x) is roughly at its long-run average, meaning the compression is a market-wide 'priced to perfection' phenomenon (Fed easing, dormant bond volatility, contained defaults) rather than a quality-tier-specific distortion. A spread this far into its own historical distribution is a mean-reversion setup by construction -- percentile extremity is the definition of 'far from typical', independent of any directional call on WHEN it normalizes. z-scored log(HYG/LQD) is a cheap proxy for this same credit-quality spread (HYG carries the high-yield credit-risk premium, LQD the investment-grade one; TLT/duration risk is not isolated out, but the spread between two credit ETFs of different quality tiers moves first and most with credit-spread changes specifically): a swing toward historically-typical OAS levels shows up as HYG underperforming LQD, i.e. the log-spread moving through its z-score bands. Params: z_window=75d (~15wk, mid-term relative to the >=6yr period richest-decile framing covers), entry/stop widened versus this ledger's existing shorter-window HYG/LQD pair_zscore rows (90d/2.0-4.0, 252d/2.5-5.0) since a spread already near a historical extreme should need a proportionally further extension, not a tighter one, before this signal treats it as 'unusually stretched' relative to its OWN recent trailing window.

Citation: ICE BofA US High Yield Index Option-Adjusted Spread, BAMLH0A0HYM2, FRED (fred.stlouisfed.org/series/BAMLH0A0HYM2), and ICE BofA US Corporate Index OAS (investment-grade) as summarized via Convex (convextrade.com/metrics/bamlh0a0hym2) and MacroMicro US Credit Spread collection, data as of 2026-08-12/17.


### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_SPY_USO_126_42` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-24, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=SPY, ticker_b=USO, lookback_a=126, lookback_b=42. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: STRUCTURAL, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM -- no assertion is made about how SPY, USO, equities or any live/paper strategy has recently performed; asset_class_trend_hedge combines two independent single_asset_trend legs into one candidate, and the case made here is for why their trend directions should tend to OFFSET around a specific, still-live episode, not that the two happen to be historically uncorrelated. THE CURRENT STRUCTURAL CONDITION: WTI crude's futures curve remains in outright backwardation as of mid-August 2026, with near-term prices commanding a premium over deferred months -- WTI trading in the mid-$80s (recently testing $84-86) and Brent holding a $91-92 premium -- driven by the still-unresolved Strait of Hormuz situation: shipping volumes through the strait remain well below pre-crisis levels and confidence in safe passage remains low, a persistent physical supply constraint, not a one-off price spike that has already round-tripped. THE MECHANISM linking that to SPY specifically: this is the identical oil-supply-shock-to-equity-discount-rate channel already pre-registered in this ledger for rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_SPY_GLD_252_252 (frozen 2026-08-05, citing the same Hormuz closure) -- a geopolitically-driven oil supply shock lifts realized and expected inflation, which lifts discount rates and compresses broad equity valuations, while the SAME shock mechanically and directly lifts the price of oil itself. That gives SPY's trailing-return trend a negative expected sign around oil-shock episodes at the same time USO's trend is positively driven by the shock's physical supply leg -- an offsetting relationship tied to a specific, named channel (the discount-rate/inflation pass-through from a persistent energy supply constraint), not a coincidental historical decorrelation. Unlike the already-pre-registered SPY_GLD candidate, which pairs equity against a safe-haven/reserve-diversification asset, this candidate pairs equity directly against the commodity actually experiencing the physical shortage -- a more direct expression of the same real mechanism, and a ticker pair not yet tested by this pipeline (QQQ_USO and SPY_DBC/SPY_GLD are proposed already; SPY_USO specifically is not). PARAMETERS: lookback_a=126 (~6 months) for SPY, matched to the multi-month horizon over which discount-rate repricing from a sustained (not transient) supply shock actually shows up in broad-index trailing returns; lookback_b=42 (~2 months) for USO, shorter to track the faster-moving physical curve/backwardation dynamics described as still actively evolving week-to-week in mid-August coverage. freq=M matches the multi-month horizon of lookback_a, the coarser of the two legs, consistent with this primitive's existing entries. Honest limitation: like every trend leg proposed here, each side on its own sits in the trend family this lab has killed many times over; the claim under test is only whether this specific cross-asset-class pairing earns its place under gate 2, and the mechanical asset-class-difference and calibration-window decorrelation guards (both already checked post-proposal for every asset_class_trend_hedge candidate) apply here exactly as they do to every other entry.

Citation: Investing.com, 'Oil Trading in August 2026: Backwardation, Crack Spreads and Hormuz Risk Explained' (https://www.investing.com/analysis/oil-trading-in-august-2026-backwardation-crack-spreads-and-hormuz-risk-explained-200686148), mid-August 2026: WTI/Brent levels, continued backwardation, shipping volumes below pre-crisis levels, low confidence in safe passage. CME Group, 'Implications of WTI Oil Futures In Backwardation Amid the Supply Crunch' (https://www.cmegroup.com/insights/economic-research/2026/implications-of-wti-oil-futures-in-backwardation-amid-the-supply-crunch.html), 2026. Internal precedent: this repo's own STRATEGIES.md pre-registration for rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_SPY_GLD_252_252 (frozen 2026-08-05), which establishes and cites the same oil-shock-to-discount-rate-to-equity-valuation channel for the Feb 2026 Hormuz closure -- this proposal applies the identical, already-accepted mechanism to the commodity directly experiencing the shock (USO) rather than to a safe-haven proxy (GLD).


### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_VNQ_TLT_189_63` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-24, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=VNQ, ticker_b=TLT, lookback_a=189, lookback_b=63. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: asset_class_trend_hedge holds two independent single_asset_trend legs (each leg's own trailing-return sign over its own lookback) in one candidate, and requires the two tickers to come from DIFFERENT ASSET_CLASS buckets (here: VNQ = real_estate, TLT = rates) -- a mechanical stand-in for 'these two legs have a real, checkable relationship', not a correlation search, and this is verified mechanically against 2007-2017 calibration data before the proposal can proceed regardless of how this citation reads. CURRENT STRUCTURAL CONDITION: the 2s10s Treasury curve spent 2022-2024 in its longest inversion since the 1970s (26 months), began un-inverting in September 2024, and by Q4 2025 had settled into a sustained, positive slope across all major tenors after the Fed's late-2024 100bp cut and a further measured 2025 easing cycle. As of 20 Aug 2026 the 10s2s spread sits at roughly +46bps and the 10Y-3M gap at roughly +78bps -- both durably positive, read by commentators as a 'healthy expansion' signal rather than a recession warning. This is a REGIME-LEVEL fact (curve shape has structurally reset from inverted to normal-upward over roughly two years), not a claim about how any strategy has recently performed. SPECIFIC MECHANISM CONNECTING THE TWO LEGS: REITs are widely documented as long-duration, rate-sensitive 'bond proxies' -- their valuations move with long-term rate direction via the cap-rate discount channel, with academic work (e.g. NYU Stern's 'Interest Rate Risk of Real Estate Investment Trusts' and EPRA's 'The Interest Rate Sensitivity of Public Real Estate') estimating REIT interest-rate betas directly against long Treasury proxies and finding effective duration driven by lease-renewal structure, the same underlying long-rate exposure TLT is built to trend-follow. A curve that has just completed a multi-quarter regime shift from inverted to a sustained positive slope is exactly the kind of environment in which VNQ's own trailing-return trend and TLT's own trailing-return trend would be expected to decorrelate from whatever static relationship they held during the older inverted regime, which is the calibration-window property this candidate is actually gated on. PARAMETERS: lookback_a=189 trading days (~9 months) on VNQ, long enough to filter REIT-specific noise (sector rotations, individual property-market headlines) and capture the multi-quarter re-coupling with the rate regime rather than any single data print; lookback_b=63 (~3 months) on TLT, shorter because Treasury long-end direction reprices faster on Fed communication and macro data than REIT valuations do -- an asymmetric long/short lookback pairing, the same shape as this ledger's one ALIVE pipeline candidate, rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_SPY_IEF_252_63. freq=W to avoid over-trading either leg's already-multi-month signal. HONESTY CAVEATS: (1) the REIT-rate literature is explicit that this sensitivity is TIME-VARYING and has switched sign historically (e.g. triple-net-lease REITs show high positive rate betas while hotel REITs have shown negative ones; VNQ is a broad blend), and 'there is no long-run predictive rule' governing the relationship -- this is a plausible-mechanism bet, not a proven constant. (2) VNQ+TLT specifically and this exact lookback pair have not been tested in this pipeline before (checked against pipeline_ideas.csv and graveyard.csv), but the same no-lower-bar rule applies as to every other candidate: 20/21 prior asset_class_trend_hedge proposals verdicted DEAD and one ALIVE (SPY/IEF), and nothing here claims this pair is exempt from that base rate. (3) the calibration-window correlation-cap check (CALIB_CORR_CAP=0.3) is the actual arbiter of whether this claimed relationship holds up in 2007-2017 data; this rationale states the mechanism and its citation, not a prediction that it will pass.

Citation: Yield curve: ycharts.com/indicators/10_2_year_treasury_yield_spread and centralbank.watch/tools/yield-curve/us-yield-curve/ (10s2s +46bps, 10Y-3M +78bps as of 20 Aug 2026, both positive, no recession signal); MUFG Americas, 'Longest 2s10s Yield Curve Inversion Since the 1970s' (chart-of-the-day, data as of 1 May 2024, documenting the 2022-2024 inversion); Seeking Alpha, '2025: The Year Of Un-Inverted Yield Curves' (seekingalpha.com/article/4749829), on the Sept 2024 initial un-inversion and the curve's sustained positive slope across tenors by Q4 2025. REIT rate sensitivity: NYU Stern, 'Interest Rate Risk of Real Estate Investment Trusts' (stern.nyu.edu/sites/default/files/assets/documents/Interest Rate Risk of REITs.pdf); EPRA, 'The Interest Rate Sensitivity of Public Real Estate' (prodapp.epra.com/media/The_interest_rate_sensitivity_of_public_real_estate); International Real Estate Review, 'Interest Rate Sensitivities of REIT Returns' (gssinst.org/irer), on time-varying, sector-dependent REIT rate betas and duration driven by lease-renewal structure.


### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_QQQ_GLD_90_60` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-24, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=QQQ, ticker_b=GLD, lookback_a=90, lookback_b=60. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: Two independent, currently dated structural findings argue this pair should trend-offset rather than merely happen to be historically uncorrelated. First, the equity/bond diversification this repo already leans on structurally is degraded right now: the rolling 3-year stock-bond correlation is in the 89th percentile of the last 35 years, and in 9 of the last 11 months equities and bonds fell together (IMF blog, 'Stock-Bond Diversification Offers Less Protection From Market Selloffs', 2026-02-18) -- the same source attributes this specifically to a shock-type shift: monetary-policy and inflation-surprise shocks push stock-bond correlation POSITIVE, while only demand shocks and flight-to-quality episodes push it negative. QQQ is the highest-duration equity exposure in UNIVERSE (long-dated growth cash flows), making it the sleeve most exposed to exactly the policy/inflation-shock channel now dominating. Second, gold is currently being driven by the OTHER shock type -- a genuine flight-to-quality/currency-debasement channel, not the policy-rate channel: gold ran from the low-$4000s to $4590-4671/oz in mid-to-late August 2026 as DXY fell toward 98.6-99.6 (down ~2.4% on the month) on investor reaction to the Treasury's long-bond buyback announcement, read as a signal of debt-sustainability concern with federal debt having topped $40 trillion (Daily Caller, 'Dollar Sinks As Investors Flock To Gold, Crypto After Scott Bessent's Treasury Buyback', 2026-08-21; Barchart DXY technical note, 2026-08-2x; USAGOLD Daily Precious Metals Market Report, 2026-08-18/21). So the two legs are structurally exposed to different shock channels precisely when the more commonly-used equity/bond hedge (already tried repeatedly in this ledger, e.g. SPY_IEF, IWM_TLT) is degraded by both channels moving the same way -- a debasement/flight-to-quality shock should show up as GLD trending up while a monetary-policy/inflation shock should hit QQQ (high-duration equity) harder than the broader market, giving the pair a chance to decorrelate for a real, nameable reason rather than a historical accident. lookback_a=90 (QQQ) sized to roughly one quarter, matching the multi-month regime the elevated correlation reading describes; lookback_b=60 (GLD) sized to the several-week timescale of the actual August debasement-driven move cited above, following the same reasoning the ledger's prior GLD_UUP_63_63 proposal used for gold's own reaction speed.

Citation: IMF Blog, 'Stock-Bond Diversification Offers Less Protection From Market Selloffs' (imf.org/en/blogs/articles/2026/02/18/stock-bond-diversification-offers-less-protection-from-market-selloffs); Daily Caller, 'Dollar Sinks As Investors Flock To Gold, Crypto After Scott Bessent's Treasury Buyback' (2026-08-21); Barchart, 'DXY Technical Analysis August 2026: US Dollar Index at 99.60 Tests Rising Channel Support Below Declining 200-Day MA'; USAGOLD Daily Precious Metals Market Report, 2026-08-18 through 2026-08-21 (gold $4387-$4671/oz, DXY 98.6-99.6).


### `rp_cross_sectional_rank_momentum_60_3` (primitive `cross_sectional_rank`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-24, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: metric=momentum, lookback=60, k=3. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: cross_sectional_rank ranks the full 15-ticker UNIVERSE by a fixed metric over a lookback and goes long the top-K / short the bottom-K, so it only has something to extract when the CURRENT period's return dispersion across the universe's asset-class buckets (equity/rates/commodity/real_estate/currency) is driven by genuinely different forces rather than one shared factor -- a long-short just nets out a common factor otherwise. CURRENT STRUCTURAL CONDITION: three separate, real market moves are running concurrently right now in DIFFERENT directions across exactly the buckets this UNIVERSE spans. (1) Rates: AI-hyperscaler capex financing has driven roughly $200B of corporate bond issuance from the largest tech firms alone this year -- Nomura estimates this is equivalent to ~25% of net Treasury note/bond issuance to private investors, about 5x its 2025 share -- competing directly with Treasury issuance for duration buyers; the 30-year Treasury closed at 5.31% on 17 Aug 2026, its highest level since 2007, while the 2-year has moved far less (2yr +5bp vs 30yr +37bp since 30 Jun 2026) (247wallst.com, 19 Aug 2026; Seeking Alpha, 'How the AI economy is adding pressure to Treasury yields'). (2) Currency: DXY fell from ~101.70 in late July to a ~3.25-month low near 98.6-99.6 in mid-to-late August, in part on the Treasury's own announcement it would increase long-bond buybacks, read by markets as a debt-sustainability signal (stockpil.com, 22 Aug 2026). (3) Commodities: gold and silver have both continued to run -- gold above $4,600 and silver above $69 as of 21 Aug 2026 (USAGOLD Daily Market Report, 21 Aug 2026) -- on the same debasement-fear channel pushing the dollar down, a DIFFERENT shock than the rates-supply story hitting Treasuries. Rates (weak), currency (weak) and commodities (strong) are thus being pushed by three distinct, nameable structural mechanisms (duration-supply glut, debt-sustainability/debasement fear, and the resulting hard-asset bid) at the same time -- real, current cross-sectional dispersion by construction, exactly the condition cross_sectional_rank with metric=momentum needs to have anything to extract from a long-top/short-bottom ranking. PARAMETER CHOICE: metric=momentum (not reversal) because all three moves above are explicitly ongoing, multi-week TRENDS, not one-off shocks that already mean-reverted; lookback=60 trading days (~3 calendar months) is chosen to span back before the 30 Jun 2026 inflection point in the 2yr/30yr divergence cited above, long enough to capture the building trend rather than just the latest week's headline print; k=3 (top-3/bottom-3 of 15 tickers) is a moderate-breadth choice matching that three distinct buckets are diverging right now, rather than a narrower top/bottom-1 that would just re-express a single pair. HONESTY CAVEAT: this exact (lookback, k) combination has not been tested in this pipeline before (checked against pipeline_ideas.csv and graveyard.csv), but 9 prior cross_sectional_rank momentum proposals at other lookback/k combinations already sit in this ledger with no exemption claimed here -- the calibration/OOS gates downstream are the actual arbiter, not this rationale.

Citation: 247wallst.com, '30-Year Treasury Yield Hits Highest Level Since 2007 as AI Spending and Deficits Collide' (19 Aug 2026); Seeking Alpha, 'How the AI economy is adding pressure to Treasury yields' (Aug 2026), citing Nomura's ~25%-of-net-issuance estimate for AI-related corporate bond supply; stockpil.com, 'Dollar Steadies as Stocks Rally; Gold Hits 3.25-Month High on Debasement Fears' (22 Aug 2026), DXY ~98.6-99.6 post Treasury buyback announcement; USAGOLD Daily Precious Metals Market Report (21 Aug 2026), gold >$4,600/oz, silver >$69/oz.


### `rp_curve_carry_230` (primitive `curve_carry`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-24, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: lookback=230. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: curve_carry trend-follows the real FRED 10y-2y curve slope (DGS10-DGS2) with a DV01-neutral TLT/IEF position, so it only has something to extract when the curve slope is in a genuine, persistent directional TREND rather than chopping around a level -- a trend-follower on a mean-reverting series just churns costs. CURRENT STRUCTURAL CONDITION: the 10y-2y spread is in a real, sustained steepening trend right now, not a one-week print. As of 13-20 Aug 2026 the spread sits at roughly +0.46 to +0.51 percentage points (DGS2 ~4.15%, DGS10 ~4.61-4.63%; 10-2yr Treasury spread widened to 0.48% on 20 Aug 2026 from 0.44% the prior week) -- convextrade.com DGS2/DGS10 series (13 Aug 2026); thecuriosityvine.com Treasury Rates Update (20 Aug 2026). This is described by multiple independent sources as a continuation of a MULTI-QUARTER un-inversion that began in late 2024 and is still running in 2026 (fractionalx.com, "Yield Curve 2026: What the Bear Steepener Actually Signals"), driven by a specific, nameable, structural mechanism distinct from any performance claim: the Fed cutting the short end while long-end yields are pushed up by rising term premium, heavy Treasury issuance funding persistent deficits, and (per a separately-sourced 2026 thread) record AI-hyperscaler-driven corporate bond issuance competing directly with Treasury supply for duration buyers -- a bear steepener, not a bull steepener, meaning the move is a genuine shift in the term-premium/duration-supply relationship rather than a growth-optimism re-rating that could reverse on a single data print. PARAMETER CHOICE: lookback=230 trading days (~11 months) is chosen, near the top of the pre-registered [20,252] range, specifically BECAUSE the steepening is characterized above as a sustained, multi-quarter secular trend (since late 2024) rather than a short-lived move -- a long lookback is the honest match to that structural claim, since a short lookback here would really be trading the same recent-performance signal the routine instructions explicitly warn against, dressed up in structural language. This lookback has not been tested by this primitive before (checked against pipeline_ideas.csv, which already has 20 curve_carry proposals spanning 21-252 but not 230; and graveyard.csv, which has no rp_curve_carry_230 verdict). HONESTY CAVEAT: curve_carry as a primitive already has DEAD verdicts at several other lookbacks (21, 126, 147, 189, 210, 252) in graveyard.csv -- this proposal does not claim those are wrong, only that this specific untested lookback, motivated by the specific multi-quarter-trend structural claim above, has not itself been screened; the downstream calibration/OOS gates are the actual arbiter.

Citation: convextrade.com, DGS2 (2Y Treasury Yield, 4.15%, 13 Aug 2026) and DGS10 (10Y Treasury Yield, 4.63%, 13 Aug 2026); thecuriosityvine.com, "Treasury Rates Update: August 20th, 2026" (10-2yr spread 0.48%, up from 0.44% the prior week); fractionalx.com, "Yield Curve 2026: What the Bear Steepener Actually Signals" (multi-quarter un-inversion since late 2024, term-premium/deficit-driven bear steepener).


### `rp_pair_zscore_HYG_LQD_100_2p25_4p5` (primitive `pair_zscore`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-24, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=HYG, ticker_b=LQD, z_window=100, z_entry=2.25, z_stop=4.5. Rebalance freq: D.

Rationale: MECHANISM: pair_zscore mean-reverts the z-scored log-spread between two UNIVERSE tickers, so it only has something to extract when that spread is currently pushed to an unusual extreme relative to its own recent history -- entering only past z_entry, flattening back near zero. CURRENT STRUCTURAL CONDITION: the credit-risk premium embedded in HYG (junk) vs LQD (investment grade) is, independently of any performance claim, sitting at a historically stretched LEVEL right now, not merely "working" lately. The ICE BofA US High Yield OAS is 2.75% (271-275bps) as of 12-20 Aug 2026, described by one tracker as sitting in the richest decile of its own history against a long-run median near 450bps (thetrading.tools Credit Spreads Tracker, 20 Aug 2026; Convex BAMLH0A0HYM2, 12 Aug 2026). The HY/IG spread ratio (~3.5x, IG ~81bps, BBB ~100bps) shows this compression is broad-based across the credit stack, not concentrated in the lowest-quality names alone -- i.e. a level condition on the whole credit curve, the two rungs of which HYG and LQD each track. The same tracker independently computes its OWN z-score-style regime read on the HYG/LQD ratio itself (0.7532 at close 17 Aug 2026) and flags it TIGHT, day 30 of that regime -- meaning a source built specifically to watch this ratio's own distribution, not this pipeline, is reporting the ratio as currently stretched from its own norm, which is exactly the precondition pair_zscore needs to have anything to revert. PARAMETER CHOICE: z_window=100 trading days (~5 months) is chosen mid-range so the rolling mean/std the z-score is measured against spans back before the "day 30" tight regime began, so the z-score is measuring the spread against a baseline that still includes normal-regime observations rather than being dominated by the compressed regime itself (a much shorter window would just re-measure the regime against itself and rarely trigger). z_entry=2.25 and z_stop=4.5 (both above this primitive's narrower defaults used in prior proposals) are chosen because the OAS level itself is described as historically rare (richest decile), so a wider net is needed to only trigger on comparably rare z-score extremes, consistent with the level story rather than routine noise. HONESTY CAVEAT: HYG/LQD has been proposed as a pair_zscore candidate three times before in this ledger (z_window/entry/stop of 90/2.0/4.0, 252/2.5/5.0, and 75/2.75/5.5) with no verdict yet in graveyard.csv; this exact (100, 2.25, 4.5) combination has not been tested (checked against pipeline_ideas.csv and graveyard.csv). This proposal does not claim the prior ones were wrong, only that this untested combination is motivated by the specific richest-decile/TIGHT-regime evidence above; the downstream calibration/OOS gates are the actual arbiter, not this rationale.

Citation: thetrading.tools, "Credit Spreads Tracker: HYG/LQD Ratio, Z-Score Regimes & Risk-Off Signal" (HYG/LQD ratio 0.7532 at close 17 Aug 2026, regime TIGHT day 30); Convex, BAMLH0A0HYM2 HY Credit Spread (OAS) 271bps (12 Aug 2026); govspending.org, BAMLH0A0HYM2 High-Yield Credit Spread (OAS) 2.75% (20 Aug 2026); IG ~81bps / BBB ~100bps and HY/IG ratio ~3.5x cited in the same August 2026 credit-spread research sweep.


### `rp_static_spread_carry_SLV_DBC_a` (primitive `static_spread_carry`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-25, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=SLV, ticker_b=DBC, long_leg=a. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: MECHANISM: static_spread_carry is a fixed, always-on long-short -- a structural risk-premium bet, not a mean-reversion or trend signal -- so it only has something to extract when one leg has a persistent, dateable, non-price-based reason to outperform the other, not merely a level or correlation observation. STRUCTURAL CONDITION, NOT A PERFORMANCE CLAIM: silver is entering its SIXTH consecutive annual global supply deficit, with the Silver Institute's World Silver Survey 2026 describing the largest deficit on record (2026 deficit estimates across trackers range from roughly 46 to 215 million oz depending on scrap/recycling assumptions, but all describe the same multi-year shortfall trend, not a single-year print). This is structurally different from an ordinary commodity supply squeeze because the supply side cannot respond quickly: new mine supply takes 5-10 years to bring online, so the deficit is a multi-year state variable rather than a shock that clears on the next price signal. The demand side driving it is also structurally different from a discretionary/investment flow: industrial applications (solar photovoltaics, EVs, electronics) are now roughly 60% of total silver consumption, up from about 50% a decade ago, and are described as compulsory -- a roughly fixed silver loading per solar cell/EV unit -- rather than price-elastic the way discretionary jewelry or coin demand is. DBC, the short leg, is a 14-commodity futures basket (WTI, Brent, heating oil, RBOB gasoline, natural gas, gold, silver, aluminum, zinc, copper, corn, wheat, soybeans, sugar per its own index methodology) that is currently priced overwhelmingly off an UNRELATED shock: the ongoing Strait of Hormuz oil-supply disruption has kept the WTI futures curve in backwardation into August 2026 (front-month meaningfully above deferred contracts), a geopolitical energy story with no connection to silver's own mine-supply economics. Silver is itself only a small weight inside DBC's own basket, so shorting DBC is not 'shorting silver twice' -- it nets out the shared broad-commodity/inflation beta both instruments carry, so the position isolates the silver-specific structural deficit rather than a generic long-commodities bet, which is exactly the always-on structural risk-premium framing this primitive calls for. This is NOT a claim that the SLV/DBC ratio has recently moved or is due to revert -- long_leg='a' (SLV) is a fixed position regardless of where the ratio currently sits, consistent with static_spread_carry's own always-on description. DISTINCTNESS: this exact spec (SLV/DBC, static_spread_carry) has not been proposed before -- checked against pipeline_ideas.csv and graveyard.csv, which already carry SLV/GLD and SLV/USO static_spread_carry proposals (both DEAD -- disclosed here, not as the motivation: this is a different pairing built on a different economic mechanism, silver's own multi-year mine-supply deficit versus broad commodity beta, not silver versus gold's debasement narrative or versus a single energy proxy) but no SLV/DBC pairing exists under any primitive in this ledger. static_spread_carry is also among the less-proposed primitives to date (22 of 127 proposals so far), a secondary reason for this choice, not the motivating one. HONESTY CAVEAT: every static_spread_carry candidate verdicted so far in graveyard.csv is DEAD; this proposal does not claim otherwise, only that the specific mechanism here -- an industrial-demand-driven, multi-year mine-supply-lag deficit, netted against an unrelated broad-commodity/energy shock -- has not itself been screened under this pairing. The downstream calibration/OOS gates are the actual arbiter, not this rationale.

Citation: The Silver Institute, World Silver Survey 2026 (sixth consecutive annual global deficit, described as the largest on record; new mine supply requiring 5-10 years to bring online), as reported in Money Metals Exchange / Advisor Perspectives, 'Silver Market Expected to Run Sixth Straight Supply Deficit This Year' (2026-02-15, https://www.advisorperspectives.com/commentaries/2026/02/15/silver-market-expected-supply-deficit-this-year) and PRNewswire, 'Silver Demand Hits a Sixth Straight Deficit, The Supply Math Doesn't Add Up' (https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/silver-demand-hits-a-sixth-straight-deficit-the-supply-math-doesnt-add-up-302698677.html). Industrial demand roughly 60% of total silver consumption (up from about 50% a decade ago), driven by solar/EV/electronics: Minted Metal, 'Silver Industrial Demand 2026: Solar and EVs' (https://mintedmetal.com/analysis/silver-industrial-demand-2026/). Deficit persistence given the multi-year mine-supply response lag: Discovery Alert, 'Silver Supply Deficit Crisis: What Investors Must Know for 2026' (https://discoveryalert.com.au/silver-supply-demand-imbalance-2026-shortage-scenarios/) and EBC Financial Group, 'Silver Supply Deficit Explained: Why Tight Inventories Could Still Squeeze Prices In 2026' (https://www.ebc.com/forex/silver-supply-deficit-explained-why-tight-inventories-could-still-squeeze-prices-in-2026). DBC's 14-commodity futures-basket composition and index methodology: Invesco DB Commodity Index Tracking Fund product page (https://www.invesco.com/us-rest/contentdetail?contentId=1fd207c649400410VgnVCM10000046f1bf0aRCRD&dnsName=us) and etf.com DBC profile (https://www.etf.com/DBC). Strait of Hormuz-driven WTI backwardation as DBC's current dominant, silver-unrelated energy driver into August 2026: Investing.com, 'Oil Trading in August 2026: Backwardation, Crack Spreads and Hormuz Risk Explained' (https://www.investing.com/analysis/oil-trading-in-august-2026-backwardation-crack-spreads-and-hormuz-risk-explained-200686148).


### `rp_single_asset_trend_IWM_189` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-25, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=IWM, lookback=189. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: MECHANISM: single_asset_trend takes IWM long or short by the sign of its own trailing return over a lookback, so it only has something to extract when a real, currently-unfolding shock reaches IWM's price gradually (in installments) rather than all at once -- a trend rule on a series that reprices instantly on a single data point just churns costs (Hong & Stein 1999 gradual-information-diffusion condition, the same precondition already used in this ledger to motivate the now-DEAD rp_single_asset_trend_VNQ_126 proposal; disclosed, not concealed). CURRENT STRUCTURAL CONDITION, VERIFIED DIRECTLY AGAINST THE FED'S OWN STATEMENT, NOT A PERFORMANCE CLAIM. The FOMC's July 29, 2026 statement (federalreserve.gov) held the federal funds rate at 3.50%-3.75% by a 9-3 vote -- the fifth consecutive meeting on hold -- with three members (Hammack, Kashkari, Logan) dissenting in favor of a 25bp HIKE, and the Committee's own language citing inflation 'elevated relative to the Committee's 2 percent goal, in part reflecting supply shocks that have driven price increases in certain sectors, including energy,' tied to the ongoing Middle East conflict. This is a genuine regime marker, not a recycled headline: an initial, less rigorous read surfaced earlier in this same research session (a secondary source claiming an 85%-odds September cut off the back of '3 cuts since Sept 2025') was corrected against the primary source -- the actual FOMC statement shows the opposite: a Committee that has stopped cutting, is now debating whether to reverse and hike, and names an unresolved supply shock as the reason inflation hasn't returned to target. That is a real shift from 'further easing is coming' to 'higher for longer, possibly still rising,' and it reached the market only as of the July 29 meeting -- under four weeks before this proposal. WHY IWM SPECIFICALLY. Small-cap balance sheets carry disproportionate floating-rate debt exposure -- roughly 32-40% of Russell 2000 constituents' debt floats, versus under 10% for the S&P 500 (Penn Mutual Asset Management chart-of-the-week; corroborated qualitatively by 2026 Russell 2000 outlook coverage). That asymmetry means a 'no further cuts, maybe a hike' repricing lands disproportionately on IWM's own constituents' interest expense relative to the broad-market benchmarks it sits alongside in this UNIVERSE, and the transmission is inherently staggered: floating-rate resets, credit-facility renewals, and next-quarter guidance don't all reprice on the day of one FOMC statement -- they filter through over the following quarters, exactly the gradual-diffusion condition a single-asset trailing-sign rule needs. This is a claim about a mechanism that would generate a persistent OWN-price trend in IWM in WHATEVER direction the regime shift actually resolves to -- the sign rule takes either side -- not a forecast that IWM falls; the position is symmetric. PARAMETER CHOICE: lookback=189 trading days (~9 months) is chosen to span back to roughly December 2025 -- before the run of five consecutive FOMC holds culminating in the July 29, 2026 statement -- so the trailing window captures the market's own gradual repricing from 'cutting cycle continues' to 'on hold, hawkish dissent' as it has actually unfolded meeting-by-meeting, rather than measuring only the latest print in isolation (too short) or diluting the current regime against pre-hold-streak price action (a lookback near the 252 cap). freq=M matches this primitive's own prior rp_single_asset_trend_IWM_168 precedent and the quarterly cadence at which floating-rate costs actually reach small-cap earnings and guidance -- a finer rebalance would only add turnover against an input that updates on FOMC/earnings cycles, not daily. DISTINCTNESS AND HONESTY. single_asset_trend on IWM has one prior verdict in this ledger: rp_single_asset_trend_IWM_168 is DEAD (OOS Sharpe -0.015, graveyard.csv). This exact (IWM, 189) combination is untested (checked against pipeline_ideas.csv and graveyard.csv). This proposal does not claim the prior verdict was wrong -- it wasn't motivated by this Fed-hold/hawkish-dissent mechanism, which only became verifiable as of the July 29, 2026 statement, weeks after that earlier proposal. single_asset_trend and cross_sectional_rank are this ledger's two least-proposed primitives (20 each, before this row, of 6 total); this row is chosen partly to keep the vocabulary's breadth explored over time, but the Fed-statement mechanism above is the actual motivation, not the tally. Every trend-family verdict in this ledger to date -- tsmom_*, donchian_*, green_line_200d, and every single_asset_trend proposal so far -- is DEAD; this proposal does not claim otherwise. SOURCE-QUALITY CAVEAT: the 32-40% floating-rate-debt figure and the quarters-long transmission-lag claim come from secondary market-commentary sources, not a primary balance-sheet dataset -- flagged as the weakest link in this citation, unlike the FOMC statement itself, read directly from federalreserve.gov. The downstream calibration/OOS gates are the actual arbiter, not this rationale.

Citation: Federal Reserve Board, "Federal Reserve issues FOMC statement" (July 29, 2026), https://www.federalreserve.gov/newsevents/pressreleases/monetary20260729a.htm -- target range held at 3.50%-3.75% by 9-3 vote, fifth consecutive hold; Hammack, Kashkari, and Logan dissenting in favor of a 25bp hike; inflation "elevated relative to the Committee's 2 percent goal, in part reflecting supply shocks... including energy." Corroborated by Advisor Perspectives / dshort, "Fed's Interest Rate Decision: July 29, 2026" (https://www.advisorperspectives.com/dshort/updates/2026/07/29/feds-interest-rate-decision-july-29-2026). Russell 2000 floating-rate debt exposure (~32-40% of constituents vs <10% for S&P 500): Penn Mutual Asset Management, "Highly (Interest Rate) Sensitive," Chart of the Week (https://www.pennmutualam.com/market-insights-news/blogs/chart-of-the-week/2024-06-13-highly-interest-rate-sensitive), corroborated by BingX, "Russell 2000 Forecast 2026: Small-Cap Breakout or Floating-Rate Debt Trap?" (https://bingx.com/en/learn/article/russell-2000-rty-outlook-and-price-prediction-is-russell-2000-a-good-investment). Mechanism: Hong & Stein (1999), "A Unified Theory of Underreaction, Momentum Trading, and Overreaction in Asset Markets," Journal of Finance 54(6), 2143-2184.


### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_EFA_IEF_60_45` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-25, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=EFA, ticker_b=IEF, lookback_a=60, lookback_b=45. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: asset_class_trend_hedge combines two INDEPENDENT single-ticker trend legs from different asset classes, so it only has something to extract when the two legs' trailing-return signals are currently driven by genuinely separate, decorrelated forces rather than one shared global-risk factor -- two legs riding the same factor just doubles exposure, not diversification. CURRENT STRUCTURAL CONDITION: monetary policy in the US and the euro area is actively diverging right now, not merely 'different lately.' The ECB raised its deposit rate to 2.25% in June 2026 as euro-area inflation pressure returned, and its own Economic Bulletin (Issue 2, 2026) frames the live question as whether 2.25% holds or rises further in H2 -- while the Fed is on the opposite path: a 9-3 FOMC split and a Treasury buyback pushed DXY to a 9-week low of 98.55 on 2026-08-22 as markets price at least two more 2026 cuts. EFA's trailing return is currently propelled by region-specific fundamentals independent of Fed policy -- euro-area fiscal and defence-spending expansion, a reported ~15% 2026 Eurozone earnings-growth consensus, and crowding-in of private capex around digital/AI investment -- while IEF's trailing return is propelled by the US rate-cut repricing described above. That is the specific mechanism expected to decorrelate the two legs: EFA trends on ECB-hold/hike-era European earnings and fiscal expansion, IEF trends on Fed-cut duration demand, two structurally distinct policy regimes rather than a single shared global rates factor. Lookback_a=60 (~3 months) spans since the June ECB hike that reset the region's policy trajectory; lookback_b=45 (~2 months) is shorter, tuned to the more recent, still-unfolding Fed dovish pivot. Weekly rebalance: both legs' drivers are policy-meeting-paced, not daily-headline-paced.

Citation: European Central Bank, Economic Bulletin Issue 2, 2026, https://www.ecb.europa.eu/press/economic-bulletin/html/eb202602.en.html; Vantage Markets, 'US Dollar Index (DXY) Falls to 98.55 as Yields Ease, Fed Splits' (2026-08-24), https://www.vantagemarkets.com/market-analysis/us-dollar-index-dxy-falls-98-55-jackson-hole-august-24-28-2026/


### `rp_curve_carry_200` (primitive `curve_carry`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-25, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: lookback=200. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: curve_carry trend-follows the REAL FRED 2s10s slope (DGS10-DGS2) with a DV01-neutral TLT/IEF position (steepening -> short TLT/long IEF), so it only has something to extract when the curve's slope is in a genuine, multi-month directional trend, not range-bound chop around a stable level. CURRENT STRUCTURAL CONDITION, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM: the 2s10s spread is in an ongoing, well-documented bear-steepening trend that accelerated through late 2025 into 2026 and remains live and unresolved as of this week -- currently ~51-58bp, up roughly 13.3% over the past 12 months. Two INDEPENDENT, mutually reinforcing forces are driving it, which is what makes this a structural trend rather than one-off noise: (1) the short end is being pulled down by priced Fed rate cuts (the dovish pivot already cited in this ledger's most recent asset_class_trend_hedge EFA/IEF proposal -- DXY at a 9-week low, markets pricing further 2026 cuts), while (2) the long end is being pushed up independently by fiscal and term-premium pressure: the CBO raised its 2026 annual budget deficit projection to $2.1 trillion (+$200bn from its February estimate), and market commentary attributes the curve's un-inversion less to growth optimism than to investors demanding more term premium for absorbing the resulting Treasury issuance. DoubleLine's Bill Campbell frames this explicitly as a still-live, not-yet-exhausted trade -- his published view is that the 2-10 spread could widen another 75-100bp further through the cycle from both forces acting together -- and separate 2026 rates outlooks put the 2s10s at 90-100bp by H2 2026, well above the current level. That the spread is described as 'near its 10-year average but rising' (not already at an extreme) is itself evidence the trend is real and unresolved, exactly the condition curve_carry's trend-following slope signal needs, rather than a level already primed to mean-revert. PARAMETER CHOICE: lookback=200 trading days (~9.5 months) is chosen to span back to roughly the 'late 2025' point repeatedly cited as when this specific steepening episode became pronounced, capturing the full run of the current regime without stretching to the primitive's 252-day cap, which would reach back further than this specific bear-steepener episode's own start and dilute it against whatever the curve was doing beforehand. freq=W because both cited drivers -- FOMC policy expectations and Treasury auction/issuance flow -- move on a multi-week cadence, not daily headline noise; this matches most (15 of 21) prior curve_carry proposals in this ledger, which also chose weekly. DISTINCTNESS: lookback=200 with freq=W has not been proposed before -- checked against all 21 prior curve_carry rows in pipeline_ideas.csv, whose lookbacks span 21-252 with the two nearest neighbors at 189 (monthly) and 210 (weekly), neither equal to 200. HONESTY CAVEAT: curve_carry is tied for this ledger's least-proposed primitive (21 of 133 prior rows, tied with cross_sectional_rank and single_asset_trend, versus 22-24 for the other three) -- a secondary reason for this choice, not the motivating one; the steepening evidence above is the actual motivation. Every curve_carry candidate verdicted in graveyard.csv so far (6 of the 21 proposed, at lookbacks 126/147/189/210/230/252) is DEAD, consistent with every predictive strategy in this lab so far; this proposal does not claim otherwise, only that lookback=200 is untested and this week's steepening evidence is genuinely current, not recycled from an earlier proposal. The downstream calibration/OOS gates are the actual arbiter, not this rationale.

DoubleLine Capital: Fed Rate Cuts Will Further Widen 2/10Y Treasury Spread' (https://www.itiger.com/news/1118571731) -- Fed cuts + fiscal-driven term premium together projected to widen the 2-10 spread a further 75-100bp through the cycle; also PRNewswire, 'DoubleLine Foresees Further Treasury Yield Curve Steepening, Risk of Elevated Term Premium' (https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/doubleline-foresees-further-treasury-yield-curve-steepening-risk-of-elevated-term-premium-302574413.html). ### `rp_static_spread_carry_VNQ_IEF_b` (primitive `static_spread_carry`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-26, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=VNQ, ticker_b=IEF, long_leg=b. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: MECHANISM: static_spread_carry is a fixed, always-on long-short -- a structural risk-premium bet on which leg structurally deserves to be the compensated one, not a timing call. The premium this pair isolates is real estate's compensation over a duration-matched government bond: REIT cap rates trade at a spread over the 10-year Treasury yield to compensate holders for rate sensitivity, vacancy/occupancy risk, capex and illiquidity that a Treasury doesn't carry (Chervachidze & Wheaton 2013, Journal of Real Estate Finance and Economics, document this cap-rate-to-Treasury spread as the standard structural risk-premium framework for commercial real estate). CURRENT STRUCTURAL CONDITION, NOT A PERFORMANCE CLAIM: that structural premium is currently unusually thin. CBRE Investment Management's own cap-rate-spread series puts the broad commercial real estate cap-rate-to-10yr-Treasury spread at 172bp as of Q3 2025 -- the 24th percentile of readings since 1965 and well inside the roughly 150-300bp range CBRE cites as the historical norm. The compression shows up even more starkly leg-by-leg: with the 10-year Treasury yield at 4.63% (92.7th percentile of the past year), a bellwether net-lease REIT's dividend yield spread over the 10-year has narrowed to just 46bp. At this compression, the market is pricing close to zero incremental compensation for real-estate-specific risk over simply holding the duration-matched government bond -- the structural argument for OVER-weighting the real estate leg is currently at its weakest, and the safer, duration-matched leg (IEF, the conventional 10yr-equivalent proxy) is the side of this spread currently better compensated per unit of risk. PARAMETER CHOICE: long_leg='b' (long IEF, short VNQ) takes that side explicitly -- the OPPOSITE direction from this ledger's only other VNQ/IEF static_spread_carry row (rp_static_spread_carry_VNQ_IEF_a, 2026-08-04, long_leg='a', long VNQ/short IEF, the standard 'harvest the REIT premium' direction). freq=M matches every other static_spread_carry proposal in this ledger bar one (23 of 24 prior rows use M), appropriate for a structural, low-turnover position rather than a signal reacting to weekly noise. DISTINCTNESS: VNQ/IEF long_leg='b' has not been proposed before -- the only prior VNQ/IEF row in pipeline_ideas.csv is long_leg='a'. HONESTY CAVEAT: the VNQ/IEF long_leg='a' candidate this reverses has not yet reached a verdict in graveyard.csv (only a pair_zscore VNQ/IEF row exists there, DEAD, a different primitive and mechanism entirely); every predictive strategy tested in this lab so far is DEAD, and this proposal does not claim the reversed direction will fare differently, only that the current cap-rate-spread compression is a genuine, citable structural condition and this specific direction is untested. The downstream calibration/OOS gates are the actual arbiter, not this rationale.

Citation: Chervachidze, S. & Wheaton, W. (2013), 'What Determined the Great Cap Rate Compression of 2000-2007, and the Dramatic Reversal During the 2008-2009 Financial Crisis?', Journal of Real Estate Finance and Economics 46(2) -- establishes the cap-rate-to-Treasury spread as the standard real-estate structural-risk-premium framework. CBRE Investment Management, 'The Case For and Against Narrow Cap Rate Spreads' (https://www.cbreim.com/insights/articles/the-case-for-and-against-narrow-cap-rate-spreads) -- cap-rate-to-10yr-Treasury spread at 172bp as of Q3 2025, 24th percentile since 1965, vs a 150-300bp historical norm. TS2, 'Yield Spread Between Realty Income and Treasuries Narrows to 46 Basis Points on Climbing Treasury Rates' (https://ts2.tech/en/realty-income-stock-yield-spread-shrinks-to-46-basis-points-as-treasuries-rise/) -- 10-year Treasury yield at 4.63% (92.7th percentile of the past year), bellwether REIT dividend yield spread over the 10yr narrowed to 46bp.


### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_IWM_UUP_60_45` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-26, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=IWM, ticker_b=UUP, lookback_a=60, lookback_b=45. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: asset_class_trend_hedge combines two INDEPENDENT single_asset_trend legs (own trailing-return sign, own lookback) from two different asset classes into one candidate -- it captures value when each leg has its own live, current catalyst, not when the two happen to be correlated. ticker_a=IWM (equity) and ticker_b=UUP (currency) satisfy the mechanical different-asset-class requirement. IWM/UUP has not been proposed before -- checked against all 24 prior asset_class_trend_hedge rows in pipeline_ideas.csv, which pair UUP with TLT/VNQ/EEM/HYG(x2)/GLD/USO/DBC(x2)/EFA but never IWM, and pair IWM only once, with TLT (rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_IWM_TLT_105_189, 2026-08-19), a rates leg, not currency. CURRENT STRUCTURAL CONDITION, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM: both legs are live expressions of the SAME ongoing 2026 Fed easing cycle, through two structurally DIFFERENT transmission channels, which is what makes this a genuine two-leg hedge rather than one bet expressed twice. Leg one (IWM): WisdomTree and EBC Financial Group research both document that nearly 40% of Russell 2000 constituents carry floating-rate debt, so Fed cuts flow through to small-cap balance sheets directly and with less lag than to large caps -- the 'small-cap rotation' this drives is explicitly framed as a fixed-vs-floating debt-exposure story, not a beta-to-the-market story. FXStreet (2026-08-13, twelve days before this proposal) reports the Russell 2000 'catching up with the giants' as that rate-cut-driven decoupling continues. Leg two (UUP): the same Fed easing cycle is compressing rate-hike odds and pulling the dollar down through the interest-rate-differential channel -- CME FedWatch's next-meeting hike probability fell from 47% to 35% over the past month, and DXY has fallen from roughly 101.70 in late July 2026 to about 99.50 by mid-August, testing support below its declining 200-day MA (Barchart, 2026-08). These are mechanically distinct channels (corporate floating-rate refinancing cost vs. cross-border rate-differential capital flows) reacting to one shared cause, which is the intended shape for this primitive: not a correlation search, but a checkable claim about WHY two different asset classes both have a live catalyst right now. PARAMETER CHOICE: lookback_a=60 (~3 months) for IWM spans back before the Russell 2000's documented 2026 acceleration phase without reaching into the pre-rate-cut period this specific rotation doesn't apply to. lookback_b=45 (~2 months) for UUP spans slightly earlier than the weak-July-payrolls catalyst repeatedly cited as this dollar downleg's trigger, so the rolling trend signal isn't measured against a window that starts exactly at the event itself. freq=W matches the majority (15 of 24) of prior asset_class_trend_hedge proposals and fits a signal meant to track a multi-week policy-cycle move, not daily noise. HONESTY CAVEAT: of 24 prior asset_class_trend_hedge proposals, 20 have reached a graveyard.csv verdict -- 18 DEAD, 2 ALIVE (rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_SPY_IEF_252_63 and rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_QQQ_GLD_90_60). This proposal is not chasing that result -- IWM/UUP is an unrelated, untested pair and parameter combination, and the base rate across this lab remains overwhelmingly DEAD. The downstream calibration corr-cap guard and OOS gates are the actual arbiter, not this rationale.

Citation: WisdomTree, 'The Small-Cap Rotation Is Focused on Fixed vs. Floating Debt Exposure' (https://www.wisdomtree.com/us/insights/blog/the-small-cap-rotation-is-focused-on-fixed-vs-floating-debt-exposure) -- ~40% of Russell 2000 constituents carry floating-rate debt. EBC Financial Group, 'Russell 2000: Small Caps Steal the Spotlight in 2026's Great Rotation' (https://www.ebc.com/forex/russell-2000-small-caps-great-rotation-2026) -- Fed pivot and late-2025/2026 rate cuts as the driver of the floating-rate-debt tailwind. FXStreet, 'Russell 2000 catches up with the giants' (2026-08-13) (https://www.fxstreet.com/news/russell-2000-catches-up-with-the-giants-202508131435). Barchart, 'DXY Technical Analysis August 2026: US Dollar Index at 99.60 Tests Rising Channel Support Below Declining 200-Day MA' (https://www.barchart.com/story/news/3912243) -- DXY decline from ~101.70 (late July 2026) to ~99.50 (mid-August 2026). CME FedWatch Tool hike-probability history (https://www.cmegroup.com/markets/interest-rates/cme-fedwatch-tool.html) -- next-meeting hike probability fell from 47% to 35% over the trailing month.


### `rp_single_asset_trend_EFA_50` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-26, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=EFA, lookback=50. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: single_asset_trend holds a single ticker long or short by the sign of its own trailing return over a lookback window -- it only has something to extract when that ticker is in a genuine, multi-week directional regime driven by a live catalyst, not chop around a stable level. CURRENT STRUCTURAL CONDITION, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM: this is a DIFFERENT mechanism from every prior EFA proposal in this ledger. The two existing single_asset_trend EFA rows (lookback 126, DEAD in graveyard.csv at OOS Sharpe -0.146 at lookback 252) and the two existing asset_class_trend_hedge rows pairing EFA with UUP and with IEF all rest on the SAME unhedged-FX-translation / dollar-weakness channel. This proposal deliberately does NOT re-run that channel. Instead the catalyst is a distinct, event-dated shock to EFA's LOCAL-CURRENCY equity return itself: the ECB raised its key rate by 25bp to 2.25% on 2026-06-11 -- its first hike since September 2023 -- explicitly citing inflation pressure from the US-Iran war's effect on energy costs, and simultaneously lifted its inflation forecast to average 3% in 2026 (vs a 2% target). That hike landed against an economy already contracting: Eurozone GDP shrank 0.2% quarter-on-quarter in Q1 2026, prompting economists to warn explicitly of stagflation -- a tightening central bank responding to a supply-side (oil) shock while growth is negative is the textbook condition for a genuine equity-valuation regime shift (higher discount rate, weaker growth outlook), not the FX-translation arithmetic the earlier EFA proposals tested. The shock is still live and unresolved as of this week: Middle East energy-supply risk remains elevated (a tanker disabled near Oman, Houthi attacks on shipping in the Red Sea, the US threatening penalties on countries trading with Iran) even as crude has pulled back from an August high near $87/bbl to about $82.5/bbl on intermittent de-escalation signals -- i.e. a genuinely two-sided, actively repricing catalyst, not a level that has already settled. European equities are themselves showing the early, ambiguous stage of a reaction: the STOXX 50 and STOXX 600 both eased on 2026-08-24 (-0.2% and -0.1%) on rising oil prices, higher bond yields and Middle East tension, even as EFA itself sits close to its 52-week high (108.55 on 2026-08-09, 52-week range 89.88-108.76) -- exactly the kind of turning-point tension a trend-sign signal is built to resolve mechanically rather than by forecast. PARAMETER CHOICE: lookback=50 trading days (~10 weeks) is chosen to span back to essentially the ECB's 2026-06-11 hike itself (about 53 trading days before this proposal), so the trailing-return window is measuring price action SINCE the stagflation catalyst began, not diluted by the pre-hike months the existing lookback=126 (DEAD) and lookback=252 EFA rows also span. freq=W matches the pace at which the underlying shock is actually moving -- oil headlines, tanker incidents and ECB commentary are arriving on a weekly cadence, not a single daily print, and this differs from the monthly freq used by every prior EFA row in this ledger. DISTINCTNESS: EFA has been proposed 4 times before in this pipeline (single_asset_trend at 126 and 252, asset_class_trend_hedge paired with UUP and with IEF) -- all four cite the dollar/FX-translation channel; none uses a 50-day lookback or cites the ECB-hike/oil-shock/stagflation mechanism. single_asset_trend is tied for this ledger's least-proposed primitive (22 of 139 prior rows, tied with curve_carry and cross_sectional_rank) -- a secondary reason for this choice, not the motivating one. HONESTY CAVEAT: this lab has killed trend-following broadly (including EFA's own 252-day version, DEAD at OOS Sharpe -0.146) and the direction here is explicitly NOT forecast -- rate hikes can just as easily support a currency and reduce inflation credibility risk as they can compress equity valuations, and EFA's proximity to a 52-week high is itself evidence the market has not yet priced this as a bearish shock. The claim under test is only that a live, event-dated, macro-fundamental catalyst distinct from every prior EFA proposal's channel creates a window where trailing-return sign is worth testing again on this ticker, not that it will finally survive. The downstream calibration/OOS gates are the actual arbiter, not this rationale.

Citation: ECB rate decision and rationale: CNBC, 'ECB hikes interest rates for first time since 2023 as Iran war ramps up energy costs' (2026-06-11, https://www.cnbc.com/2026/06/11/ecb-hikes-interest-rates.html); Euronews, 'ECB raises interest rates for the first time in three years as Iran war fuels inflation' (https://www.euronews.com/business/2026/06/11/ecb-raises-interest-rates-for-the-first-time-in-three-years-as-iran-war-fuels-inflation); Morningstar, 'European Central Bank Raises Interest Rates and Lifts Inflation Forecasts' (https://www.morningstar.com/economy/european-central-bank-raises-interest-rates-lifts-inflation-forecasts) -- 25bp hike to 2.25%, first since September 2023, 2026 inflation forecast raised toward 3%, Eurozone Q1 2026 GDP -0.2% QoQ, stagflation warnings. Oil/Middle East risk context: CNBC, 'Oil prices are little changed after Iran's president indicates Tehran wants war to end soon' (2026-08-21, https://www.cnbc.com/2026/08/21/oil-prices-us-iran.html); Trading Economics and Investing.com coverage of Strait of Hormuz-area tanker incidents and Red Sea Houthi attacks on shipping (2026-06 through 2026-08); crude falling from an August high near $87/bbl (WTI, 2026-08-21) to about $82.5/bbl (2026-08-25) on partial de-escalation signals. European equity reaction: Investing.com, European markets close/summary coverage for the week of 2026-08-24 -- STOXX 50 -0.2% to 6,450, STOXX 600 -0.1%, cited to rising oil prices, higher bond yields and Middle East tension.
