EFA price level and 52-week range (108.55 close 2026-08-09, range 89.88-108.76): Yahoo Finance / Investing.com EFA quote pages. Prior verdict disclosed above (rp_single_asset_trend_EFA_252, DEAD, OOS Sharpe -0.146) is this repo's own graveyard.csv, not an external source.


### `rp_static_spread_carry_GLD_SLV_a` (primitive `static_spread_carry`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-26, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=GLD, ticker_b=SLV, long_leg=a. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: Structural, not a track-record claim -- no assertion is made about how GLD, SLV, or any live book has recently performed; the direction (long GLD / short SLV) is a standing structural-flow bet, not a forecast tuned to a recent chart. THE STRUCTURAL ASYMMETRY: gold and silver are exposed to fundamentally different demand bases, and one of those bases has undergone a real multi-year regime shift while the other has not. Central-bank official-sector gold buying has averaged ~1,000 tonnes/year over the past four years, double the ~500t/year decade-prior average (World Gold Council). 2026 continues this: 244t bought in Q1 2026 alone (Poland and China notable buyers), JPMorgan forecasts ~755t for the full year, and the WGC 2026 Central Bank Gold Reserves Survey (published 2026-06-16) found 45% of surveyed central banks plan to INCREASE gold holdings over the next 12 months while 74% expect the dollar's reserve share to keep declining over five years -- a policy-driven, multi-year reserve-diversification program, not a speculative trade. Silver has no equivalent -- it is not a reserve asset any central bank accumulates; its price is set by industrial demand (solar, electronics) and retail/speculative investment flow, both cyclical and reversible in a way official-sector reserve accumulation is not. WHY THIS FAVORS A STATIC, ALWAYS-ON TILT RATHER THAN A SIGNAL: static_spread_carry makes no claim about short-run direction or timing -- it is paid for bearing the asymmetry that GLD carries a standing, policy-backed buyer of last resort that SLV structurally lacks, the same shape as this lab's one surviving book (crypto funding carry: compensation for a real, persistent structural asymmetry, not a directional forecast). CROSS-CHECK, cited for context only (not as a performance claim driving the choice): the gold/silver price ratio sat at 61.7 on 2026-06-15, below the 50-year historical average of 65-70, and JPMorgan projects it normalizing toward 70 over H2 2026 and toward 75 in 2027 -- consistent with, not the basis for, the demand-asymmetry argument above. Neither this pair nor this direction has been proposed before: the one prior GLD/SLV pipeline proposal (rp_static_spread_carry_SLV_GLD_a, 2026-08-07) is the OPPOSITE leg (long SLV / short GLD); this is a new, distinct spec.

Citation: World Gold Council, Central Bank Gold Reserves Survey 2026 (published 2026-06-16), https://www.gold.org -- 45% of respondents plan to increase gold holdings over the next 12 months, 74% expect declining USD reserve share over five years; J.P. Morgan Global Commodities Research, 2026 gold/silver forecast -- ~755t 2026 central-bank gold purchases, gold/silver ratio normalizing toward 70 (H2 2026) and 75 (2027) from 61.7 on 2026-06-15; De-Dollarization 2.0: Analyzing the 2026 Central Bank Surge in Gold Accumulation, US Gold Bureau, https://www.usgoldbureau.com/news/post/de-dollarization-2.0 -- 244t central-bank gold purchases in Q1 2026, ~1,000t/year 4-year average vs ~500t/year decade-prior average.


### `rp_curve_carry_150` (primitive `curve_carry`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-26, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: lookback=150. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: curve_carry holds a DV01-neutral TLT/IEF position whose direction trend-follows the real FRED 10Y-2Y slope (DGS10-DGS2) over a lookback window -- steepening triggers short TLT/long IEF, flattening the reverse, sized so the two legs duration exposure roughly offsets. CURRENT STRUCTURAL CONDITION (checked live, not backfit): as of Aug 20 2026 the 10Y-2Y spread sits at roughly +0.46 to +0.51 percentage points -- positive throughout all of August 2026, up about 13.3% over the trailing 12 months, and near its own 10-year average -- continuing the gradual post-inversion steepening that began when the curve un-inverted in September 2024 after its 2022-2024 inversion (the longest since the 1970s). This is a multi-quarter REGIME-LEVEL fact about the shape of the curve itself (still actively steepening, not yet plateaued), not a claim about how any strategy has recently performed -- the trend-following mechanism this primitive implements is exactly what a continuing, gradual steepening move is suited to catch. A 150-trading-day (~7-month) lookback is chosen to span roughly two Fed-meeting cycles, long enough to filter the week-to-week noise in DGS10-DGS2 while still reacting within the current multi-quarter steepening move rather than needing a full year to turn.

Citation: FRED T10Y2Y series (10-Year Treasury Constant Maturity Minus 2-Year Treasury Constant Maturity), fred.stlouisfed.org/series/T10Y2Y, and centralbank.watch US yield-curve tracker (2s10s spread / recession-probability tool), both queried for August 2026 levels.


### `rp_pair_zscore_HYG_LQD_110_2p75_5p5` (primitive `pair_zscore`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-26, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=HYG, ticker_b=LQD, z_window=110, z_entry=2.75, z_stop=5.5. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: US high-yield credit spreads are sitting at a historically stretched extreme: the ICE BofA HY OAS was 275bps on 2026-08-20 and iShares HYG OAS 244.10bps on 2026-08-24, both in the richest (tightest) decile of the post-2008 range versus a long-run median near 450bps, and the HYG/LQD credit ratio itself was +3.07 sigma vs its own 1-year history (+2.44 sigma vs 3-year) as of 2026-08-17 -- a TIGHT-regime reading, not a directional call. Two concrete catalysts sit ahead of any further compression: the heaviest post-summer HY issuance calendar of the year lands in September, a supply test against spreads priced near perfection, and energy names are roughly a tenth of the HY index at a moment Brent is near $91 on Middle East supply risk -- an oil reversal would hit that slice of the index directly. This is a valuation-extreme argument for mean reversion in the HYG/LQD spread, not a claim that credit-spread trading has recently been profitable: pair_zscore is exactly the primitive built to harvest a z-scored spread reverting off a stretched level, long-the-spread when it compresses further than typical and short when it snaps back wide, flat near the mean -- HYG/LQD is a natural fit since HYG carries the credit-spread risk LQD mostly does not, so their ratio is close to a pure credit-risk-premium gauge.

Citation: Convex HY Credit Spread (OAS) tracker: 275bps as of 2026-08-20 (convextrade.com/metrics/bamlh0a0hym2); thetrading.tools Credit Spreads Tracker: HYG/LQD ratio 0.7532, +3.07 sigma vs 1-year history / +2.44 sigma vs 3-year history, TIGHT regime as of 2026-08-17 (thetrading.tools/credit-spreads); iShares HYG fund page, OAS 244.10bps as of 2026-08-24 (ishares.com/us/products/239565/ishares-iboxx-high-yield-corporate-bond-etf).


### `rp_pair_zscore_GLD_SLV_25_2p25_4p5` (primitive `pair_zscore`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-27, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=GLD, ticker_b=SLV, z_window=25, z_entry=2.25, z_stop=4.5. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: pair_zscore mean-reverts the z-scored log-spread of GLD vs SLV -- long-the-spread (long GLD / short SLV) when the spread is unusually low, short-the-spread when unusually high, flat near the mean. It bets that once the spread's rolling z-score hits an extreme, the two legs' short-run divergence normalizes back toward the window's own recent equilibrium -- it does not require forecasting which metal outperforms over the long run, only that the spread oscillates around a level rather than moving in one direction without bound. STRUCTURAL CONDITION, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM: the gold/silver ratio underwent a genuine, dated regime shift and has since settled into a new, tighter band rather than continuing to trend. USAGOLD's daily precious-metals report for 2026-08-04 records the ratio 'compressing below 69' as silver surged 2.5% to $59; by 2026-08-25 the same source has it 'near 68.2,' essentially unchanged three weeks later, with gold at $4,627.36 and silver at $67.82. That is a level, not a trend: after an initial multi-week compression the ratio has been oscillating in a narrow sub-69 band for ~3 weeks rather than continuing to fall. The structural driver behind the initial move is the Silver Institute's 2026 World Silver Survey deficit figure (46.3M oz this year, ~762M oz cumulative since 2021, via goldsilver.com's 'Why Is Silver Outperforming Gold? A 6-Year Deficit') colliding with gold's own historic move -- gold is on pace for its strongest monthly gain since September 1999 (usagold.com, 2026-08-25 report). Two large, differently-timed shocks (a slow-building industrial supply deficit vs. a fast, macro-driven gold rally) landing on the same ratio in the same month is exactly the kind of setup where the spread's DAILY path stays noisy and mean-reverting around its new settled level even while the level itself has shifted -- the two legs are not moving in lockstep, they are each getting pushed by a different, dateable force, which is what keeps a log-spread oscillating rather than trending. PARAMETER CHOICE: z_window=25 trading days (~5 weeks) reaches back to roughly 2026-07-21, just before the 2026-08-04 compression-below-69 print, so the rolling mean the z-score is measured against is anchored to the CURRENT settled regime rather than diluted by the older, higher-ratio months this specific episode does not include. z_entry=2.25 and z_stop=4.5 sit at the wider end of this ledger's typical range (vs. e.g. 2.0/4.0 elsewhere) to account for the still-elevated realized volatility from gold's record monthly move -- a tighter entry would risk over-triggering on ordinary noise from that volatility rather than genuine spread extremes. freq=W matches the roughly-weekly cadence at which the cited trackers themselves update (USAGOLD's own report cadence is daily-to-weekly summaries), avoiding both intraday/daily noise and a monthly lag that would miss the ratio's now-narrow oscillation band entirely. DISTINCTNESS: GLD/SLV has been proposed once before as pair_zscore (2026-08-05, window=60/entry=2.0/stop=4.0 -- a materially different window spanning back before this episode even began) and once as static_spread_carry (2026-08-25, verdicted DEAD in graveyard.csv as of this run) -- a fixed directional bet on the ratio's level, a different mechanism from this primitive's spread-oscillation bet, so that verdict does not speak to this proposal. This specific (25, 2.25, 4.5) combination is new. pair_zscore has otherwise concentrated 5 of its 24 prior proposals on a single pair (HYG/LQD, three already DEAD in graveyard.csv) -- this proposal deliberately does not add a sixth HYG/LQD variant, choosing a different, comparably-motivated pair instead so the primitive's exploration doesn't collapse onto one ticker pair. HONESTY CAVEAT: this lab's other GLD/SLV proposal (static_spread_carry, a directional bet) was just verdicted DEAD; this proposal does not dispute or lean on that result, since it tests a different mechanism (spread oscillation, not a fixed directional stance) on the same pair. Whether the ratio's new sub-69 band is itself stable enough for mean-reversion to have paid off -- as opposed to still drifting -- is exactly what the downstream DSR/CPCV gate decides, not this rationale.

Citation: Ratio level and gold/silver spot prices: USAGOLD, 'Physical Gold Eases From A Three-Month High As Silver Slips Below $68 Ahead Of PCE' (2026-08-25, https://www.usagold.com/daily-precious-metals-market-report-august-25-2026/). Initial compression print: USAGOLD, 'Silver Surges 2.5% To $59 As The Gold-Silver Ratio Compresses Below 69; Physical Gold Holds $4,060' (2026-08-04, https://www.usagold.com/daily-precious-metals-market-report-august-4-2026/). Structural silver deficit: goldsilver.com, 'Why Is Silver Outperforming Gold? A 6-Year Deficit' (https://goldsilver.com/industry-news/goldsilver-news/why-is-silver-outperforming-gold-2026/), citing the Silver Institute's 2026 World Silver Survey (46.3M oz 2026 deficit, ~762M oz cumulative since 2021). Gold's historic monthly move: USAGOLD 2026-08-25 report (strongest monthly gain since September 1999). Prior GLD/SLV proposals and the static_spread_carry DEAD verdict, and the HYG/LQD proposal-count concentration, are this repo's own pipeline_ideas.csv and graveyard.csv, not external sources.


### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_SLV_TLT_168_65` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-27, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=SLV, ticker_b=TLT, lookback_a=168, lookback_b=65. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: asset_class_trend_hedge holds two independent single_asset_trend legs (own trailing-return sign, own lookback) in one candidate. ticker_a=SLV (commodity) and ticker_b=TLT (rates) come from different ASSET_CLASS buckets, satisfying the primitive's mechanical requirement. The citation must name a SPECIFIC mechanism expected to make the two legs' trend signals decorrelate, not just an assertion of low correlation -- here it is that each leg's trend is CURRENTLY dominated by a distinct, largely orthogonal driver, not a shared macro factor. SLV LEG -- IDIOSYNCRATIC PHYSICAL-MARKET DRIVER: the Silver Institute's World Silver Survey 2026 puts the global silver market in its SIXTH consecutive annual supply deficit, widening to 46.3 Moz in 2026 from 40.3 Moz in 2025 -- and, critically, this widening is happening DESPITE falling industrial demand: silver usage in photovoltaics is projected to drop 19% in 2026 (~151 Moz) on thrifting/substitution, with total industrial fabrication down 2% to a four-year low (~650 Moz). A deficit that widens even as its largest industrial demand component shrinks means the binding constraint is the SUPPLY side (mine output response takes 5-10 years), not a demand or macro shock -- so silver's price trend right now is plausibly set more by this multi-year physical shortfall than by the rate/dollar cycle that normally links commodities to macro. TLT LEG -- FISCAL/TERM-PREMIUM DRIVER, NOT A CREDIT OR GROWTH SHOCK: the long end of the Treasury curve has been in a pronounced bear steepening, the 30-year hitting 5.33% in the week of August 24, 2026, which multiple sources attribute specifically to elevated term premium from federal debt (now >$40 trillion) and heavy ongoing Treasury issuance that the market must absorb, not to a growth or recession repricing. That is a fiscal-supply mechanism specific to long-duration government debt, structurally unrelated to a commodity's physical supply/demand balance. Because the two legs' current trends trace to two different, non-overlapping causal chains (mine-supply economics vs. sovereign-debt-supply economics), this is a reasoned basis to expect their trend SIGNALS to run largely independently over the calibration window's own history, which is what the mechanical decorrelation guard actually tests -- this citation only argues why the pairing is a reasonable one to test now, not that it must pass. PARAMETER CHOICE: lookback_a=168 (~8 months) for SLV to smooth over the multi-quarter, supply-driven deficit narrative rather than react to short-term PV-demand headlines. lookback_b=65 (~3 months) for TLT to capture the recent intensification of the bear-steepening move (described as accelerating specifically in the week of Aug 24, 2026) rather than the multi-year term-premium rise since 2020 lows, which a much longer window would instead track. freq=W matches the weekly cadence at which both the silver-deficit and Treasury-supply commentary cited here is itself being updated -- avoiding daily noise on two macro-narrative-driven legs while not going stale at monthly. DISTINCTNESS: this SLV/TLT pairing has not been proposed before in this ledger's asset_class_trend_hedge rows (checked against pipeline_ideas.csv), and neither ticker's current pairing partner here has been tried together previously. asset_class_trend_hedge (25 prior uses) and the five other primitives are close in proposal count (23-26 each as of this run) -- none heavily under-represented -- so this choice is driven by today's research fit, not a coverage gap.

Citation: Silver deficit: Silver Institute, 'Global Silver Investment to Remain Strong in 2026 Against the Backdrop of a Sixth Consecutive Annual Market Deficit' (https://silverinstitute.org/global-silver-investment-to-remain-strong-in-2026-against-the-backdrop-of-a-sixth-consecutive-annual-market-deficit/); Carbon Credits, 'Silver in 2026 and Beyond: Rising Prices, Solar Substitution, and a Market Still in Deficit' (https://carboncredits.com/silver-in-2026-and-beyond-rising-prices-solar-substitution-and-a-market-still-in-deficit/); Discovery Alert, 'Silver Market Deficit Widens in 2026 Despite Falling Solar Demand' (https://discoveryalert.com.au/silver-market-deficit-falling-solar-demand-2026/). Treasury bear steepener / term premium: SWBC, 'Market Commentary: Week of August 24, 2026' (https://blog.swbc.com/investmenthub/market-commentary-week-of-august-24-2026?hs_amp=true); Goldman Sachs, 'How Inflation and Fiscal Policy Are Driving US Treasury Markets' (https://www.goldmansachs.com/insights/the-markets/how-inflation-and-fiscal-policy-are-driving-us-treasury-markets); Empower, 'Between the margins August 2026' (https://www.empower.com/investment-insights/research/between-margins-august-2026). Prior asset_class_trend_hedge pairings and primitive proposal tally are this repo's own pipeline_ideas.csv, not an external source.


### `rp_pair_zscore_VNQ_TLT_70_2p0_4p0` (primitive `pair_zscore`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-27, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=VNQ, ticker_b=TLT, z_window=70, z_entry=2.0, z_stop=4.0. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: pair_zscore mean-reverts the z-scored log-spread of VNQ vs TLT -- long-the-spread (long VNQ / short TLT) when the spread is unusually low, short-the-spread when unusually high, flat near the mean. It bets on a specific short-run dislocation between REIT pricing and the long-duration Treasury yield that REIT valuations are supposed to discount against, not on which of the two ultimately outperforms in the long run. STRUCTURAL CONDITION, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM: REIT valuation is conventionally anchored to the long end of the curve -- cap rates and dividend-discount models price real estate cash flows against a long-duration risk-free reference rate, and REITs have historically traded at a yield PREMIUM to long Treasuries to compensate for illiquidity/equity risk. That premium has now inverted: VNQ's dividend yield was 3.46% as of 2026-08-17 (US News Money/Kavout) while the 30-year Treasury yield hit 5.33% in the week of 2026-08-24 (SWBC Market Commentary), a roughly 187bp NEGATIVE gap -- REITs yielding meaningfully less than the long bond they are supposed to price at a premium over. This coincides with two dateable, opposite-signed developments: VNQ has rallied roughly 19% in 2026 (AOL/Nasdaq REIT coverage, both partly attributing the move to anticipated Fed rate cuts drawing income investors back to REITs), while the long end has simultaneously been in a bear steepener specifically attributed to rising term premium from federal debt >$40 trillion and heavy Treasury issuance (SWBC; Goldman Sachs, 'How Inflation and Fiscal Policy Are Driving US Treasury Markets') -- a fiscal-supply move, not a growth/recession repricing. REIT pricing appears to be extrapolating declining long rates that the actual long end has not delivered this year; pair_zscore does not require calling which side gives way, only that this VNQ/TLT spread is presently stretched relative to its own recent range and has some tendency to oscillate back toward it. PARAMETER CHOICE: z_window=70 trading days (~3.5 months) reaches back to roughly when the bear-steepening move intensified (spring/summer 2026, the same episode window used by this ledger's other recent TLT-based proposal), so the rolling mean is anchored to the current regime rather than diluted by the earlier, differently-driven part of the year. z_entry=2.0/z_stop=4.0 are this ledger's standard, previously-effective range for a real-estate/rates pair rather than an aggressive outlier. freq=W matches the weekly cadence of the REIT-flow and Treasury-term-premium commentary cited here. DISTINCTNESS: VNQ/TLT has never been proposed as pair_zscore before (checked against pipeline_ideas.csv); it has been tried once before under asset_class_trend_hedge (rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_VNQ_TLT_189_63, verdicted DEAD) -- a wholly different mechanism (two independent trend legs, not a spread-mean-reversion bet), so that verdict does not speak to this proposal. pair_zscore itself is this ledger's least-proposed primitive (25 of 149 prior rows as of this run) among a fairly even 23-26 spread across all six primitives -- a secondary reason for the choice, not the motivating one. HONESTY CAVEAT: every VNQ-involving proposal in this pipeline so far has verdicted DEAD (7 of 7: single_asset_trend, three asset_class_trend_hedge pairings, three pair_zscore pairings), and I could not find a report that separates the YTD REIT rally into a rate-cut-hope component versus other drivers (sector fundamentals, supply/demand for space), so the 'REITs are pricing in cuts the long end hasn't delivered' framing is my own inference from the two facts (the yield-gap inversion and the sources' own rate-cut-hope framing of the rally), not a single source stating it directly. The downstream calibration/DSR/CPCV gates are the actual arbiter, not this rationale or VNQ's prior 0-for-7 record, which this proposal does not dispute.

Citation: VNQ price/yield: US News Money, 'Vanguard Real Estate ETF (VNQ)' (https://money.usnews.com/funds/etfs/real-estate/vanguard-real-estate-etf/vnq); Kavout, 'VNQ and Leading REIT ETFs: In-Depth Analysis for Diversified Real Estate Investment' (https://www.kavout.com/market-lens/vnq-and-leading-reit-etfs-in-depth-analysis-for-diversified-real-estate-investment), both reporting VNQ at 98.07 with a 3.46% dividend yield as of 2026-08-17. REIT 2026 rally: AOL, 'REITs Are Quietly Up ~19%, This 7.7% Monthly Payer Owns the Highest-Yielding Ones' (https://www.aol.com/articles/reits-quietly-19-7-7-193624000.html); Nasdaq, '3 REIT ETFs Could Be Red-Hot in 2026' (https://www.nasdaq.com/articles/3-reit-etfs-could-be-red-hot-2026), both linking the rally partly to anticipated/actual Fed rate cuts. 30-year Treasury level and bear-steepener/term-premium/fiscal-issuance driver: SWBC, 'Market Commentary: Week of August 24, 2026' (https://blog.swbc.com/investmenthub/market-commentary-week-of-august-24-2026?hs_amp=true); Goldman Sachs, 'How Inflation and Fiscal Policy Are Driving US Treasury Markets' (https://www.goldmansachs.com/insights/the-markets/how-inflation-and-fiscal-policy-are-driving-us-treasury-markets). Prior VNQ/TLT asset_class_trend_hedge proposal and verdict, VNQ's full 0-for-7 pipeline record, and pair_zscore's proposal count are this repo's own pipeline_ideas.csv and graveyard.csv, not external sources.


### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_QQQ_LQD_126_63` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-27, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=QQQ, ticker_b=LQD, lookback_a=126, lookback_b=63. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: asset_class_trend_hedge holds two independent single_asset_trend legs (own trailing-return sign, own lookback) in one candidate -- it does not bet on either asset's direction, only that each leg's own trend, taken on its own asset class, is informative right now, with the two classes required (and mechanically checked against 2007-2017 calibration data) to be different. STRUCTURAL CONDITION, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM: this is not proposed because tech stocks or IG credit 'have been working' -- it is proposed because a single, dateable shock (the hyperscalers' AI-capex debt binge) is currently pushing QQQ's own equity-class trend and LQD's own rates/credit-class trend through two distinct, opposite-signed transmission channels, which is exactly the kind of shared-origin, different-channel setup this primitive is built to combine. Roughly a third of 2026 AI capex is debt-financed (Goldman Sachs estimate, cited in Introl's 2026 hyperscaler capex piece), and hyperscaler/AI-adjacent issuers (Nvidia included) had issued ~$225B in US corporate bonds by mid-2026, a 973.7% jump versus the same point a year earlier (MLQ News, citing CreditSights); Forbes put cumulative AI-related debt on track toward $570B by mid/late-2026, and Fortune's 2026-07-31 piece describes 'hidden borrowing' (off-balance-sheet SPV/JV financing on top of the disclosed bonds) reaching $1.65T. The credit-side channel is now visibly under strain, not merely large: MLQ/CreditSights report spreads on hyperscaler bonds maturing beyond 20 years widened to 118bp over Treasurys (from tighter 2025 levels), and CNBC's 2026-02-23 piece frames this as the market starting to price hyperscalers -- previously treated as near-risk-free, reliable-cash-flow credits -- more like ordinary leveraged issuers as the debt load compounds; this is IG-market-wide term-premium and spread pressure, not one issuer's idiosyncratic story, so it plausibly shows up in LQD's own trailing return, not just in single-name spreads. The equity-side channel is the mirror image: the same capex that is straining credit investors' patience is the capex equity investors are paying up for as a growth driver, so QQQ's own trend reflects the market's currently more sanguine read on the same spending. Two different investor bases pricing the same underlying cash outlay through two different instruments, in opposite sentiment directions, is a real, checkable mechanism for why the two legs' trends should not simply move together -- exactly the citation asset_class_trend_hedge's own description requires, and the calibration-window decorrelation guard checks this mechanically regardless of how the citation reads. PARAMETER CHOICE: lookback_a=126 (~6 months) for QQQ spans roughly the period this AI-capex financing acceleration has been building through 2026 (the CreditSights/MLQ figures are 'through midyear' 2026 comparisons), so QQQ's trend sign reflects the market's read on the buildout over the same window the debt financing itself ramped. lookback_b=63 (~3 months) for LQD is shorter and deliberately more recent, matching the sharper, more acute framing of the credit-strain evidence (Fortune's 'can't-last-forever' piece and CNBC's market-pricing-shift framing are both mid/late-2026 dated, i.e. within the last quarter), since the spread-widening/fatigue signal is described as a recent inflection, not a six-month-old one. freq=W matches this pipeline's typical cadence for cross-asset-class hedge proposals. DISTINCTNESS: checked pipeline_ideas.csv and graveyard.csv -- no prior QQQ/LQD proposal under any primitive. QQQ has one prior asset_class_trend_hedge pairing (GLD/QQQ, unrelated commodity-vs-equity mechanism) and LQD has several prior static_spread_carry/pair_zscore pairings (HYG/LQD, LQD/IEF) that are about credit spread level or duration, not this specific AI-debt-issuance channel -- none argue the mechanism used here. HONESTY CAVEAT: I did not find a single source that explicitly nets QQQ's AI-capex enthusiasm against LQD's AI-debt-issuance strain as one trade; that pairing is my own synthesis of two separately-reported sides of the same financing story, not a claim any cited source makes directly. The calibration decorrelation guard and the downstream DSR/CPCV gates are the actual arbiters, not this rationale.

Citation: Introl Blog, 'The $600B AI Infrastructure Buildout' (hyperscaler capex 2026 estimates, ~1/3 debt-financed per Goldman Sachs) https://introl.com/blog/hyperscaler-capex-600b-ai-infrastructure-debt-financing-2026; MLQ News, 'Hyperscaler bond issuance reaches about $220 billion as AI financing costs rise' (citing CreditSights spread data, ~$225B issued through mid-2026, 973.7% YoY jump, 20yr+ spreads to 118bp) https://mlq.ai/news/hyperscaler-bond-issuance-reaches-about-220-billion-as-ai-financing-costs-rise/; Forbes, 'Bond Investors Push Back As AI Debt Heads Toward $570 Billion' (2026-07-17) https://www.forbes.com/sites/robertszczerba/2026/07/17/bond-investors-push-back-as-ai-debt-heads-toward-570-billion/; Fortune, 'After a nearly 1,000% surge, the AI debt orgy can't last forever, while hidden borrowing has exploded to $1.65 trillion' (2026-07-31) https://fortune.com/2026/07/31/ai-debt-hypescalers-capex-capital-spending-hidden-borrowing-bond-issuance/; CNBC, 'How the AI debt binge shattered hyperscalers' unspoken contract with investors' (2026-02-23) https://www.cnbc.com/2026/02/23/big-techs-ai-bond-binge-shatters-unspoken-contract-with-investors.html


### `rp_pair_zscore_HYG_IWM_90_2p25_4p5` (primitive `pair_zscore`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-27, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=HYG, ticker_b=IWM, z_window=90, z_entry=2.25, z_stop=4.5. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: pair_zscore mean-reverts the z-scored log-spread between two tickers, so it only has something to extract when the two legs share a real common driver whose relative pricing occasionally overstretches and snaps back -- not a directional forecast of either leg. HYG and IWM are both, structurally, claims on the same population of smaller, more leveraged, lower-credit-quality US issuers: HYG holds their bonds directly, and Russell 2000 constituents are disproportionately the same kind of borrower (thin balance sheets, heavy reliance on external financing), so the two have historically compressed and widened together through risk-on/risk-off cycles. CURRENT STRUCTURAL DIVERGENCE, not a performance claim about either instrument: as of 20 Aug 2026 the ICE BofA US High Yield OAS sits at 275bp, in the richest (most compressed) decile of its own history against a ~450bp long-run median, and separately closed at 2.84% on 30 Jul 2026, only 21bp off its 2026 tight -- credit spreads are pricing near-zero incremental default risk for exactly this borrower population (Convex, BAMLH0A0HYM2; Finexus Research, 'Credit Spreads Hit Single-Digit Percentiles'). At the same time, the equity side of that same borrower population faces a concrete, dated stress event that has nothing to do with any trading strategy's track record: Russell 2000 constituents carry roughly 40% floating-rate debt against under 10% for the S&P 500, and face a reported 68B debt maturity wall in 2026 that forces refinancing near 6.5% against 1-2% coupons issued years earlier, with an estimated 41-46% of index constituents classified as 'zombie companies' unable to cover interest expense from operating income (BingX Research, 'Russell 2000 Forecast 2026: Small-Cap Breakout or Floating-Rate Debt Trap?'). That is two markets pricing the same underlying credit-risk-bearing issuer base and currently disagreeing sharply: the bond side (HYG) says risk is at a historic low, the equity side's own known refinancing calendar (which flows through to IWM) says a real, scheduled stress event is imminent. The pre-registered claim is narrow and mechanical, not a bet on which side is 'right': when this pair's relative pricing has stretched this far apart historically, it has tended to close as one side catches up to the other, and z_window=90 (~4.5 months) is chosen to span roughly one full quarter of the 2026 maturity-wall refinancing cycle without being so long it also averages in the prior, differently-driven regime. freq=W keeps rebalancing matched to how slowly this divergence itself is likely to close, since neither the credit-spread cycle nor a corporate maturity wall reprices within days. HONEST CASE AGAINST: this lab has tested LQD/HYG under pair_zscore three times (name-collision-free variants at windows 75/90/100/110/252), all DEAD -- the credit-basis mean-reversion mechanism specifically has already failed within the rates asset class. HYG/IWM is a genuinely different, previously untested pairing (cross rates/equity, not rates/rates) built on the same divergence logic, but the prior itself should be DEAD given this lab's own record on adjacent constructions. DISTINCTNESS: no prior pipeline_ideas.csv row or graveyard.csv row pairs HYG with IWM under any primitive; HYG has been tested in 8 prior pipeline rows (mostly against LQD, GLD, UUP, TLT, USO, SPY, IEF) and IWM in 8 (mostly single_asset_trend, IWM/SPY, IWM/TLT, IWM/UUP), but never against each other.

Citation: Convex, 'HY Credit Spread (OAS): 275 bps (Aug 20, 2026)', https://convextrade.com/metrics/bamlh0a0hym2 (sourced from FRED BAMLH0A0HYM2); Finexus Research, 'Credit Spreads Hit Single-Digit Percentiles as High Yield Tightens to 285 Basis Points', https://api.finexus.net/api/news/economic/33623333-2600-44b6-b11d-853c64af94e6/html; BingX Research, 'Russell 2000 Forecast 2026: Small-Cap Breakout or Floating-Rate Debt Trap?', https://bingx.com/en/learn/article/russell-2000-rty-outlook-and-price-prediction-is-russell-2000-a-good-investment (40% floating-rate debt vs S&P 500's under 10%, $368B 2026 maturity wall, refinancing near 6.5% vs 1-2% prior coupons, 41-46% zombie-company estimate).


### `rp_cross_sectional_rank_low_vol_100_3` (primitive `cross_sectional_rank`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-27, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: metric=low_vol, lookback=100, k=3. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: STRUCTURAL, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM: this is a bet on the cross-sectional DISPERSION of realized volatility itself, not on any asset's historical return. Two independent, current pieces of evidence describe an unusually TWO-TIERED volatility regime across this UNIVERSE right now. (1) Broad rates/credit vol is deeply compressed: the ICE BofA MOVE index for Treasury vol sits at 74.7 (a dormant reading) alongside a Fed funds rate of 3.63% (mid-cutting-cycle) and a 2s10s curve that has re-steepened to +35bp, while credit is priced for calm to an extreme -- the HYG/LQD credit ratio closed 2026-08-17 at 0.7532, +3.07 standard deviations above its own trailing 1-year mean, its 30th consecutive day in a 'TIGHT' regime, with the ICE BofA HY OAS at 275bp (2026-08-20) against a long-run median near 450bp. (2) Simultaneously, energy-linked commodity vol is NOT compressed: the WTI futures curve has been in steep backwardation since the war between the US, Israel and Iran broke out on 2026-02-28, persisting even after the 2026-04-07 ceasefire, with December-2026 WTI priced as much as $40/bbl below near-month contracts (spot mid-$80s) -- a live, unresolved Strait-of-Hormuz supply-disruption premium keeping realized/implied vol in the energy complex elevated while nearly every other sleeve of this UNIVERSE (rates, credit, broad equity) sits in a historically calm regime. cross_sectional_rank's low_vol metric ranks the FULL UNIVERSE by trailing realized volatility, long the calmest K names and short the most turbulent K -- exactly the axis this two-tiered regime is splitting the UNIVERSE along right now (a rates/credit calm regime vs. an energy-geopolitical-shock regime), a structural dispersion story, not a claim that any of these instruments has recently outperformed. lookback=100 (~5 trading months) is chosen to span from shortly after the 2026-04-07 ceasefire through today -- the window over which the energy-complex vol elevation has specifically persisted DESPITE the ceasefire, rather than capturing only the initial war-outbreak spike itself. k=3 (of the UNIVERSE's 15 tickers) matches this primitive's existing precedent scale, and freq=W matches a slow-moving volatility-regime signal without paying daily turnover on noise. This exact combination (low_vol, lookback=100, k=3) has never been logged under this primitive before -- prior low_vol rows used lookback/k of 126/5, 252/2, 45/3, 63/3 and 84/3 only.

Citation: Credit regime: thetrading.tools, 'Credit Spreads Tracker: HYG/LQD Ratio, Z-Score Regimes & Risk-Off Signal' (https://www.thetrading.tools/credit-spreads) -- HYG/LQD=0.7532 at close 2026-08-17, +3.07 sigma vs 1-year history, TIGHT regime day 30, Fed funds 3.63%, 2s10s +35bp, MOVE 74.7; Convex, 'HY Credit Spread (OAS): 275 bps (Aug 20, 2026)' (https://convextrade.com/metrics/bamlh0a0hym2). Oil futures curve backwardation: Investing.com, 'Oil Trading in August 2026: Backwardation, Crack Spreads and Hormuz Risk Explained' (https://www.investing.com/analysis/oil-trading-in-august-2026-backwardation-crack-spreads-and-hormuz-risk-explained-200686148); CME Group, 'Implications of WTI Oil Futures In Backwardation Amid the Supply Crunch' (https://www.cmegroup.com/insights/economic-research/2026/implications-of-wti-oil-futures-in-backwardation-amid-the-supply-crunch.html) -- December-2026 WTI futures priced up to $40/bbl below near-month contracts amid the conflict that began 2026-02-28 and persisting past the 2026-04-07 ceasefire.


### `rp_cross_sectional_rank_momentum_210_3` (primitive `cross_sectional_rank`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-27, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: metric=momentum, lookback=210, k=3. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: cross_sectional_rank(metric=momentum, lookback=210, k=3) ranks the full UNIVERSE by ~10-month trailing return, long the top-3 / short the bottom-3 -- a bet that 2026's structural policy-divergence regime is producing genuine, multi-quarter cross-asset trends, not noise a shorter or mean-reverting lookback would misread. MECHANISM: cross-sectional momentum only profits when relative outperformance persists, which itself requires a driver operating on a multi-quarter clock rather than daily noise. 2026's defining macro condition is exactly that kind of driver: major central banks are on genuinely divergent paths this cycle -- the Fed has held 3.50-3.75% for five straight FOMC meetings since its Sept/Oct/Dec-2025 cuts but is priced for ~100bp more cuts through 2028, while the ECB looks inclined to hold through 2026 and the BOE is on a separate gradual-easing track (JPMorgan Asset Management, 'Policy divergence reshapes the front end: Implications for Global Liquidity in 2026'; BlackRock, '2026 global macro outlook: Patience', both naming divergence/dispersion as the year's defining condition because it reallocates capital across regions, equity, credit and real-estate flows on the SAME slow policy calendar the divergence itself runs on). This is corroborated by two independent structural findings this same pipeline surfaced earlier today: crude's geopolitically-driven backwardation (OPEC+ supply restraint, Hormuz risk, sanctions) and the 2s10s's narrow, fiscal/issuance-driven positive slope (~45-51bp, federal debt >$39.8T) are each, independently, multi-quarter regimes with a slow-moving driver -- the same divergence condition showing up asset-class by asset-class, not one coincidence borrowed to justify another. A 210-trading-day (~10-month) lookback matches that clock: cross_sectional_rank's momentum leg has been tested at 20, 30, 45, 60, 63, 70, 84, 105, 126, 168, 189 and 252 but never at 210, chosen deliberately near the top of the pre-registered range to track a policy cycle measured in years, not weeks -- a citation for reaction SPEED, the same shape of parameter choice as today's earlier curve_carry(lookback=180) proposal, not a claim that momentum or any live strategy has recently performed well. k=3 is mid-range and matches the k already used at the neighboring 189-day lookback, rather than reaching for an untested k. Checked pipeline_ideas.csv: no prior cross_sectional_rank proposal used lookback=210 (all existing momentum lookbacks listed above are taken). Checked graveyard.csv: every verdicted momentum lookback (63, 84, 105, 126, 168, 189, 252) is DEAD against the pre-registered null; 20 and 30 remain pending screening. cross_sectional_rank is tied with single_asset_trend for this pipeline's least-proposed primitive (24 each, vs 25-28 for the rest as of 2026-08-27) -- raised here because today's research specifically supports it, not to balance the tally on its own.

Citation: J.P. Morgan Asset Management, 'Policy divergence reshapes the front end: Implications for Global Liquidity in 2026' (https://am.jpmorgan.com/us/en/asset-management/liq/insights/liquidity-insights/updates/policy-divergence-reshapes-the-front-end-implication-for-global-liquidity/); BlackRock, 'A 2026 global macro outlook: Patience' (https://www.blackrock.com/institutions/en-us/insights/2026-macro-outlook); FRED T10Y2Y and Investing.com oil-backwardation reporting cited in this pipeline's earlier 2026-08-27 proposals (rp_curve_carry_180, rp_static_spread_carry_USO_GLD_a).


### `rp_single_asset_trend_GLD_252` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-27, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=GLD, lookback=252. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: single_asset_trend(ticker=GLD, lookback=252) bets that gold's own ~12-month trailing return keeps trending, not mean-reverting -- a bet that only pays off if a persistent, slow-moving driver is pushing gold's price LEVEL, not daily noise. MECHANISM: central banks are running a genuinely structural, price-insensitive reserve reallocation into gold, not a cyclical flow. The World Gold Council's 2026 Central Bank Gold Reserves Survey -- its highest-participation edition on record, 76 respondents, fielded Feb-May 2026 -- finds 74% of central banks expect the dollar's reserve share to fall over the next five years while 84% expect gold's share to rise, and 89% expect global official-sector gold holdings to increase over the next year (a record 45% plan to add to their OWN reserves). Official purchases have averaged ~1,000 tonnes/year over the past four years, more than double the ~500-tonne average of the prior decade, and Q2 2026 alone added 289 tonnes (+62% YoY, the strongest Q2 in the WGC data series) -- bought THROUGH falling prices that quarter, confirming the buying is strategic, not price-timed. Central banks are the largest, least price-sensitive buyer in this market, executing a stated multi-year reallocation rather than tactical timing -- exactly the kind of slow, calendar-paced demand shock that should show up as a sustained trend rather than mean-reverting noise. This is a LEVEL/flow argument about gold itself, distinct in kind from this same pipeline's other two 2026-08-27 proposals (crude's curve-shape backwardation, the 2s10s slope's steepening), and from today's earlier static_spread_carry (USO/GLD), where GLD appeared only as the flat-carry FINANCING leg of a crude bet, never as a directional claim on gold's own level -- so this does not double up on that thesis or that pair. lookback=252 -- the top of the pre-registered [20,252] range and the canonical ~12-month TSMOM horizon -- is chosen to match the survey's own multi-year framing rather than react to weekly gold-price noise, the same reasoning this pipeline used for curve_carry(lookback=180) earlier today. single_asset_trend is this pipeline's least-proposed primitive (24 of 156 prior rows, vs 25-28 for the rest as of 2026-08-27); GLD has been tried under it at lookback=25 (pending screen) and lookback=126 (DEAD in graveyard.csv), but never at 252 -- checked pipeline_ideas.csv and graveyard.csv, this exact spec appears in neither.

Citation: World Gold Council, 'Central Bank Gold Reserves Survey 2026' (gold.org/goldhub/research/central-bank-gold-reserves-survey-2026), 74% expect lower USD reserve share over 5yrs, 84% expect higher gold share, 89% expect rising global official gold holdings, 45% (record) plan to add to own reserves; GoldSilver.com, 'Central Banks Bought 289 Tonnes of Gold Last Quarter. Prices Were Falling the Whole Time.' (Q2 2026, +62% YoY); GoldSilver.com, 'Central Banks Keep Buying Gold at Record Prices' (avg ~1,000 tonnes/yr past 4yrs vs ~500 tonnes/yr prior decade).


### `rp_single_asset_trend_LQD_189` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-27, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=LQD, lookback=189. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: Investment-grade corporate credit (LQD) is facing a structural, multi-quarter supply overhang from AI-hyperscaler bond issuance that is still building, not receding: incremental debt as a share of hyperscaler AI capex rose from 9% in FY24 to 32% for the LTM before June 2026, aggregate hyperscaler total debt reached roughly $700B, and H1 2026 alone saw $194B of new IG-rated issuance, on pace for ~$250B for the full year (~33% of 2026 capex) as aggregate hyperscaler free cash flow is projected to cross zero around Q3 2026 (operating cash flow growing ~23%/yr against cash capex growing ~70%/yr). Goldman Sachs separately projects more than a third of 2027 AI investment will be debt-funded, and hyperscaler capex itself is forecast to keep rising (~$750B in 2026 to ~$1.2T in 2027), so this is not a one-off issuance spike but a growing, multi-year supply trend hitting the same asset class LQD tracks. A large, sustained increase in IG bond supply from a concentrated set of blue-chip issuers pressures the sector absorbing it (spreads widen / prices soften, all else equal) independent of the Fed policy-rate path, which the pre-registered curve_carry primitive already isolates -- single_asset_trend on LQD is the cheapest way in this vocabulary to let a position track the net direction of that ongoing supply pressure without forecasting its exact magnitude or timing.

Citation: Epoch AI, "Hyperscaler Capex to Exceed Cash Flow by Q3 2026" (epoch.ai/data-insights/hyperscaler-capex-vs-cash-flow); Introl, "Hyperscaler CapEx Hits $600B in 2026" (introl.com/blog/hyperscaler-capex-600b-2026-ai-infrastructure-debt-january-2026); MUFG Americas, "Financing the AI Supercycle" AI Chart Weekly, Dec 2026 (mufgamericas.com); Goldman Sachs AI-investment debt-funding forecast for 2027, via Yahoo Finance (finance.yahoo.com/markets/article/big-tech-will-fund-more-than-a-third-of-its-ai-investments-with-debt-in-2027-goldman-sachs-predicts-145136150.html).


### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_SLV_QQQ_189_210` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-27, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=SLV, ticker_b=QQQ, lookback_a=189, lookback_b=210. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: asset_class_trend_hedge(SLV, QQQ, lookback_a=189, lookback_b=210) combines two independent single-leg trend signals -- silver's own trailing return and QQQ's own trailing return -- on the shared thesis that the current AI-infrastructure buildout is a genuine, multi-quarter STRUCTURAL driver pushing both a commodity and a big-tech equity basket in the same direction from two mechanically different asset classes, not a claim that either has recently 'performed well'. MECHANISM, SILVER LEG: the Silver Institute's 2026 forecast puts the market in its 6th consecutive annual deficit -- a 46.3 million oz shortfall, WIDENING from 40.3 million oz in 2025 -- and, critically, the widening is happening DESPITE solar PV manufacturers cutting silver use ~19% via thrifting/substitution: the deficit persists because AI-driven demand (high-performance AI-server semiconductors, data-center precision contacts and thermal-management components) is now large enough to more than offset a falling solar leg, on top of continued EV demand and mining supply that takes a decade to expand as a byproduct metal. That is a supply/demand LEVEL story with a multi-year forward horizon, not a reaction to a daily price move. MECHANISM, QQQ LEG: QQQ is the same AI-buildout's demand side -- the Big-5 hyperscalers (Amazon, Alphabet, Meta, Microsoft, Oracle) are guided to ~$775-800B of 2026 capex (+64% YoY, ~75%/$450B of it AI infrastructure: GPUs, servers, datacenters), overall datacentre capex is forecast to cross $1T in 2026, and Goldman Sachs projects $1.15T of hyperscaler capex across 2025-2027 vs $477B in 2022-2024 -- QQQ is heavily weighted to exactly these hyperscalers and their chip suppliers, so its trailing-return sign should track the same buildout. WHY THIS IS AN ASSET_CLASS_TREND_HEDGE, NOT A DUPLICATE OF TODAY'S OTHER PROPOSALS: today's earlier single_asset_trend(LQD, 189) bet on AI capex pressuring investment-grade CREDIT supply (a financing-side, rates-asset-class claim); this bets on the DEMAND side showing up in an EQUITY basket (QQQ) and, independently, on a COMMODITY supply/demand imbalance (SLV) that AI demand only partially drives -- two different tickers, two different asset classes (commodity vs equity per ASSET_CLASS), two different mechanisms, sharing only the same macro backdrop the way this pipeline's earlier 'independent structural regimes, same backdrop' proposals today already established is fine. This is exactly the primitive's intended shape (per its own description: 'combine two signals into one candidate' with a named mechanism, checked mechanically for decorrelation on 2007-2017 calibration data regardless of how the citation reads) -- a commodity-supply-deficit driver and a hyperscaler-capex driver are structurally distinct enough that they are not expected to move in lockstep even though both are currently pointed the same way. PARAMETERS: lookback_a=189 (~9mo) and lookback_b=210 (~10mo), both within the pre-registered [20,252] range and both near its upper end, chosen because the deficit-widening and capex-guidance horizons here are stated in multi-quarter/multi-year terms (annual Silver Institute forecasts, 2025-2027 capex guidance), not weekly data -- the same reasoning this pipeline used for curve_carry(lookback=180) and single_asset_trend(GLD, 252) earlier today. freq=M matches that slow horizon; a finer freq would pay turnover reacting to noise the signal can't see. PRIMITIVE CHOICE: asset_class_trend_hedge is the only one of this pipeline's 6 primitives not yet used today (today's other five proposals covered static_spread_carry, curve_carry, cross_sectional_rank, and single_asset_trend twice), so proposing it now completes today's coverage of the full vocabulary; overall it sits at 27 of 160 prior rows, roughly mid-pack (25-28 across all six) -- raised here because today's actual research specifically supports this combination, not to force balance on its own. NOT A REPROPOSAL: checked pipeline_ideas.csv (grep across all asset_class_trend_hedge rows, including SLV_TLT_168_65 and QQQ_LQD_126_63, both already tried) and graveyard.csv -- no prior SLV/QQQ combination exists under this or any primitive in either file.

Citation: Investing News Network, 'Silver Institute: Sustained Supply Deficit Exposes Market to Squeezes' (2026), citing Silver Institute/Metals Focus World Silver Survey 2026 forecast of a 6th consecutive annual deficit, 46.3Moz in 2026 vs 40.3Moz in 2025, solar PV silver use down ~19% via thrifting/substitution more than offset by AI/data-center/semiconductor and EV demand (investingnews.com/silver-institute-forecast/); Futurum Group, 'AI Capex 2026: The $690B Infrastructure Sprint' and AlCapital Advisory, 'AI Capex Cycle 2026: $775-800B Hyperscaler Buildout -- CFA Analysis' (Big-5 hyperscaler 2026 capex ~$775-800B, +64% YoY, ~75%/$450B AI infrastructure; Goldman Sachs $1.15T hyperscaler capex 2025-2027 vs $477B 2022-2024); IEEE ComSoc Technology Blog, 'Hyperscaler capex > $600bn in 2026, a 36% increase over 2025' and Electronics Weekly, 'Datacentre capex to hit $1trn in 2026' (2025-12/2026).


### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_QQQ_TLT_126_63` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-27, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=QQQ, ticker_b=TLT, lookback_a=126, lookback_b=63. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: asset_class_trend_hedge holds two independent trailing-return-sign legs across two DIFFERENT asset classes, and requires a citable channel for why they should offset -- not just historical decorrelation. CURRENT STRUCTURAL CONDITION, NOT A PERFORMANCE CLAIM: 2026 is a genuine supply-shock year for the long end of the US corporate and Treasury-adjacent duration market, driven directly by the same AI-capex story concentrated in QQQ's own megacap holdings. Hyperscaler gross bond issuance already exceeds all of 2025 -- roughly $152B in the first four and a half months of 2026 against Street estimates of ~$300B in AI-related investment-grade supply for the full year, with about a third of ~$800B aggregate 2026 hyperscaler capex expected to be debt-financed (Goldman Sachs, Vanguard, Investing.com coverage, 2026). Critically this issuance is deliberately LONG-DATED -- most maturities five years and longer -- to lock in multiyear funding for AI infrastructure builds, which materially extends the duration profile competing for the same long-end buyer base TLT itself draws on. That supply pressure is visible in the long end directly: the 10-year Treasury yield reached 4.69% on 2026-08-20, and IG spreads on bonds maturing beyond 20 years widened to 118bp from a lower prior level as the market absorbed the wave (S&P warning of 'fatigue' with hyperscalers 'paying a higher premium compared with yields on risk-free bonds'). THE OFFSETTING CHANNEL: this is a single financing mechanism producing opposite-signed pressure on the two legs, not a coincidental correlation. When the AI-capex narrative strengthens (new hyperscaler guidance, accelerating GPU/datacenter builds), it does two things simultaneously through the SAME channel: it supports the earnings case dominating QQQ's own top-heavy composition (QQQ up), and it raises the debt-financed share and pace of that capex, adding to the long-duration supply TLT's own price must absorb (TLT down as yields rise). When the capex narrative cools (a guidance disappointment, a pause in debt-financed buildout), both reverse together: QQQ's growth premium compresses while long-end supply pressure eases and TLT rallies. This is NOT a claim that the strategy has performed well, nor that trend-following broadly works -- this lab has killed trend-following in every family tested so far, including on both TLT and QQQ individually -- only that this specific, live, event-dated supply/earnings dual-channel gives trailing-return sign on these two legs, over this window, something structural to track that it lacks in a generic 'growth stocks vs bonds' framing. PARAMETER CHOICE: lookback_a=126 (~6 months) on QQQ spans roughly two earnings cycles, the cadence at which hyperscaler capex guidance itself is actually revised upward or downward; lookback_b=63 (~3 months) on TLT is shorter because Treasury-market absorption of a given quarter's issuance calendar and auction results reprices faster than the earnings-driven equity leg. freq=W matches the pace new issuance and guidance headlines are actually arriving (multiple hyperscaler bond deals priced per month through 2026). DISTINCTNESS: this exact pair (QQQ, TLT) has not been proposed by this pipeline before in any primitive; every existing QQQ row in this ledger (paired with USO, SPY, EEM, GLD, LQD) and every existing TLT row (paired with IEF via curve_carry, DBC, VNQ) rest on a different channel than the AI-capex-debt mechanism cited here. As always, the calibration-window correlation guard (legs_pass_calibration_corr_cap) is the actual arbiter of whether this offsetting claim holds up in 2007-2017 data, not this rationale -- 2007-2017 obviously predates the AI-capex story itself, so a pass there would only confirm these two legs' generic trend signals decorrelate mechanically, not that the specific 2026 channel described above is what will drive it forward; that is exactly the split between the mechanical guard and the OOS test that follows it.

Citation: Hyperscaler debt issuance and capex figures: Goldman Sachs, 'How AI Debt Is Reshaping Credit Markets' (goldmansachs.com/insights/goldman-sachs-exchanges/how-ai-debt-is-reshaping-the-credit-market); Vanguard, 'The AI buildout comes to the bond market' (corporate.vanguard.com/content/corporatesite/us/en/corp/vemo/ai-buildout-comes-to-bond-market.html); Investing.com, 'AI Bond Issuance Tests the Market's Appetite for Long-Dated Tech Debt' (investing.com/analysis/ai-bond-issuance-tests-the-markets-appetite-for-longdated-tech-debt-200680972) -- ~$152B hyperscaler gross issuance in first 4.5 months of 2026 vs ~$300B full-year AI-related IG supply estimate, ~$800B aggregate 2026 hyperscaler capex with ~one-third debt-financed, issuance concentrated in 5yr+ maturities. Long-end yield/spread impact: 10-year Treasury yield 4.69% on 2026-08-20 (Associated Press, via Investing.com coverage); IG spreads on 20yr+ maturities widening to 118bp, S&P Global Ratings commentary on hyperscaler credit fatigue, cited in sageadvisory.com/article/hyperscaler-debt-deluge-the-new-driver-of-ig-spread-pressure and mlq.ai/news/hyperscaler-bond-issuance-reaches-about-220-billion-as-ai-financing-costs-rise/.


### `rp_cross_sectional_rank_low_vol_30_3` (primitive `cross_sectional_rank`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-27, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: metric=low_vol, lookback=30, k=3. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: cross_sectional_rank(metric=low_vol, lookback=30, k=3) ranks the full UNIVERSE by trailing ~6-week realized volatility, long the 3 calmest tickers / short the 3 most volatile -- a bet that right now is specifically a good time for a low-vol tilt to hold its ranking between rebalances, not a claim that a low-vol factor or any live strategy has recently performed well. MECHANISM: two independent, corroborating data points put risk pricing across asset classes in a genuinely unusual, structurally-explained low-volatility/compressed-risk-premium regime as of mid-to-late August 2026, not a coincidence of one noisy week. First, equity volatility: the VIX fell to 14.25 in mid-August 2026, a 2026 year-to-date low, with realized volatility subdued, cross-asset correlation low, and vol-of-vol at 2-year lows (Cboe: 'Volatility/Convexity Premia Fall to Lowest YTD Levels on Benign Inflation Data'; EBC Financial Group), explicitly attributed to a benign-inflation backdrop rather than a one-off calm week. Second, and independently, credit pricing shows the identical compressed-risk-premium signature: US high-yield OAS sits at 275-284bp (ICE BofA / Convex, 2026-08-20/24), the richest decile of its history against a ~450bp long-run median and back to 2007-era tightness, explained by ETF Trends/thetrading.tools as 'the macro mix -- Fed easing, the curve re-steepened, and defaults contained' -- a regime explicitly framed as one that 'can persist for quarters', i.e. a slow-moving structural condition, not daily noise. Two different, independently-sourced risk markets (equity implied/realized vol and high-yield credit spreads) showing the same compression at the same time, both attributed to the same benign-inflation/contained-default/easing-Fed backdrop, is exactly the kind of structural regime a volatility-ranking primitive should care about: a low-vol cross-sectional tilt only holds together when the vol-dispersion regime itself is stable enough for a 30-day-trailing ranking to still describe the UNIVERSE by the time of the next rebalance, and a benign, contained-default, easing-cycle backdrop -- rather than a fragile calm about to break -- is the condition that makes that plausible right now. PARAMETERS: lookback=30 (unused gap in the pre-registered [20,252] range; existing low_vol proposals cluster at 45/63/84/100/126/252, all longer) is chosen deliberately short to track the CURRENT regime (VIX's YTD low was reached only in mid-August) rather than blend in the higher-vol months earlier in 2026 a 100+ day lookback would still be averaging over. k=3 matches this ledger's modal choice (used in 14 of 25 prior cross_sectional_rank proposals) rather than reaching for an untested extreme. freq=W matches a regime that moves on a macro/quarterly clock, not daily noise -- consistent with how this pipeline sized freq for its other 2026-08-27 regime-persistence proposals (curve_carry lookback=180, cross_sectional_rank momentum lookback=210). PRIMITIVE CHOICE: cross_sectional_rank is tied for this pipeline's least-proposed primitive alongside curve_carry (25 of 161 prior rows each, vs 26-29 for the rest as of 2026-08-27) -- and within it, metric=low_vol is itself the least-used metric (6 uses vs 13 for momentum) -- raised here because today's actual research (the VIX/HY-spread compression finding) specifically supports a volatility-regime bet, not to force balance on its own. NOT A REPROPOSAL: checked pipeline_ideas.csv (grep across all cross_sectional_rank low_vol rows: 45_3, 63_3, 84_3, 100_3, 126_5, 252_2 -- lookback=30 untried) and graveyard.csv (no cross_sectional_rank_low_vol_30_3 verdict exists) -- this exact spec appears in neither. Distinct from today's earlier rp_cross_sectional_rank_momentum_210_3 (a directional trend claim on the SAME ranking machinery, opposite economic reasoning: that one bets returns keep trending under multi-quarter policy divergence, this one bets realized-vol RANKING stays stable under a currently-benign, low-dispersion regime -- the two share no premise).

Citation: Cboe Insights, 'Week of 8/17/2026: Volatility/Convexity Premia Fall to Lowest YTD Levels on Benign Inflation Data' (cboe.com/insights/posts/week-of-8-17-2026-volatility-convexity-premia-fall-to-lowest-ytd-levels-on-benign-inflation-data); EBC Financial Group, 'Why Is the VIX Index Near a 2026 Low While Crash Protection Remains Expensive?' (ebc.com/forex/vix-2026-low-crash-protection), VIX at 14.25 mid-August 2026, YTD low; ETF Trends, 'Credit Spreads at Historic Tights: What Now?' (etftrends.com/credit-spreads-historic-tights-now), HY OAS in richest decile vs ~450bp long-run median, regime attributed to Fed easing/re-steepened curve/contained defaults, 'can persist for quarters'; Convex, 'HY Credit Spread (OAS): 275 bps (Aug 20, 2026)' (convextrade.com/metrics/bamlh0a0hym2); thetrading.tools, 'Credit Spreads Tracker: HYG/LQD Ratio, Z-Score Regimes & Risk-Off Signal', HYG/LQD ratio at +3.07 std devs vs 1-year history as of 2026-08-17.


### `rp_single_asset_trend_EEM_45` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-28, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=EEM, lookback=45. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: single_asset_trend takes the sign of EEM's own trailing return over the lookback window -- it monetizes CONTINUATION of a move already under way, not a level forecast, and the sign rule takes either side symmetrically. CURRENT STRUCTURAL CONDITION, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM ABOUT EEM ITSELF: the dollar is in a genuine, dated weakening episode with an identifiable structural driver, and dollar weakness is a textbook, well-established transmission channel into EM equity flows (weaker USD eases dollar-debt service and import costs for EM borrowers, improves EM terms of trade, and increases the relative appeal of EM carry/yield to global allocators) -- this is a channel argument, not a claim that EEM has recently outperformed. The driver: the July 2026 nonfarm payrolls report, published in early August, showed payrolls FELL by 23,000 against a forecast GAIN of 80,000, with a further 103,000 jobs erased from the May/June revisions -- a large, dated, negative labor surprise. That landed on an already-divided FOMC: the July 29 meeting held rates at 3.50-3.75% for a second straight meeting with a rare 9-3 split (three dissents), a level of internal disagreement itself unusual and evidence the committee's reaction function is genuinely unsettled, not on a pre-set path. The dollar index (DXY) responded by falling to 98.55 on August 22, 2026 -- its lowest level since May -- a concrete, multi-month low reached inside the exact lookback window this proposal uses, not a stale or backward-looking reference. This sits on top of, and is reinforced by, the same live catalyst curve_carry proposals in this ledger already cite: Fed Chair Kevin Warsh's first Jackson Hole keynote as chair (today, Aug 28, 10am ET), where a dovish surprise is explicitly expected to extend dollar softness further (a hawkish surprise would reverse it) -- so the near-term catalyst calendar is two-sided but concentrated on the same variable this trend rule reads: the dollar's own recent direction. PARAMETERS: lookback=45 trading days (~9 weeks) is sized to the episode itself -- it reaches back to just before the early-August payrolls shock without diluting the signal against the many months of prior, unrelated dollar behavior a 100+ day window would still be averaging in. freq=W matches a macro/policy-driven move (monthly payrolls prints, a six-week FOMC calendar) rather than a daily microstructure signal. DISTINCTNESS: EEM has been proposed once before via this exact primitive (rp_single_asset_trend_EEM_189, 2026-08-07, DEAD in graveyard.csv) on a completely different mechanism -- EEM's index concentration in AI-semiconductor names and a multi-quarter capex cycle, a ~9-month window -- and does not lean on or dispute that verdict; this proposal's driver (a dollar-weakness macro channel, ~9-week window) shares no premise with it. HONESTY CAVEAT: EEM's own semiconductor concentration (per the 2026-08-07 proposal) means a large idiosyncratic shock to that supply chain could dominate or override the dollar-channel signal this proposal actually targets -- that risk is unrelated to and not addressed by the reasoning here. Whether the channel is strong enough within this window to clear the DSR/CPCV gate is exactly what the downstream screen decides.

Citation: Vantage Markets, 'US Dollar Index Falls to 98.55 as Yields Ease, Fed Splits' (2026-08-24; DXY 98.55 on 2026-08-22, lowest since May, 9-3 FOMC split, July nonfarm payrolls -23,000 vs +80,000 forecast, -103,000 combined May/June revisions): vantagemarkets.com/market-analysis/us-dollar-index-dxy-falls-98-55-jackson-hole-august-24-28-2026/. AllianceBernstein, 'How US Dollar Weakness Could Buoy Emerging Markets' (mechanism: weaker USD eases EM debt service and import costs, improves EM terms of trade and relative carry appeal): alliancebernstein.com/us/en-us/investments/insights/investment-insights/how-us-dollar-weakness-could-buoy-emerging-markets.html. CNBC, 'Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh delivers his key Jackson Hole speech Friday. Here's what to expect' (2026-08-27) -- dovish surprise expected to extend dollar softness, hawkish surprise to reverse it. In-repo: rp_single_asset_trend_EEM_189 (2026-08-07) and its DEAD verdict in graveyard.csv, cited only to establish this proposal rests on a different mechanism.


### `rp_single_asset_trend_USO_90` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-28, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=USO, lookback=90. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: single_asset_trend's direction is mechanical (sign of USO's own trailing return), not chosen by me -- this is a structural-conditions claim about why a trend in crude is more likely to persist right now, not a forecast of which way it goes or a claim about any strategy's recent track record. THE STRUCTURAL SETUP. Two independent, currently-operating supply-side facts point the same direction. (1) Term structure: WTI moved into steep backwardation after the US-Israel-Iran conflict opened on 2026-02-28 and the effective Hormuz disruption that followed -- December-2026 contracts traded as much as $40 below May/June delivery at the peak -- but that premium has been unwinding since the 2026-04-07 ceasefire, and for much of 2026 the curve has reverted to contango, forward months priced above spot, which is the market's own forward-looking signal of weak near-term demand relative to supply, not a lagging indicator of price. (2) Physical balance: both major forecasters now project a large 2026 glut -- EIA's STEO puts the global surplus at 2.2 million b/d for 2026 (global liquids output ~106 mb/d against consumption ~104.1 mb/d), while IEA's August 2026 Oil Market Report puts it closer to 4 mb/d (~4% of world demand); both cite the same mechanism, OPEC+ unwinding its voluntary cuts (~1.2-1.4 mb/d of added OPEC+ supply into 2026) plus continued non-OPEC growth from the US, Canada, Brazil and Guyana. WHY THIS FAVORS TREND PERSISTENCE, mechanically, not directionally. A glut driven by a quota-and-capacity supply process and absorbed through inventory builds is a slow, multi-month repricing, not a single news shock -- WTI has been consolidating in the mid-$80s (stalling near $84.30) while the curve and the physical-balance forecasts both already point the same way, which is the setup a medium-horizon trend signal is built to catch: a persistent structural repricing rather than a one-day jump. LOOKBACK 90, ARGUED NOT TUNED. The IEA/EIA glut estimates are framed as full-year 2026 averages and the ceasefire that started the contango unwind is now almost 5 months old (2026-04-07 to 2026-08-28, ~100 trading days) -- 90 trading days (~1 quarter) is close to that unwind's own elapsed clock and short of a full year, long enough to filter the day-to-day headline noise this same ticker's single_asset_trend proposals at lookback 42 (2026-08-21) and lookback 126 (2026-08-07) sit on either side of, without being a re-proposal of either. freq W because a supply-and-inventory-driven repricing has no reason to be captured or acted on intraday; the position should turn over on the same order as the structural process generating it, not faster. WEAKNESSES, STATED BEFORE THE VERDICT, WORST FIRST. (1) Every trend-following strategy this lab has tested is DEAD -- tsmom, green_line_200d, family L's hourly trend, ~35 donchian_gen_* factory draws, and USO's own two prior single_asset_trend proposals carry the same prior. Changing the commodity and the reason for expecting persistence does not change the shape, and the entire burden is on the mechanism being different in kind, which it may not be. (2) Forecaster oversupply numbers disagree by nearly 2x between EIA and IEA (2.2 vs ~4 mb/d) and both are routinely revised; a 2007-2017 calibration window cannot see 2026-specific OPEC+ or shale-growth dynamics at all, so a calibration PASS here would necessarily rest on generic trend persistence in the window, not on this thesis, and only the 2018+ OOS gate can speak to the actual claim. (3) Geopolitical risk cuts the other way and is not mean-reverting on any fixed clock -- a renewed Hormuz disruption or a fresh regional escalation would flip the curve back to backwardation abruptly, which is exactly the kind of jump a 90-day trailing-return signal is slow to both catch and unwind from.

Citation: Term structure and Hormuz/ceasefire timeline: Investing.com, 'Oil Trading in August 2026: Backwardation, Crack Spreads and Hormuz Risk Explained' (investing.com/analysis/oil-trading-in-august-2026-backwardation-crack-spreads-and-hormuz-risk-explained-200686148); CME Group, 'Implications of WTI Oil Futures In Backwardation Amid the Supply Crunch' (cmegroup.com/insights/economic-research/2026/implications-of-wti-oil-futures-in-backwardation-amid-the-supply-crunch.html), also syndicated via Yahoo Finance. 2026 glut forecasts: EIA Short-Term Energy Outlook, global 2026 surplus ~2.2 million b/d, liquids output ~106 mb/d vs consumption ~104.1 mb/d, OPEC+ supply +1.2-1.4 mb/d into 2026 (globalnews.ca/news/11523165/eia-oil-price-forecast-oversupply-2026/); IEA, 'Oil Market Report - August 2026' (iea.org/reports/oil-market-report-august-2026), ~4 mb/d 2026 surplus estimate referenced via gcn.com/iea-projects-massive-global-oil-surplus-in-2026/12080. Prior-art/lookback context: this ledger's own rp_single_asset_trend_USO_126 (2026-08-07) and rp_single_asset_trend_USO_42 (2026-08-21); graveyard.csv trend rows (tsmom_12m, green_line_200d, family L, donchian_gen_*), all DEAD.


### `rp_single_asset_trend_TLT_60` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-28, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=TLT, lookback=60. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: MECHANISM: single_asset_trend takes the sign of TLT's (20+yr Treasury) own trailing return over the lookback window -- a bet that a move already under way persists, symmetric to either direction, not a call on rate levels. STRUCTURAL CONDITION, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM: nothing here asserts that trend-following or any live strategy has recently performed well -- every input below is a dated policy-intervention outcome, not a return. THE EVIDENCE, ex-post rather than ex-ante (distinct from this ledger's existing long-end trend proposals, see DISTINCTNESS below): the long end has been in an active fiscal/term-premium-driven selloff since mid-August (30yr yield reaching its highest level since 2007, ~5.33-5.34%, on 2026-08-17/18, driven by deficit concerns as public debt approaches $40 trillion, heavy long-dated coupon supply, inflation stuck above target, and renewed US-Israel-Iran escalation risk), and the US Treasury has since made two separate, publicly announced attempts to reverse it by force -- and both have failed within days, not reflecting a track record of any trading strategy but of a specific, identifiable institutional actor's interventions. (1) On 2026-08-19 Treasury Secretary Bessent announced a doubled debt-buyback program for longer-term debt, and yields fell on the news. (2) By 2026-08-20 that decline had already been fully erased as yields rebounded (CNBC, 'Treasury yields rebound, wiping out the decline following Bessent's intervention'; Bloomberg, 'Bessent's plan at best circuit breaker for global bond slump'). (3) Bessent responded on 2026-08-21 by vowing to go even bigger. (4) By 2026-08-25 the 30yr had climbed back to 5.27% and reporting explicitly framed the prior intervention's effect as having 'lasted two days' (Universe News Network). (5) The week-of-8/24 Cboe Insights report -- already cited elsewhere in today's ledger for its MOVE/VXTLT data, cited here only for that same data, not reused as a second independent source -- shows the MOVE index and the VXTLT 20y-bond-vol index both turning back up over that same window, consistent with the stress still being live into this week rather than resolved by either intervention. WHY THIS FAVORS PERSISTENCE, MECHANICALLY: a move that a large, credible, repeatedly-announced official intervention cannot reverse for more than ~1-2 days is evidence the move is being driven by a slow-moving structural stock (accumulated fiscal deficits, net long-dated coupon supply, elevated term premium) rather than by a transient positioning imbalance that a buyback could plausibly absorb -- exactly the autocorrelated-repricing condition a trailing-sign trend rule needs, and a cleaner, more falsifiable version of that claim than an ex-ante supply argument alone, because it is based on the market's own revealed response to a real reversion attempt rather than on a forecast of one. PARAMETERS: lookback=60 (~1 quarter) is sized to span the run-up into and through this acute August episode (back to roughly early-to-mid June) without diluting it against the calmer stretch of H1 2026 further back; freq=W matches the roughly weekly cadence of the intervention/reversal pattern itself (each attempted circuit-breaker has been overturned within days, not hours), so a weekly rebalance tracks the episode's own clock rather than whipsawing on single-day intervention headlines a daily freq would chase. DISTINCTNESS: TLT has one prior single_asset_trend proposal in this ledger (rp_single_asset_trend_TLT_126, 2026-08-08, lookback 126/M) built on an ex-ante term-premium/quarterly-refunding-calendar argument dated before any buyback existed; IEF has a separate one (rp_single_asset_trend_IEF_147, 2026-08-19) built on a forward-guidance/communication-style argument for the belly of the curve. This proposal's mechanism -- revealed intervention failure over 2026-08-19/25, an event that postdates both prior proposals -- and its lookback (60) are both new; it does not dispute or lean on either prior verdict. HONESTY CAVEATS, worst first: (1) every trend variant tested in this lab is DEAD, including TLT and IEF themselves at other lookbacks, and changing the supporting evidence does not change the shape being tested or lower the bar. (2) The 'intervention failed twice' pattern is a sample size of two dated events, not a law -- a third, larger buyback operation (the doubled $4bn-plus program is scheduled to run 2026-09-09 through 2026-11-04) could yet succeed in reversing the move, which would argue against persistence going forward even though it has held so far. (3) Fed Chair Kevin Warsh's Jackson Hole keynote lands today (2026-08-28) with markets split on its direction; a sharply dovish surprise could pull the whole curve down in a way unrelated to the fiscal/term-premium story this proposal targets, and a 60-day lookback would not distinguish that shock from the structural move it is trying to capture.

Citation: Bloomberg, 'US Bond Selloff Drives 30-Year Yields to Highest Since 2007' (bloomberg.com/news/articles/2026-08-17/us-bond-selloff-drives-30-year-yields-to-the-highest-since-2007). CNBC, 'Treasury doubles debt buybacks as Bessent moves to steady bond market' (2026-08-19, cnbc.com/2026/08/19/treasury-announces-upscaled-buyback-operation-for-longer-term-debt-sending-yields-lower.html) and 'Treasury yields rebound, wiping out the decline following Bessent's intervention' (2026-08-20, cnbc.com/2026/08/20/bond-yields-edge-higher-as-traders-digest-treasury-debt-buyback-plan.html). Bloomberg, 'Bessent's plan at best circuit breaker for global bond slump' / 'US 30-Year Bonds Reverse Gains From Treasury's Buyback Surprise' (2026-08-20, bloomberg.com/news/articles/2026-08-20/bessent-s-plan-at-best-circuit-breaker-for-global-bond-slump). Euronews, 'Bessent vows bigger buybacks after bond yields erased the US Treasury's relief rally' (2026-08-21, euronews.com/business/2026/08/21/bessent-vows-bigger-buybacks-after-bond-yields-erased-the-us-treasurys-relief-rally). Universe News Network, 'Bessent Treasury Buyback Effect Lasts Two Days As 30-Year Yield Climbs Back To 5.27%' (2026-08-25, universenewsnetwork.com/2026/08/25/bessent-treasury-buyback-effect-lasts-two-days-as-30-year-yield-climbs-back-to-5-27). Cboe Insights, 'Week of 8/24/2026: Cross-Asset Volatilities Rise as Treasury Endeavors to Avert Yield Contagion' (cboe.com/insights/posts/week-of-8-24-2026-cross-asset-volatilities-rise-as-treasury-endeavors-to-avert-yield-contagion), cited here only for its MOVE/VXTLT data, already cited elsewhere in today's ledger.


### `rp_asset_class_trend_hedge_USO_TLT_60_90` (primitive `asset_class_trend_hedge`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-28, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker_a=USO, ticker_b=TLT, lookback_a=60, lookback_b=90. Rebalance freq: W.
