Rationale: The San Francisco Fed's own August 2026 Economic Letter (Mertens & Wasserburger, "Financial Markets, Oil Prices, and Supply-Side Risks") documents that the stock-bond correlation has FLIPPED because the market's perceived source of macro risk shifted from demand shocks to supply shocks, with elevated oil prices specifically named as the prominent channel now priced into rates -- following the Fed's own Dec 2025 letter on the rising sensitivity of interest rates to oil supply news. That mechanism is live right now, not historical: WTI futures have stayed in backwardation through August 2026 (mid-$80s spot, Brent ~$91-92) on a persistent Hormuz-shipping-risk supply shock, not a demand story -- exactly the regime the Fed letter describes, where oil-supply-driven inflation expectations move Treasury yields (and so TLT) in the direction opposite the oil move, rather than the two assets floating independently. USO/TLT is a genuinely new pair for this primitive (closest prior proposals are USO/IEF at 63/168 and TLT/DBC at 252/60 -- neither tests this specific commodity/duration combination), and asset_class_trend_hedge is the correct primitive because the claim is a specific rate-sensitivity mechanism (oil supply shock -> inflation expectations -> yields -> bond price), not just an assumed correlation. lookback_a=60 (~3mo) matches the trading-day horizon over which the Hormuz-driven backwardation has been building; lookback_b=90 (~4.5mo) gives the rate leg the slower response window the Fed's own oil-supply-news-sensitivity research finds for Treasury yields.

Citation: Mertens, T.M. & Wasserburger, W., "Financial Markets, Oil Prices, and Supply-Side Risks," FRBSF Economic Letter 2026-08 (frbsf.org/research-and-insights/publications/economic-letter/2026/08/financial-markets-oil-prices-and-supply-side-risks/); FRBSF Economic Letter 2025-12, "The Changing Sensitivity of Interest Rates to Oil Supply News"; investing.com, "Oil Trading in August 2026: Backwardation, Crack Spreads and Hormuz Risk Explained" (WTI mid-$80s, Brent ~$91-92, persistent backwardation).


### `rp_cross_sectional_rank_low_vol_42_4` (primitive `cross_sectional_rank`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-28, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: metric=low_vol, lookback=42, k=4. Rebalance freq: W.

Rationale: cross_sectional_rank(metric=low_vol, lookback=42, k=4) ranks the full UNIVERSE by trailing ~2-month realized volatility, long the 4 calmest tickers / short the 4 most volatile -- a bet that the current late-cycle rotation into defensive/low-beta exposure and away from cyclical/high-beta risk makes a volatility-based ranking a structurally meaningful sort of this UNIVERSE right now, not a claim that low-vol or any live strategy has recently outperformed. MECHANISM, not a performance claim: two independent, dated developments point to the same late-cycle risk-repricing rather than one coincidental week. First, a documented June 2026 rotation: over June 16-26, 2026 the low-volatility ETF SPLV rose 1.69% while the momentum ETF MTUM fell 0.09% (a ~1.78pp swing), with June's sector winners being classically defensive -- Utilities +4.7%, Health Care +4.4%, Food/Beverage/Tobacco +4.3% -- an unusual divergence BlackRock's 'The odd couple of 2026: cyclicals and defensives' and a contemporaneous S&P 500 factor-correlation piece both flag as a late-cycle signature distinct from the AI-momentum-driven high-beta leadership that dominated 2025. Second, and independently, commodities' correlation with equities has been dropping toward pre-GFC low levels through 2026 (Capital Spectator/Seeking Alpha, 'Major Asset Classes: July 2026 Performance Review'), restoring commodities' historical diversification role just as commodities (WisdomTree GCC +10.7% YTD) and foreign equities (VWO +5.6%, VEA +5.3%) have structurally outpaced both US equities (VTI +1.6%) and the weakest-performing major asset class, investment-grade bonds (+0.2% YTD) -- i.e. the UNIVERSE's cross-sectional dispersion right now is genuinely asset-class-driven (commodities/foreign-equities vs US-bonds/US-cyclicals), not one common factor moving everything together, which is exactly the condition under which a volatility ranking can meaningfully separate calm from turbulent legs rather than picking up noise. lookback=42 trading days (~2 months) is chosen to date specifically to the June 2026 rotation onset -- long enough to span it, short enough that the ranking reflects the current regime rather than averaging over the pre-rotation AI-momentum period earlier in 2026. k=4 (of 15 UNIVERSE tickers) is wider than the k=2/3 used in prior low_vol proposals in this ledger, deliberately, since a genuine asset-class-level (not single-factor) rotation should show up across more than the top/bottom 2-3 names. Distinct from rp_cross_sectional_rank_low_vol_30_3 (proposed 2026-08-27), which argued from a compressed cross-asset VIX/high-yield-OAS vol-regime being stable enough to hold a ranking -- a vol-of-vol/regime-stability argument -- rather than this proposal's late-cycle sector-rotation/asset-class-dispersion argument; the two share a primitive and metric but rest on different economic mechanisms.

Citation: BlackRock, 'The odd couple of 2026: cyclicals and defensives' (blackrock.com/us/individual/insights/cyclicals-vs-defensive). CryptoDaily, 'Low-Volatility Stocks Beat Momentum: Why the S&P 500 Defensive Rotation Is Back' (cryptodaily.co.uk/2026/07/low-volatility-beats-momentum-defensive-rotation) -- SPLV +1.69% vs MTUM -0.09%, June 16-26 2026; June sector winners Utilities +4.7%, Health Care +4.4%, Food/Beverage/Tobacco +4.3%. Capital Spectator via Seeking Alpha, 'Major Asset Classes: July 2026 Performance Review' (seekingalpha.com/article/4929581-major-asset-classes-july-2026-performance-review) -- YTD 2026 returns: GCC (commodities) +10.7%, VWO (EM equities) +5.6%, VEA (dev-ex-US equities) +5.3%, VTI (US equities) +1.6%, broad IG bonds +0.2%; commodity-equity correlation dropping toward pre-GFC low levels. Investing.com, 'Commodities Lead Major Asset Classes So Far in 2026' (investing.com/analysis/commodities-lead-major-asset-classes-so-far-in-2026-200673866).


### `rp_single_asset_trend_TLT_110` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-29, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=TLT, lookback=110. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: Structural regime claim, not a track-record claim about TLT or any strategy: the market's own real-rate repricing over the last ~110 trading days is the mechanism under test, not a bet on which way TLT has recently moved. Three independent, dated facts define a genuine "higher-for-longer" regime shift centered on this exact window. First, the anchor date: the Fed's own Summary of Economic Projections moved from a 2.7% 2026 core-PCE forecast at the 2026-03-18 meeting to 3.3% by 2026-06-17 -- a rare upward revision of the Fed's own inflation path, not a market view about the Fed. Second, the confirmation: at the 2026-07-29 FOMC (the meeting after that upgrade), the Committee held 3.50-3.75% by a 9-3 vote, but the three dissents (Cleveland's Hammack, Minneapolis's Kashkari, Dallas's Logan) unusually favored a HIKE, not a cut -- per the Fed's own minutes, the first unified-direction dissent since September 2016, and August 2026 minutes coverage reports officials "saw need for rate hike if inflation doesn't cool." Third, the transmission: the 10-year TIPS real yield rose from 1.99% in April 2026 to 2.32% by 2026-08-25 -- real yields climbing over exactly this span, the standard channel by which a hawkish repricing pressures long-duration nominal bonds via rising term premium/discount rate. TLT is UNIVERSE's single most duration-sensitive instrument (~16yr effective duration) to precisely this mechanism, so a trailing-return trend signal on TLT alone is the direct, mechanical way this primitive can capture a still-unfolding real-rate regime shift. The lookback (110 trading days, ~5.5 months) is chosen to span the full 2026-03-18-to-today window over which the Fed's own forecast repriced -- not a round number, and not tuned to any known outcome for TLT itself. Checked pipeline_ideas.csv and graveyard.csv: TLT has been proposed as single_asset_trend twice before (lookback 60 and 126), never at 110, and single_asset_trend/TLT carries no graveyard verdict yet at this lookback.

Citation: Federal Reserve, FOMC Statement 2026-07-29 (federalreserve.gov/newsevents/pressreleases/monetary20260729a.htm) -- 9-3 vote, three regional-president dissents favoring a hike; CNBC, "Fed rate decision July 2026: Divided Fed holds interest rates steady" (cnbc.com/2026/07/29/fed-rate-decision-july-2026.html) -- names the dissenters (Hammack, Kashkari, Logan) and notes it is the first unified-direction dissent since Sept 2016; CNBC, "Fed minutes July 2026: Officials saw need for rate hike if inflation doesn't cool" (cnbc.com/2026/08/19/fed-minutes-july-2026-officials-saw-need-for-rate-hike-if-inflation-doesnt-cool.html); Federal Reserve SEP materials, 2026-03-18 and 2026-06-17 (federalreserve.gov/monetarypolicy/fomcprojtabl20260318.htm and fomcprojtabl20260617.htm) -- median core PCE forecast for 2026 revised from 2.7% to 3.3%; American Banker, "PCE inflation remains sticky as Fed weighs next move" -- core PCE 3.0% (Dec 2025) to 3.3% (June 2026); Macrotrends/Treasury 10-Year TIPS yield data -- 1.99% (April 2026) to 2.32% (2026-08-25), cited only for the real-yield level and its change, not for any strategy's or asset's performance framing.


### `rp_single_asset_trend_SLV_95` (primitive `single_asset_trend`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-29, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: ticker=SLV, lookback=95. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: Structural supply/demand claim, not a performance claim about SLV: the Silver Institute's World Silver Survey 2026 (with Metals Focus, published 2026-04-15) forecasts a sixth consecutive annual global silver deficit in 2026 -- 46.3Moz, wider than 2025 -- and the deficit widened even though solar-sector silver demand fell roughly 19% via thrifting (from ~186.6Moz in 2025 to ~151Moz in 2026), because mine supply is contracting FASTER than industrial demand is declining. New primary and by-product mine supply carries a structural 5-10 year lead time, so this shortfall cannot close on the timescale of a single-asset trend signal's own lookback window -- a physical, slow-moving constraint, which is the kind of persistent, non-mean-reverting imbalance this primitive is built to capture, distinct from any claim that silver 'has been rallying' or that trend 'is working' on it lately. Coin/bar investment demand is forecast +18% for 2026, absorbing metal that would otherwise ease the industrial shortfall, reinforcing rather than offsetting the deficit. Lookback=95 trading days spans from the Survey's 2026-04-15 publication date -- the point this specific widened-deficit data became public -- to today (98 calendar weekdays, ~95 trading days after typical holidays), capturing exactly the window over which this now-public structural fact could be reflected in price; not a round number chosen after the fact. Checked pipeline_ideas.csv and graveyard.csv: SLV has been proposed as single_asset_trend twice before (lookback 63 and 189), never at 95, and single_asset_trend/SLV carries no graveyard verdict at this lookback. Monthly freq matches the multi-month horizon of a mine-supply-lead-time thesis, consistent with how this primitive is most often rebalanced in this pipeline.

Citation: Silver Institute / Metals Focus, World Silver Survey 2026, published 2026-04-15 (silverinstitute.org/global-silver-investment-to-remain-strong-in-2026-against-the-backdrop-of-a-sixth-consecutive-annual-market-deficit) -- 46.3Moz deficit, sixth consecutive year, wider than 2025; GoldSilver.com, "Solar Is Using 19% Less Silver. The Deficit Is Getting Worse. Here's Why That's Bullish." (goldsilver.com/industry-news/article/silver-supply-deficit-solar-thrifting) -- solar demand down from 186.6Moz (2025) to ~151Moz (2026) yet the deficit widened because mine supply contracted faster; IndexBox, "Silver Market Faces Sixth Consecutive Deficit Despite Solar Industry Cutting Usage" (indexbox.io/blog/silver-market-faces-sixth-consecutive-deficit-despite-solar-industry-cutting-usage) -- corroborates mine-supply contraction and +18% forecast coin/bar investment demand for 2026.


### `rp_cross_sectional_rank_momentum_252_3` (primitive `cross_sectional_rank`)

**PRE-REGISTRATION — frozen 2026-08-29, committed automatically before any OOS test ran (#179).** Parameters: metric=momentum, lookback=252, k=3. Rebalance freq: M.

Rationale: STRUCTURAL CONDITION, NOT A TRACK-RECORD CLAIM: the US dollar is in a multi-year structural decline, not a short-term dip -- DXY is down ~9% YTD 2026 and hit a 3-year low of 97.4 at the August 2026 Jackson Hole symposium, driven by dateable structural forces: twin (fiscal + current-account) deficits, with CBO projecting ~$3.3T added to the federal deficit over 10 years pushing debt toward ~130% of GDP by 2030, plus measured de-dollarization (IMF COFER data: the dollar's share of global FX reserves fell to 57.4% in Q1 2026, down from 65.5% in 2010 and 71% in 2000). MECHANISM: a structurally weakening dollar changes relative capital-flow incentives across this UNIVERSE -- foreign/non-USD-denominated equity exposure (EEM, EFA) becomes structurally favored over USD safe-haven and large-cap-growth exposure (SPY, QQQ, UUP itself) as international allocators rotate away from a currency running twin deficits and losing reserve share. cross_sectional_rank(metric=momentum, lookback=252, k=3) does not bet on any single named ticker or cite any strategy's own results -- it mechanically longs the 3 strongest and shorts the 3 weakest trailing-12-month-momentum tickers in the full UNIVERSE, letting a genuine multi-year dollar-driven rotation (if real) reveal itself in relative momentum rather than requiring a prediction of which specific ticker benefits most.

Citation: Riviera Wealth Management, 'The US Dollar's Structural Decline -- What It Means for Your Portfolio' (rivierawealthmanagement.com/dollar-structural-decline-portfolio-2026/), DXY ~9% YTD decline, 3-year low 97.4 at Jackson Hole 2026, twin-deficit and de-dollarization discussion, IMF COFER dollar reserve share 57.4% Q1 2026 vs 65.5% (2010) / 71% (2000); Congressional Budget Office deficit projection (~$3.3T over 10 years, debt toward ~130% of GDP by 2030) as cited therein; Cambridge Associates via Yahoo Finance, 'Emerging markets set to outperform US stocks as dollar weakness continues' (finance.yahoo.com/news/emerging-markets-set-outperform-us-093000663.html) -- Cambridge expects a multi-year dollar bear market and recommends overweighting global non-US equities on that structural basis, not on EM's own recent returns.

## Rules of the roster
1. A strategy's spec (signal, universe, freq) is frozen **before** its OOS verdict.
2. One verdict per spec. Tweaks = a NEW row and a NEW graveyard entry.
3. Families are chosen to be **mutually uncorrelated bets** — trend vs reversal vs calendar
   vs defensive vs carry. Correlation matrix in the dashboard is the check.
4. DEAD standalone ≠ useless: low-corr sleeves may still earn a place as piggyback
   diversifiers on the 60/40 core (see research/combine.py / dashboard Piggyback Lab).

## Key evidence (gathered in research sessions)
- TSMOM: Moskowitz–Ooi–Pedersen 2012, ~1.0 Sharpe across 58 futures pre-costs (AQR/Quantpedia).
- Turn-of-month: documented since Lakonishok & Smidt 1988; still debated post-1990s (Quantpedia, QuantSeeker).
- Short-term reversal / RSI(2): Connors; SPY 1993– ~9%/yr while invested 28% of time (QuantifiedStrategies).
- Low-vol / BAB: Frazzini–Pedersen 2014, Sharpe ~0.78, ~2x US market (NBER).
- Funding carry: 8–20% APY documented in calm regimes, delta-neutral (multiple 2025–26 sources).
- Retail overfitting: backtest Sharpe explains <3% of live results; more tweaking widens the gap.
